### SetKE: Knowledge Editing for Knowledge Elements Overlap
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20972v1)
- **Authors**: Yifan Wei, Xiaoyan Yu, Ran Song, Hao Peng, Angsheng Li
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) excel in tasks such as retrieval and question answering but require updates to incorporate new knowledge and reduce inaccuracies and hallucinations. Traditional updating methods, like fine-tuning and incremental learning, face challenges such as overfitting and high computational costs. Knowledge Editing (KE) provides a promising alternative but often overlooks the Knowledge Element Overlap (KEO) phenomenon, where multiple triplets share common elements, leading to editing conflicts. We identify the prevalence of KEO in existing KE datasets and show its significant impact on current KE methods, causing performance degradation in handling such triplets. To address this, we propose a new formulation, Knowledge Set Editing (KSE), and introduce SetKE, a method that edits sets of triplets simultaneously. Experimental results demonstrate that SetKE outperforms existing methods in KEO scenarios on mainstream LLMs. Additionally, we introduce EditSet, a dataset containing KEO triplets, providing a comprehensive benchmark.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper "SetKE: Knowledge Editing for Knowledge Elements Overlap" addresses a limitation in current Knowledge Editing (KE) methods, which often overlook the "Knowledge Element Overlap" (KEO) phenomenon where multiple factual triplets share common elements. The authors demonstrate the prevalence of KEO in existing KE datasets and its negative impact on existing KE methods.  They introduce a new problem formulation, Knowledge Set Editing (KSE), and propose SetKE, a method that edits sets of triplets simultaneously using bipartite matching for optimization.  Experimental results on benchmark LLMs show that SetKE outperforms existing methods in KEO scenarios. The paper also presents EDITSET, a new dataset specifically designed for KEO, to provide a comprehensive benchmark for evaluating KE methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in identifying and formalizing the KEO problem, which existing KE literature has largely ignored. The proposed KSE formulation and SetKE method, which leverages bipartite matching, offer a potentially valuable approach to addressing KEO. The EDITSET dataset contributes by providing a focused benchmark for KEO scenarios. However, while the bipartite matching approach is relatively new to KE, the use of bipartite matching itself is not new.

* **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from the practical relevance of KEO in real-world knowledge editing scenarios.  The experiments highlight a performance degradation of existing KE methods in KEO situations, demonstrating the need for specialized approaches.  SetKE appears to offer a tangible improvement, and the EDITSET dataset is expected to accelerate research on KEO.

* **Strengths:**
    * The paper clearly identifies and articulates a significant limitation in existing KE research.
    * The experimental evaluation is thorough, using benchmark LLMs and a newly created dataset.
    * The bipartite matching approach provides a reasonable optimization strategy for editing sets of knowledge triplets.
    * The paper analyzes the source of the problem via observation that the neurons share information and therefore the editor overwrites them.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * The approach of the proposed method (bipartite matching) is used in many other areas.
    * The main results are numerical with some analysis; it would be beneficial to present qualitative results that make the differences between the original and new triplets explicit, perhaps with some examples.
    * There are multiple hyper-parameters used by the authors, but it is not possible to assess how sensitive they are. For example, location settings and weights that update.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the KE field by shifting focus towards set-based knowledge editing approaches and highlighting the importance of consistency when editing overlapping knowledge elements. EDITSET will likely become a commonly used benchmark.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper introduces a meaningful problem (KEO) and a new framework (KSE) along with a reasonable solution (SetKE using bipartite matching), the technical novelty of the solution itself is limited, and the analysis is mostly numerical. The reliance on standard techniques and limited qualitative analysis reduces the impact somewhat.

### Equivariant non-linear maps for neural networks on homogeneous spaces
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20974v1)
- **Authors**: Elias Nyholm, Oscar Carlsson, Maurice Weiler, Daniel Persson
- **Abstract**: This paper presents a novel framework for non-linear equivariant neural network layers on homogeneous spaces. The seminal work of Cohen et al. on equivariant $G$-CNNs on homogeneous spaces characterized the representation theory of such layers in the linear setting, finding that they are given by convolutions with kernels satisfying so-called steerability constraints. Motivated by the empirical success of non-linear layers, such as self-attention or input dependent kernels, we set out to generalize these insights to the non-linear setting. We derive generalized steerability constraints that any such layer needs to satisfy and prove the universality of our construction. The insights gained into the symmetry-constrained functional dependence of equivariant operators on feature maps and group elements informs the design of future equivariant neural network layers. We demonstrate how several common equivariant network architectures - $G$-CNNs, implicit steerable kernel networks, conventional and relative position embedded attention based transformers, and LieTransformers - may be derived from our framework.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a novel framework for constructing equivariant non-linear neural network layers on homogeneous spaces.  It generalizes the theory of G-CNNs, previously limited to linear layers, to incorporate non-linear operations inspired by self-attention and input-dependent kernels.  The paper derives generalized steerability constraints that such layers must satisfy and claims to prove the universality of the construction.  Finally, it demonstrates how several common equivariant network architectures, including G-CNNs, LieTransformers, implicit steerable CNNs, and attention-based transformers, can be derived from this framework.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in extending the well-established representation theory of equivariant *linear* layers (G-CNNs) to the *non-linear* setting.  Previous work has largely focused on modifying specific non-linear layers (e.g., attention) to achieve equivariance, but this paper attempts a more general, top-down approach. The derivation of generalized steerability constraints for non-linear layers and the universality proof are significant contributions *if rigorously validated*. The paper's capacity to cast various architectures into the same mathematical formulation is original. However, the actual practicality of this approach for designing *new* highly effective non-linear equivariant layers needs careful consideration. It remains to be seen if these constraints are truly useful in the design of complex models beyond existing architectures.
*   **Significance:** The paper aims to provide a unifying theoretical understanding of disparate equivariant architectures. The significance depends heavily on the *breadth and depth* to which this framework is practically applicable and inspires further research.  If the framework truly offers a clear blueprint for designing *new* equivariant non-linear layers that outperform existing methods, it will be a major breakthrough. However, if it mainly *post-hoc* rationalizes existing architectures without providing concrete design insights, its significance will be diminished. The claim that this theory *specializes* to existing architectures such as LieTransformers and the like could be interpreted in a less favorable light as merely a descriptive re-framing without leading to new model instantiations. Claims around universality needs very careful scrutiny and verification.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   Provides a unifying mathematical framework for understanding equivariant non-linear layers.
    *   Derives generalized steerability constraints.
    *   Shows how various equivariant architectures can be viewed as special cases of the framework.
    *   The use of fiber bundles and equivariant cohomology offers a powerful geometric perspective.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The paper is heavily mathematical and may be difficult for non-experts to follow.  The accessibility could be improved with more intuitive explanations and examples.
    *   The "universality" proof is crucial, but its rigor needs careful examination by the community. The distribution formulation requires careful understanding.
    *   The practical impact is unclear.  The paper does not present any new or improved equivariant layers designed *using* the framework.  It primarily *derives* existing architectures.
    *   The connection to implicit steerable CNNs, while interesting, may be somewhat superficial if it only provides a high-level correspondence without detailed implementation guidance.
    *   The absence of experimental validation/comparison to existing models is a major deficiency. It's unclear if the proposed framework offers practical performance benefits.
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could potentially influence the design of future equivariant neural networks if the framework provides actionable insights and guidelines. It could also stimulate further research into the theoretical foundations of equivariant deep learning. However, widespread adoption depends on demonstrating its practical utility.

*Score:* 6

**Rationale for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and potentially significant theoretical contribution by generalizing equivariance theory to non-linear layers. The mathematical formulation is sophisticated and provides a unifying perspective. However, the lack of *experimental validation* and the uncertainty surrounding the practical design implications of the framework limit its current impact. The ability to *derive* existing architectures is interesting, but the crucial test is whether it can be used to *discover* superior ones. The score reflects the potential for significant impact *if* the framework proves useful in designing novel and effective architectures, balanced against the current lack of practical demonstration and the mathematical complexity. The score could potentially increase if follow-up work demonstrates that the framework leads to tangible improvements in equivariant model design. The claim of universality needs extremely rigorous verification.


- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### Real-Time Wayfinding Assistant for Blind and Low-Vision Users
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20976v1)
- **Authors**: Dabbrata Das, Argho Deb Das, Farhan Sadaf
- **Abstract**: Navigating unfamiliar places continues to be one of the most persistent and essential everyday obstacles for those who are blind or have limited vision (BLV). Existing assistive technologies, such as GPS-based navigation systems, AI-powered smart glasses, and sonar-equipped canes, often face limitations in real-time obstacle avoidance, precise localization, and adaptability to dynamic surroundings. To investigate potential solutions, we introduced PathFinder, a novel map-less navigation system that explores different models for understanding 2D images, including Vision Language Models (VLMs), Large Language Models (LLMs), and employs monocular depth estimation for free-path detection. Our approach integrates a Depth-First Search (DFS) algorithm on depth images to determine the longest obstacle-free path, ensuring optimal route selection while maintaining computational efficiency. We conducted comparative evaluations against existing AI-powered navigation methods and performed a usability study with BLV participants. The results demonstrate that PathFinder achieves a favorable balance between accuracy, computational efficiency, and real-time responsiveness. Notably, it reduces mean absolute error (MAE) and improves decision-making speed in outdoor navigation compared to AI-based alternatives. Participant feedback emphasizes the system's usability and effectiveness in outside situations, but also identifies issues in complicated indoor locations and low-light conditions. Usability testing revealed that 73% of participants understood how to use the app in about a minute, and 80% praised its balance of accuracy, quick response, and overall convenience.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents PathFinder, a novel map-less navigation system for blind and low-vision (BLV) users.  It explores different image understanding models including VLMs and LLMs but ultimately advocates for a computationally efficient approach using monocular depth estimation and a Depth-First Search (DFS) algorithm on depth images to identify the longest obstacle-free path.  The system is designed to be implemented on a single-camera smartphone, making it a low-cost and accessible solution. The authors conduct comparative evaluations against existing AI-powered navigation methods and a usability study with BLV participants. Results show PathFinder achieves a favorable balance between accuracy, computational efficiency, and real-time responsiveness, particularly in outdoor environments. The usability testing demonstrates good user understanding and appreciation for the system's accuracy, responsiveness, and convenience, while also highlighting areas for improvement in complex indoor and low-light scenarios.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its efficient combination of established techniques (monocular depth estimation and DFS) tailored for a specific assistive technology application. While neither depth estimation nor DFS is individually novel, their synergistic application within a mobile, accessible BLV navigation aid represents a worthwhile contribution.  The comparison against more complex AI-based methods is also useful in highlighting the benefits of simplicity and efficiency for real-world deployment. However, the individual components are not groundbreaking.

* **Significance:** The significance of this work rests on its potential for practical impact. By prioritizing computational efficiency and accessibility, the authors address critical barriers to the wider adoption of AI-driven assistive technologies for BLV users. The focus on a smartphone-based solution is particularly important, given the widespread availability of this technology.  The comparative analysis provides valuable insights into the trade-offs between complex AI models and more lightweight image processing approaches. The usability study, though preliminary, demonstrates the potential of PathFinder to improve the independence and accessibility of BLV individuals. However, the small sample size of the user study somewhat limits the strength of conclusions about usability. A key weakness is the lack of a detailed comparative analysis against other low-complexity pathfinding algorithms or established heuristics within robotics or navigation literature; they focus mainly on comparisons against more computationally intensive AI approaches. Furthermore, the paper could be strengthened by a more thorough discussion of the limitations of the DFS approach, such as its susceptibility to local optima and sensitivity to noise in the depth map. The evaluation metrics, while appropriate, do not fully capture the nuances of real-world navigation; metrics such as task completion rate and path optimality in complex environments would be beneficial.

* **Strengths:**
    * Focus on practical, low-cost accessibility.
    * Clear and well-motivated design choices.
    * Comparative evaluation demonstrating efficiency gains.
    * Usability study providing valuable user feedback.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * Limited novelty in the core algorithms.
    * User study sample size is relatively small.
    * Shallow comparison against existing non-AI pathfinding methods.
    * Limited discussion of DFS algorithm limitations.
    * Evaluation metrics do not fully capture real-world navigation complexities.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in assistive technology by advocating for simpler, more efficient approaches that prioritize real-time performance and accessibility.  It could also inspire further exploration of image processing and pathfinding techniques tailored for resource-constrained mobile devices. The comparative study could encourage a more nuanced evaluation of AI-powered solutions, considering not only accuracy but also computational cost and user experience.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

While the paper makes a worthwhile contribution to the field by focusing on a practical and accessible navigation system, the limited novelty in the core algorithmic approach and the weaknesses in the evaluation methodology prevent it from achieving a higher score. The prioritization of user-friendly technology and cost reduction is valuable. However, a more thorough exploration and mitigation of the DFS limitations (e.g., through integration with higher-level planning) and a deeper comparative analysis would strengthen the work significantly.

### ACE: A Security Architecture for LLM-Integrated App Systems
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20984v1)
- **Authors**: Evan Li, Tushin Mallick, Evan Rose, William Robertson, Alina Oprea, Cristina Nita-Rotaru
- **Abstract**: LLM-integrated app systems extend the utility of Large Language Models (LLMs) with third-party apps that are invoked by a system LLM using interleaved planning and execution phases to answer user queries. These systems introduce new attack vectors where malicious apps can cause integrity violation of planning or execution, availability breakdown, or privacy compromise during execution. In this work, we identify new attacks impacting the integrity of planning, as well as the integrity and availability of execution in LLM-integrated apps, and demonstrate them against IsolateGPT, a recent solution designed to mitigate attacks from malicious apps. We propose Abstract-Concrete-Execute (ACE), a new secure architecture for LLM-integrated app systems that provides security guarantees for system planning and execution. Specifically, ACE decouples planning into two phases by first creating an abstract execution plan using only trusted information, and then mapping the abstract plan to a concrete plan using installed system apps. We verify that the plans generated by our system satisfy user-specified secure information flow constraints via static analysis on the structured plan output. During execution, ACE enforces data and capability barriers between apps, and ensures that the execution is conducted according to the trusted abstract plan. We show experimentally that our system is secure against attacks from the INJECAGENT benchmark, a standard benchmark for control flow integrity in the face of indirect prompt injection attacks, and our newly introduced attacks. Our architecture represents a significant advancement towards hardening LLM-based systems containing system facilities of varying levels of trustworthiness.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "ACE: A Security Architecture for LLM-Integrated App Systems":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses security vulnerabilities in LLM-integrated app systems, where large language models use third-party apps to answer user queries.  It identifies new attack vectors that malicious apps can exploit to compromise the integrity of planning and execution, cause availability breakdowns, or leak sensitive user information.  The authors demonstrate these attacks against IsolateGPT, a recent defense solution.  To counter these threats, they propose a novel security architecture called Abstract-Concrete-Execute (ACE). ACE decouples planning into abstract and concrete phases. It uses a trusted abstract plan and enforces data and capability barriers during execution. They statically verify the generated plans using secure information flow constraints.  The authors present experimental results demonstrating that ACE is secure against attacks from the INJECAGENT benchmark, a standard benchmark, as well as against the new attacks they introduce.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary strength lies in identifying new, realistic attacks that exploit the trust relationship between the system LLM and third-party apps, a vulnerability not adequately addressed by existing defenses like IsolateGPT. The key novelty is in the architecture decoupling planning into two phases based on a trusted abstract plan.  The static analysis of information flow is also a significant contribution. The work clearly identifies the limitations of existing approaches, which either trust the app descriptions too much or focus primarily on prompt injection without considering other potential attacks.
*   **Significance:** ACE represents a significant advancement in securing LLM-integrated app systems.  By separating planning and execution and enforcing data and capability barriers, it provides a stronger security foundation than current approaches. The use of static analysis to verify information flow is particularly important for preventing sensitive data leakage.  The experimental evaluation demonstrates the effectiveness of ACE against both existing and newly introduced attacks. The work highlights the need for a more comprehensive security model for LLM systems that considers a strong attacker model with malicious apps and not just prompt injection from untrusted data sources.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Attack Identification:** The identification of new attack vectors, particularly planner manipulation, demonstrates a clear understanding of the security challenges in LLM-integrated systems.
    *   **Architecture Design:** The ACE architecture is well-reasoned and addresses the identified vulnerabilities with a clear separation of concerns and strong security guarantees.
    *   **Static Analysis:** The integration of static analysis for information flow control is a powerful technique for preventing data leakage and ensuring compliance with security policies.
    *   **Experimental Validation:** The thorough experimental evaluation on INJECAGENT and newly introduced attacks provides convincing evidence of the effectiveness of ACE.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational Complexity:** Static analysis can be computationally expensive, especially for large and complex execution plans. The paper could benefit from a discussion of the potential performance overhead of the static analysis and optimization techniques to mitigate its impact.
    *   **Limited App/Plan Complexity:** The current implementation supports standalone apps and single-query execution. Extending ACE to handle application suites, multi-query interactions, and more complex plans remains a significant challenge for future work.
    *   **Reliance on Abstract App Generation:** The core idea of an abstract app that can be linked to a concrete one could be limited in scenarios.

*   **Potential Influence:** ACE has the potential to significantly influence the design of future LLM-integrated app systems.  It offers a principled approach to security that can be adapted and extended to address new threats and challenges. The separation of planning and execution phases could be a standard component in future secure LLM architectures.
*   **Room for Improvement:** A more detailed discussion on limitations and comparison with techniques like CaMeL, that introduces fine-grained capabilities, would improve the scope of the work.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:** The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the security of LLM-integrated app systems. It identifies new attack vectors, proposes a well-designed and evaluated architecture, and emphasizes the importance of formal reasoning and static analysis for security. While limitations exist regarding complexity and performance, the core ideas of ACE are innovative and have the potential to significantly improve the security and trustworthiness of future LLM-based applications. The overall impact is that the work provides concrete evidence for a strong attacker model and that the design changes in ACE can resolve this.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 8/10

### X-Fusion: Introducing New Modality to Frozen Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20996v1)
- **Authors**: Sicheng Mo, Thao Nguyen, Xun Huang, Siddharth Srinivasan Iyer, Yijun Li, Yuchen Liu, Abhishek Tandon, Eli Shechtman, Krishna Kumar Singh, Yong Jae Lee, Bolei Zhou, Yuheng Li
- **Abstract**: We propose X-Fusion, a framework that extends pretrained Large Language Models (LLMs) for multimodal tasks while preserving their language capabilities. X-Fusion employs a dual-tower design with modality-specific weights, keeping the LLM's parameters frozen while integrating vision-specific information for both understanding and generation. Our experiments demonstrate that X-Fusion consistently outperforms alternative architectures on both image-to-text and text-to-image tasks. We find that incorporating understanding-focused data improves generation quality, reducing image data noise enhances overall performance, and feature alignment accelerates convergence for smaller models but has minimal impact on larger ones. Our findings provide valuable insights into building efficient unified multimodal models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the X-Fusion paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces X-Fusion, a new framework designed to integrate visual capabilities into pre-trained Large Language Models (LLMs) while preserving their existing language proficiency.  X-Fusion adopts a dual-tower architecture: one tower freezes the LLM's parameters to maintain language skills, while the other introduces modality-specific (vision) weights for image understanding and generation. The key innovation is the modality-specific tower. This architecture aligns visual and textual features at the intermediate processing level, not just at the input or output, by creating new trainable parameters alongside the frozen LLM. They show the design scales efficiently to new modalities. The authors present experimental results on both image-to-text (captioning) and text-to-image tasks, demonstrating that X-Fusion outperforms alternative approaches.  The paper includes ablation studies examining the impact of training data characteristics, specifically, noise and data ratios, and explores the use of pre-trained representations for feature regularization. They found a data ratio of 2:1 for visual generation to understanding helped. Also, surprisingly, using clear, non-noisy images for understanding tasks improves generations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The idea of freezing the LLM and adding a modality-specific tower for vision isn't completely revolutionary. There are existing works exploring similar concepts, such as Adapter layers or other parameter-efficient fine-tuning techniques. It is a smart, straightforward way of doing modality alignment, making it a practical approach for many applications. The data-centric analyses on the impact of data noise and balancing generation and understanding tasks are interesting and provide actionable insights. However, similar data ratio experiments have appeared in other multi-modal pre-training papers.

* **Significance:** X-Fusion offers a pragmatic way to create multimodal LLMs, by leveraging existing powerful LLMs without needing to retrain them from scratch.  The performance gains demonstrated relative to simpler architectures (like single-tower fine-tuning) support the effectiveness of the dual-tower approach. The systematic ablation studies offer useful guidelines for training such models, particularly regarding data curation and noise levels. It is not an incredibly innovative design, but it is highly effective. It simplifies multi-modal development.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear and Well-Defined Approach:**  The X-Fusion architecture is presented in a clear and understandable manner.
    * **Comprehensive Experiments:** The authors conduct extensive experiments, including ablation studies, to validate their approach and understand the impact of different design choices.
    * **Actionable Insights:** The paper provides valuable insights into training strategies for multimodal LLMs, particularly regarding the importance of data quality and balancing different tasks.
    * **Strong empirical results**: The paper demonstrates results on a range of image understanding and generation tasks, where the model achieves good performance.
    * **Simplicity**: The addition of the modality-specific weights requires relatively little effort but can lead to strong results.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Novelty:** As mentioned, the core architectural concept has some overlap with existing literature on parameter-efficient transfer learning and modality alignment.
    * **Hallucinations**: Figure B indicates the problems with hallucinations that are often present in LLMs.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper's practicality and clear results could influence researchers and practitioners looking for efficient ways to extend existing LLMs with visual capabilities. The insights on data curation could be particularly valuable for those training new multimodal models.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7** to this paper. While X-Fusion is not groundbreaking in its core architecture, it offers a pragmatic and effective solution for integrating vision into LLMs.  The clear presentation, comprehensive experiments, and actionable insights contribute significantly to the understanding of how to train such models effectively. The data noise findings are impactful, and the clear results make this a valuable piece of work that many will find useful. The weaknesses are that the novelty isn't incredibly high, and it still has some limitations of typical LLMs. Overall, it's a solid contribution that will likely be influential in the field.

### Erased but Not Forgotten: How Backdoors Compromise Concept Erasure
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21072v1)
- **Authors**: Jonas Henry Grebe, Tobias Braun, Marcus Rohrbach, Anna Rohrbach
- **Abstract**: The expansion of large-scale text-to-image diffusion models has raised growing concerns about their potential to generate undesirable or harmful content, ranging from fabricated depictions of public figures to sexually explicit images. To mitigate these risks, prior work has devised machine unlearning techniques that attempt to erase unwanted concepts through fine-tuning. However, in this paper, we introduce a new threat model, Toxic Erasure (ToxE), and demonstrate how recent unlearning algorithms, including those explicitly designed for robustness, can be circumvented through targeted backdoor attacks. The threat is realized by establishing a link between a trigger and the undesired content. Subsequent unlearning attempts fail to erase this link, allowing adversaries to produce harmful content. We instantiate ToxE via two established backdoor attacks: one targeting the text encoder and another manipulating the cross-attention layers. Further, we introduce Deep Intervention Score-based Attack (DISA), a novel, deeper backdoor attack that optimizes the entire U-Net using a score-based objective, improving the attack's persistence across different erasure methods. We evaluate five recent concept erasure methods against our threat model. For celebrity identity erasure, our deep attack circumvents erasure with up to 82% success, averaging 57% across all erasure methods. For explicit content erasure, ToxE attacks can elicit up to 9 times more exposed body parts, with DISA yielding an average increase by a factor of 2.9. These results highlight a critical security gap in current unlearning strategies.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Erased but Not Forgotten: How Backdoors Compromise Concept Erasure" introduces a new threat model called Toxic Erasure (ToxE) in the context of text-to-image diffusion models. ToxE demonstrates how backdoor attacks can circumvent concept erasure techniques, even those explicitly designed for robustness.  The authors establish a link between a trigger and the undesired content before the unlearning process, rendering subsequent erasure attempts ineffective. They instantiate ToxE using established backdoor attacks (RICKROLLING, EVILEDIT) and introduce a novel deep backdoor attack, Deep Intervention Score-based Attack (DISA), which targets the entire U-Net.  The paper evaluates several concept erasure methods against ToxE, demonstrating significant vulnerabilities in celebrity identity and explicit content erasure scenarios. The results highlight a critical security gap in current unlearning strategies.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary contribution lies in identifying and formalizing the ToxE threat model.  While backdoor attacks on generative models and concept erasure techniques exist, the intersection of these two areas, particularly in the context of text-to-image diffusion models, represents a novel and important research direction. The introduction of TOXEDISA is also a novel contribution, providing a more persistent backdoor attack strategy.

*   **Significance:** The work has significant implications for the security and ethical considerations surrounding large-scale text-to-image models. By demonstrating the vulnerability of current erasure techniques to backdoor attacks, the paper exposes a critical security gap. This has potential implications for compliance with the "right to be forgotten" and AI safety regulations. The work serves as a clear warning that simply applying existing unlearning techniques isn't sufficient and that robust adversarial testing is crucial.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly articulates the problem, threat model, and proposed attack.
    *   The empirical evaluation is comprehensive, covering multiple erasure methods, backdoor attacks, and evaluation metrics.
    *   The introduction of TOXEDISA adds a valuable technical contribution by showcasing a deeper, more resilient backdoor attack.
    *   The analysis of erasure trajectories provides valuable insights into the persistence of backdoors during the unlearning process.
    *   The detailed supplementary material enhances reproducibility and allows for a more thorough understanding of the experimental setup.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the paper introduces TOXEDISA, the defense strategies are somewhat limited and primarily point to future research directions. A more detailed exploration of potential countermeasures would have strengthened the paper.
    *   The reliance on established backdoor attack methods (RICKROLLING, EVILEDIT) for ToxE instantiation, while understandable, could be seen as a limitation. More novel or sophisticated backdoor techniques specifically designed for ToxE could have yielded even more compelling results. The ToxETextEnc and ToxEx-Attn are more limited and only demonstrate what happens with these established techniques.
    *   The paper doesn't delve deeply into the economic costs of backdoor injections, nor the possibility of injecting multiple backdoors simultaneously or sequentially. This could have added a valuable dimension to the study.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper is likely to have a significant impact on the field of secure and ethical generative AI. It highlights a previously overlooked vulnerability and will likely stimulate further research into more robust concept erasure techniques and backdoor detection/mitigation strategies.  The work may also influence best practices for deploying and managing large-scale text-to-image models.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

The paper identifies a real-world vulnerability with significant potential impact. The experiments are well-designed and executed, and the analysis is thorough. The threat model is well-defined and represents a valuable contribution to the literature on secure generative AI. While the countermeasures are limited, this is acceptable since it's a first step in the analysis of the ToxE threat model. This work is of higher significance compared to a mere incremental improvement of the state of the art, with its contribution being a comprehensive definition, analysis, and exploration.

### On the Potential of Large Language Models to Solve Semantics-Aware Process Mining Tasks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21074v1)
- **Authors**: Adrian Rebmann, Fabian David Schmidt, Goran Glavaš, Han van der Aa
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have shown to be valuable tools for tackling process mining tasks. Existing studies report on their capability to support various data-driven process analyses and even, to some extent, that they are able to reason about how processes work. This reasoning ability suggests that there is potential for LLMs to tackle semantics-aware process mining tasks, which are tasks that rely on an understanding of the meaning of activities and their relationships. Examples of these include process discovery, where the meaning of activities can indicate their dependency, whereas in anomaly detection the meaning can be used to recognize process behavior that is abnormal. In this paper, we systematically explore the capabilities of LLMs for such tasks. Unlike prior work, which largely evaluates LLMs in their default state, we investigate their utility through both in-context learning and supervised fine-tuning. Concretely, we define five process mining tasks requiring semantic understanding and provide extensive benchmarking datasets for evaluation. Our experiments reveal that while LLMs struggle with challenging process mining tasks when used out of the box or with minimal in-context examples, they achieve strong performance when fine-tuned for these tasks across a broad range of process types and industries.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the potential of Large Language Models (LLMs) to solve semantics-aware process mining tasks. It defines five specific tasks (two semantic anomaly detection tasks, semantic next activity prediction, and two semantic process discovery tasks) that benefit from understanding the meaning of activities and their relationships within a process.  The authors created benchmarking datasets based on a corpus derived from publicly available process models to evaluate LLMs on these tasks. They compare the performance of open-source LLMs (Llama-3 and Mistral-2) using both in-context learning (ICL) and supervised fine-tuning against rule-based baselines and encoder-based models (RoBERTa). The results demonstrate that LLMs struggle with these tasks when used out-of-the-box or with minimal ICL. However, fine-tuning significantly improves their performance, surpassing encoder models and achieving promising results across a variety of process types and industries.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper's novelty lies in several aspects:

*   **Defining Semantics-Aware Process Mining Tasks:** Explicitly defining NLP-oriented process mining tasks that go beyond frequency analysis and leverage the meaning of activities. While semantic process mining has been discussed before, formalizing and operationalizing it into specific NLP tasks contributes to a more structured and quantifiable research direction.
*   **Benchmarking Datasets:** Creating and publishing comprehensive benchmarking datasets for these new tasks. The creation from a large process model collection fills a critical gap and enables systematic evaluation.
*   **Systematic Evaluation of LLMs:** Conducting a systematic evaluation of LLMs using both ICL and fine-tuning. Prior works often focus on one approach or use closed-source models. Comparing multiple open-source LLMs and fine-tuning methods provides a more complete picture.

**Significance:**

The findings are significant for the following reasons:

*   **Demonstrates Feasibility of LLMs for Advanced Process Mining:** Shows that LLMs, after fine-tuning, can effectively tackle tasks that require understanding process semantics. This opens up new possibilities for process discovery, anomaly detection, and prediction that are difficult or impossible with traditional methods.
*   **Provides a Foundation for Future Research:** The defined tasks and datasets provide a solid foundation for future research on applying LLMs to process mining. This enables researchers to compare different LLMs, fine-tuning techniques, and architectures in a standardized setting.
*   **Highlights the Importance of Fine-Tuning:** The paper convincingly shows that fine-tuning is crucial for LLMs to perform well on these tasks. This suggests that pre-trained knowledge alone is insufficient and task-specific adaptation is needed.

**Strengths:**

*   Clearly defined research questions and methodology.
*   Well-structured experimental design with appropriate baselines.
*   Comprehensive evaluation of multiple LLMs and fine-tuning techniques.
*   Detailed analysis of the results and comparison across tasks.
*   Publicly available datasets and code to ensure reproducibility.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Generalizability from the Corpus:** The evaluation is based on a corpus derived from a single source (SAP-SAM).  While SAP-SAM is large, this source may introduce a certain bias towards SAP-related processes, and hence, may not fully generalize to diverse business scenarios.
*   **Lack of Comparison to Established Statistical Process Mining Methods:** It acknowledges the impossibility of direct comparison, arguing their method requires different input (process knowledge vs. event data), the argument could still be strengthened by discussing the relative tradeoffs of the two approaches (e.g. applicability, cost of data / process understanding, etc.).
*   **Fine-Tuning Effort:** While the paper mentions parameter-efficient fine-tuning, a more thorough discussion of the required computational resources and expertise would be valuable, particularly for researchers and practitioners who want to replicate the results.

**Potential Influence:**

This paper has the potential to significantly influence the process mining field by:

*   Motivating researchers to explore LLMs for advanced process analysis.
*   Providing a benchmark for evaluating new LLM-based approaches.
*   Inspiring the development of new process mining techniques that integrate LLMs with traditional methods.
*   Encouraging the creation of larger and more diverse datasets for LLM-based process mining.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper presents a well-defined, well-executed, and thoroughly analyzed study on applying LLMs to semantics-aware process mining tasks. The creation of benchmarking datasets and systematic evaluation of multiple LLMs contribute significantly to the field. While there are some limitations regarding generalizability and computational effort, the paper addresses an important research gap and provides a solid foundation for future research.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.DB
- **Score**: 8/10

### LLM Enhancer: Merged Approach using Vector Embedding for Reducing Large Language Model Hallucinations with External Knowledge
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21132v1)
- **Authors**: Naheed Rayhan, Md. Ashrafuzzaman
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs), such as ChatGPT, have demonstrated the capability to generate human like, natural responses across a range of tasks, including task oriented dialogue and question answering. However, their application in real world, critical scenarios is often hindered by a tendency to produce inaccurate information and a limited ability to leverage external knowledge sources. This paper introduces the LLM ENHANCER system, designed to integrate multiple online sources such as Google, Wikipedia, and DuckDuckGo to enhance data accuracy. The LLMs employed within this system are open source. The data acquisition process for the LLM ENHANCER system operates in parallel, utilizing custom agent tools to manage the flow of information. Vector embeddings are used to identify the most pertinent information, which is subsequently supplied to the LLM for user interaction. The LLM ENHANCER system mitigates hallucinations in chat based LLMs while preserving response naturalness and accuracy.
- **Summary**: Okay, I can provide a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "LLM-ENHANCER," a system designed to reduce hallucinations (generation of inaccurate information) in large language models (LLMs). It achieves this by integrating information from multiple online sources (Google, Wikipedia, DuckDuckGo) using custom agent tools. The system uses vector embeddings to identify the most relevant information from these sources, which is then provided to an open-source LLM (Mistral 7B) to answer user queries. The authors claim that LLM-ENHANCER mitigates hallucinations while preserving the naturalness and accuracy of responses. The paper compares the performance of the system against existing LLMs and approaches with both online and offline data.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using multiple online sources and vector embeddings to improve LLM accuracy isn't entirely novel.  Similar techniques have been explored, though the specific implementation and combination of tools presented might offer some incremental novelty. The claim of using custom "agent tools" in parallel is a potential point of innovation, but the specific implementation is not described in sufficient detail to assess its true originality. The selection of the open-source Mistral 7B model adds some value.

*   **Significance:** The problem of hallucinations in LLMs is a significant one, and any system that addresses it is potentially valuable. The paper's significance hinges on the claimed improvements in accuracy and reduction of hallucinations, which are supported by the experimental results. However, further analysis is needed.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addresses a crucial problem:** Hallucinations are a significant barrier to the real-world deployment of LLMs.
    *   **Integrates multiple data sources:**  Utilizing multiple sources (Google, Wikipedia, etc.) makes intuitive sense for improving accuracy and completeness.
    *   **Uses Vector Embeddings:** Vector embeddings are a promising approach for finding relevant information.
    *   **Uses an open-source model**: LLM-ENHANCER focuses on Mistral 7B (open-source LLM), which is great. This choice makes the system more accessible.
    *   **Includes Empirical Evaluation:** The paper attempts to demonstrate the effectiveness of LLM-ENHANCER through experiments using the WikiQA dataset and a newly created recent dataset.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Lack of technical depth:** The paper is weak on detailed explanations of the agent tools. It does not explain how the tools have been implemented to handle the flow of information and resolve conflicts across different data sources.
    *   **Limited detail on data sources:** The paper does not clearly specify how it acquires and processes data from online sources like Google, Wikipedia, and DuckDuckGo, and fails to address potential biases or inconsistencies across these sources.
    *   **Limited evaluation:** The datasets used for evaluating performance (WikiQA and custom dataset) are not described adequately. More recent datasets should be tested and analysed.
    *   **Reproducibility Concerns:** There is no source code available. So this creates concerns.
    *   **Claims are not always well-substantiated:** The claim that vector embeddings need to be built each time, and, the approach requires more execution time is a significant concern and needs to be better addressed/mitigated.
    *   **Limited comparison with existing solutions:** The authors compare LLM-ENHANCER with a sequential online tools model, but not against other state-of-the-art methods for reducing hallucinations or integrating external knowledge. This makes it difficult to assess the relative merits of their approach.

*   **Potential Impact:** If the claims are robustly validated with more comprehensive evaluations and if the implementation details are made clearer, LLM-ENHANCER could contribute to improving the reliability and trustworthiness of LLMs, particularly in applications where accuracy is paramount.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents an interesting idea and some promising preliminary results, but its limitations prevent it from being considered a truly groundbreaking contribution. The lack of technical depth and the limited evaluation make it difficult to fully assess its effectiveness. Given these weaknesses, it cannot be rated highly for novelty or potential influence.

Score: 5

**Rigorous Rationale:**

A score of 5 reflects the paper's potential value as an incremental contribution to the field. The problem it addresses is crucial, and the proposed solution has merit. However, the weaknesses related to technical detail, evaluation, and comparison against other solutions limit its overall impact. The paper presents a good starting point for further research, but it requires significant improvements before it can be considered a major advancement. The lack of available code also hampers independent validation of the claims made. Without these validations, the claims cannot be robust.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 5/10

### Efficient LLMs with AMP: Attention Heads and MLP Pruning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21174v1)
- **Authors**: Leandro Giusti Mugnaini, Bruno Lopes Yamamoto, Lucas Lauton de Alcantara, Victor Zacarias, Edson Bollis, Lucas Pellicer, Anna Helena Reali Costa, Artur Jordao
- **Abstract**: Deep learning drives a new wave in computing systems and triggers the automation of increasingly complex problems. In particular, Large Language Models (LLMs) have significantly advanced cognitive tasks, often matching or even surpassing human-level performance. However, their extensive parameters result in high computational costs and slow inference, posing challenges for deployment in resource-limited settings. Among the strategies to overcome the aforementioned challenges, pruning emerges as a successful mechanism since it reduces model size while maintaining predictive ability. In this paper, we introduce AMP: Attention Heads and MLP Pruning, a novel structured pruning method that efficiently compresses LLMs by removing less critical structures within Multi-Head Attention (MHA) and Multilayer Perceptron (MLP). By projecting the input data onto weights, AMP assesses structural importance and overcomes the limitations of existing techniques, which often fall short in flexibility or efficiency. In particular, AMP surpasses the current state-of-the-art on commonsense reasoning tasks by up to 1.49 percentage points, achieving a 30% pruning ratio with minimal impact on zero-shot task performance. Moreover, AMP also improves inference speeds, making it well-suited for deployment in resource-constrained environments. We confirm the flexibility of AMP on different families of LLMs, including LLaMA and Phi.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel structured pruning method called AMP (Attention Heads and MLP Pruning) for efficiently compressing Large Language Models (LLMs).  AMP leverages the magnitude of activations (rather than just weights) to assess the importance of attention heads and MLP neurons across layers, allowing it to selectively remove less critical components while preserving performance. AMP avoids the need for specialized hardware, requires minimal fine-tuning, and is demonstrated on different LLM families.  The authors show that AMP achieves state-of-the-art accuracy compared to other structured pruning techniques, achieves practical inference speedups, and is aligned with Green AI principles.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper presents a well-motivated and executed approach to structured pruning for LLMs. Its novelty lies in its activation-aware importance criterion and its ability to efficiently identify and remove less critical structures. However, the paper's practical impact might be limited by the relatively small compression ratios achieved and the significant performance degradation observed for smaller models (Phi family).

**Strengths:**

*   **Novel Importance Criterion:**  The use of activation magnitudes, as opposed to just weight magnitudes, is a solid idea. It's plausible that activation magnitudes capture more information about a neuron's contribution than just its weight alone. The coherence check offers convincing evidence that AMP effectively identifies less important structures.
*   **Efficiency:**  The method is computationally efficient and does not require specialized hardware, making it practical for deployment.
*   **Strong Empirical Results:**  The paper provides solid evidence that AMP outperforms existing structured pruning methods on common sense reasoning tasks, particularly for the LLaMA family.  The inference speedup is significant, and the demonstration across different LLM families (LLaMA and Phi) suggests general applicability.
*   **Green AI Alignment:** Highlighting reduced computational costs and emissions is valuable in the current context.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Compression Ratios:**  While the method demonstrates superior accuracy at certain compression rates (e.g., 30%), achieving higher compression ratios while maintaining acceptable performance remains a challenge. Other pruning approaches achieve higher sparsity (though not necessarily with the same efficiency).
*   **Performance Degradation on Smaller Models:** The substantial drop in performance on Phi models with pruning is a significant limitation. This suggests that the effectiveness of AMP might depend on model size.
*   **Limited Theoretical Justification:** While the activation-based approach seems intuitive, a deeper theoretical justification for its effectiveness would strengthen the paper.  Why *this* particular combination of activations and weights?
*   **Incremental Improvement:** While it outperforms the current state of the art, the improvement in some cases is small.  Is the added complexity justified by the relatively small gain? The improvement is only significant at the 30% pruning rate, and the Phi model experiences an accuracy drop.
*   **Limited Ablation:** The ablation of the MHA and MLP components sheds light on each component's contribution, but a more detailed ablation study of different components and parameters of the method could be beneficial. For example, what would the performance be with only the activation magnitudes?

**Significance:**

The paper demonstrates a practically useful method for structured pruning in LLMs. The activation-aware approach to importance estimation is novel. Despite the weaknesses mentioned, the proposed method can have an impact by improving efficiency, making LLMs more accessible and sustainable.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper is innovative and well-executed, presenting a practically useful structured pruning method. While it's not a groundbreaking theoretical contribution and has limitations regarding compression ratios and model sensitivity, the empirical results are compelling, and the efficiency of the method is attractive. The coherence check provides strong support for the method’s effectiveness. The paper meets the current need of an environmentally friendly means of reducing LLM's environmental footprint.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### AI-in-the-Loop Planning for Transportation Electrification: Case Studies from Austin, Texas
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21185v1)
- **Authors**: Seung Jun Choi
- **Abstract**: This study explores the integration of AI in transportation electrification planning in Austin, TX, focusing on the use of Geospatial AI (GeoAI), Generative AI (GenAI), and Large Language Models (LLMs). GeoAI enhances site selection, localized GenAI models support meta-level estimations, and LLMs enable scenario simulations. These AI applications require human oversight. GeoAI outputs must be evaluated with land use data, GenAI models are not always accurate, and LLMs are prone to hallucinations. To ensure accountable planning, human planners must work alongside AI agents. Establishing a community feedback loop is essential to audit automated decisions. Planners should place Community Experience (CX) at the center of Urban Planning AI.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the integration of AI, specifically Geospatial AI (GeoAI), Generative AI (GenAI), and Large Language Models (LLMs), into transportation electrification planning within Austin, Texas. It focuses on how these AI tools can assist with site selection, estimation, and scenario simulation for EV charging infrastructure. The paper highlights the necessity of human oversight to address potential biases, inaccuracies, and "hallucinations" inherent in AI models, emphasizing the importance of community feedback loops and placing community experience (CX) at the center of Urban Planning AI. It presents case studies illustrating the application of these AI tools and discusses the challenges and benefits of incorporating them into the planning process. The paper advocates for a balanced approach, where AI complements, rather than replaces, the expertise and ethical considerations of human planners.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper addresses a relevant and timely topic: the integration of AI into urban planning, specifically focusing on transportation electrification. The strengths of the paper lie in its practical application, offering real-world case studies in Austin, TX, and providing a clear framework for integrating GeoAI, GenAI, and LLMs into the planning process. It does a good job of highlighting the importance of human oversight and community involvement to ensure equitable and accountable planning outcomes. The paper successfully explores the Urban Al application in transportation electrification planning, as well as synthesizing the analytical findings, and discussion to offer guidance for future applications.

However, the paper's novelty and significance are somewhat limited. While it provides a useful synthesis of existing AI tools and their potential applications, it doesn't introduce any fundamentally new AI algorithms, planning methodologies, or theoretical frameworks. The case studies, while informative, lack a deep quantitative analysis of the AI's impact on planning efficiency, cost-effectiveness, or equity outcomes. The paper primarily describes the *potential* benefits of AI integration rather than *demonstrating* them rigorously with empirical data. It also does not elaborate deeply on the technical processes or architectures of the models used, relying on existing tools. Additionally, the discussion of community feedback mechanisms is somewhat underdeveloped and lacks specific details on how these loops can be effectively implemented and sustained. In several places, the paper is descriptive, rather than deeply analytical.

Moreover, the limitation that the Justice40 initiative is no longer continued under the Trump administration is outdated. The Biden Administration has re-established the initiative and continues to support it.

Overall, the paper offers a valuable overview and practical guidance for integrating AI into transportation electrification planning, but its limited novelty, reliance on existing AI tools, and a lack of rigorous quantitative analysis of AI's impact prevent it from making a major breakthrough contribution to the field.

Score: 5

Rationale: The paper presents a useful and practical overview of AI integration in urban planning, with some case study applications, but falls short of providing significant novelty, rigorous quantitative analysis, or a robust community feedback framework. Thus, the score reflects that the paper is slightly above average, but without making a substantive novel contribution to the field.

- **Classification**: cs.CY
- **Score**: 5/10

### GLIP-OOD: Zero-Shot Graph OOD Detection with Foundation Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21186v1)
- **Authors**: Haoyan Xu, Zhengtao Yao, Xuzhi Zhang, Ziyi Wang, Langzhou He, Yushun Dong, Philip S. Yu, Mengyuan Li, Yue Zhao
- **Abstract**: Out-of-distribution (OOD) detection is critical for ensuring the safety and reliability of machine learning systems, particularly in dynamic and open-world environments. In the vision and text domains, zero-shot OOD detection - which requires no training on in-distribution (ID) data - has made significant progress through the use of large-scale pretrained models such as vision-language models (VLMs) and large language models (LLMs). However, zero-shot OOD detection in graph-structured data remains largely unexplored, primarily due to the challenges posed by complex relational structures and the absence of powerful, large-scale pretrained models for graphs. In this work, we take the first step toward enabling zero-shot graph OOD detection by leveraging a graph foundation model (GFM). We show that, when provided only with class label names, the GFM can perform OOD detection without any node-level supervision - outperforming existing supervised methods across multiple datasets. To address the more practical setting where OOD label names are unavailable, we introduce GLIP-OOD, a novel framework that employs LLMs to generate semantically informative pseudo-OOD labels from unlabeled data. These labels enable the GFM to capture nuanced semantic boundaries between ID and OOD classes and perform fine-grained OOD detection - without requiring any labeled nodes. Our approach is the first to enable node-level graph OOD detection in a fully zero-shot setting, and achieves state-of-the-art performance on four benchmark text-attributed graph datasets.
- **Summary**: **Summary:**

The paper "GLIP-OOD: Zero-Shot Graph OOD Detection with Foundation Model" introduces a novel approach for out-of-distribution (OOD) detection in graph-structured data. The method leverages a graph foundation model (GFM) and large language models (LLMs) to perform OOD detection without requiring any node-level labeled data. The core idea is to use LLMs to generate pseudo-OOD labels from unlabeled data, which are then used by the GFM to distinguish between in-distribution (ID) and OOD nodes. The authors demonstrate that their approach, termed GLIP-OOD, achieves state-of-the-art performance on several benchmark datasets, even surpassing supervised methods in some cases. The approach is assessed in two settings: zero-shot with access to all true label names, and a more practical setting where only ID label names are known, using the LLM to generate pseudo-OOD labels.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper addresses a significant and relatively unexplored problem in graph machine learning: zero-shot OOD detection. Existing graph OOD methods typically rely on labeled ID data, which can be expensive and impractical to obtain in real-world scenarios. The authors' approach of leveraging GFMs and LLMs to perform OOD detection without any labeled nodes represents a substantial step forward.

**Strengths:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a genuinely novel approach to graph OOD detection, moving away from traditional supervised methods and leveraging recent advances in foundation models.
*   **Zero-Shot Setting:** The ability to perform OOD detection without any labeled node data is a significant advantage in many real-world applications.
*   **Performance:** The experimental results demonstrate that GLIP-OOD achieves state-of-the-art performance on several benchmark datasets, surpassing existing supervised methods in the fully labelled setting and remaining competitive with LLM-generated pseudo labels.
*   **Practicality:** The approach is relatively simple to implement and can be applied to various graph datasets with textual attributes.
*   **LLM-Based OOD Label Generation:** The LLM-based approach to generate OOD categories from unlabeled data, combined with a GFM, forms an interesting avenue of exploration.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Dependence on GFMs and LLMs:** The method relies heavily on the quality and availability of GFMs and LLMs. The performance of GLIP-OOD is likely to be limited by the capabilities of these underlying models. Also, the method is tested on datasets used during the pretraining of the GFM, hence one might be sceptical of the zero-shot results.
*   **TAG Limitation:** As the authors also mention, the application is tested on text-attributed graphs (TAGs). This limits the applicability to graphs that don't have textual attributes, although, the labels could potentially be generated based on other available node attributes such as embeddings derived from structure.
*   **Potential Bias:** The use of LLMs for label generation introduces the potential for bias. The generated pseudo-OOD labels may reflect the biases present in the LLM's training data, which could affect the accuracy and fairness of OOD detection.
*   **Transductive setting:** Even with generating pseudo labels for the OOD classes, the method requires transductive learning, limiting its applicability to real world open graph setting.

**Significance:**

The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of graph machine learning by enabling OOD detection in scenarios where labeled data is scarce or unavailable. The approach could be particularly valuable in domains such as social network analysis, cybersecurity, and fraud detection, where detecting novel or anomalous patterns is crucial. The use of LLMs to generate pseudo-OOD labels is a promising direction for future research in OOD detection.

The zero-shot results are slightly misleading given that the pre-trained GFMs have probably seen some of the datasets and the method inherently requires transductive inference.

Score: 7

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and practical approach to a significant problem. The experimental results are promising, and the method has the potential to influence the field. However, the dependence on GFMs and LLMs, the restriction to text-attributed graphs, the potential for bias, and the zero shot results limit the immediate impact of the work. The score reflects the significant contribution of the paper while acknowledging its limitations.
- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Artificial Intelligence for Personalized Prediction of Alzheimer's Disease Progression: A Survey of Methods, Data Challenges, and Future Directions
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21189v1)
- **Authors**: Gulsah Hancerliogullari Koksalmis, Bulent Soykan, Laura J. Brattain, Hsin-Hsiung Huang
- **Abstract**: Alzheimer's Disease (AD) is marked by significant inter-individual variability in its progression, complicating accurate prognosis and personalized care planning. This heterogeneity underscores the critical need for predictive models capable of forecasting patient-specific disease trajectories. Artificial Intelligence (AI) offers powerful tools to address this challenge by analyzing complex, multi-modal, and longitudinal patient data. This paper provides a comprehensive survey of AI methodologies applied to personalized AD progression prediction. We review key approaches including state-space models for capturing temporal dynamics, deep learning techniques like Recurrent Neural Networks for sequence modeling, Graph Neural Networks (GNNs) for leveraging network structures, and the emerging concept of AI-driven digital twins for individualized simulation. Recognizing that data limitations often impede progress, we examine common challenges such as high dimensionality, missing data, and dataset imbalance. We further discuss AI-driven mitigation strategies, with a specific focus on synthetic data generation using Variational Autoencoders (VAEs) and Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs) to augment and balance datasets. The survey synthesizes the strengths and limitations of current approaches, emphasizing the trend towards multimodal integration and the persistent need for model interpretability and generalizability. Finally, we identify critical open challenges, including robust external validation, clinical integration, and ethical considerations, and outline promising future research directions such as hybrid models, causal inference, and federated learning. This review aims to consolidate current knowledge and guide future efforts in developing clinically relevant AI tools for personalized AD prognostication.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've analyzed the provided paper on "Artificial Intelligence for Personalized Prediction of Alzheimer's Disease Progression: A Survey of Methods, Data Challenges, and Future Directions."

**Summary:**

This survey paper comprehensively reviews the application of artificial intelligence (AI) techniques for predicting the progression of Alzheimer's Disease (AD) at an individual patient level. It covers various AI methodologies, including state-space models, deep learning (RNNs, GNNs, CNNs), and digital twin frameworks. The paper also addresses the challenges associated with AD data, such as high dimensionality, missing data, and class imbalance, and explores AI-driven mitigation strategies like synthetic data generation using VAEs and GANs. The review synthesizes the strengths and limitations of existing approaches, emphasizing the trend towards multimodal integration and the need for model interpretability and generalizability. Finally, it identifies open challenges like robust external validation, clinical integration, ethical considerations, and outlines future research directions (hybrid models, causal inference, federated learning).  The paper concludes by arguing that AI has the potential to significantly advance personalized AD management.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Coverage:** The survey provides a broad overview of AI methodologies applied to personalized AD progression prediction, covering a wide range of techniques and approaches.
*   **Addresses Practical Challenges:** The paper acknowledges and discusses common data challenges specific to AD research, such as high dimensionality, missing data, and class imbalance, which are often overlooked in purely methodological papers. It is a valuable contribution to the community as these are serious practical constraints.
*   **Discusses Mitigation Strategies:**  It comprehensively discusses AI-driven methods for mitigating these challenges, with a focus on synthetic data generation, including VAEs and GANs, which are highly relevant.
*   **Identifies Open Challenges and Future Directions:** The paper highlights important open questions and future research directions, which can guide future research efforts in the field. The emphasis on clinical integration, ethical considerations, and the modeling of therapeutic interventions is particularly valuable.
*   **Well-Structured and Clear:** The paper is well-organized and written in a clear and accessible manner, making it easy to follow for researchers and practitioners.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Depth in Certain Areas:** While comprehensive, the paper cannot delve into the specific implementation details of each AI methodology due to space constraints. Some sections provide a high-level overview without going into the nuances of each method's adaptation to the AD domain.
*   **Focus on Traditional ML/DL:** While the paper covers a range of established techniques, it could benefit from including the most recent advances, such as the role of large language models for generating synthetic longitudinal data or for knowledge integration from medical literature.
*   **Limited discussion on causality:** The paper mentions causal inference as an avenue for further research, but doesn't deeply engage with the limitations on current research based on observational datasets and difficulties with learning/testing causal associations in complex datasets.
*   **Limited Evaluation of Datasets:** Although the datasets utilized are mentioned, there is a lack of analysis in comparing the datasets. ADNI is presented as a go-to dataset, but it is becoming important to analyse its limitations.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to the field by providing a well-organized and accessible survey of AI techniques for personalized AD progression prediction. Its significance lies in:

*   **Consolidating Existing Knowledge:** It brings together a fragmented literature into a cohesive overview, making it easier for researchers to understand the current state of the art.
*   **Highlighting Critical Challenges:** It emphasizes the practical challenges associated with AD data and the importance of addressing them for building robust and clinically relevant AI models.
*   **Guiding Future Research:** It identifies key open questions and future directions, which can stimulate further research efforts in the field.

While the paper doesn't present entirely novel technical contributions, its value as a comprehensive and insightful survey justifies a solid score.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:**  A score of 7.5 reflects the paper's strong comprehensiveness, clear presentation, and identification of important challenges and future directions. The survey fulfills a crucial need in the AI and AD research communities by synthesizing disparate information and highlighting key areas for future work. While it lacks deep dives into specific techniques and doesn't showcase groundbreaking methodological innovation, its comprehensive scope, practical focus, and clear articulation of challenges and future directions make it a valuable contribution to the field. The score acknowledges the robust structure and thorough research evident in the paper, as well as some limitations in depth and coverage of the newest trends.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Small or Large? Zero-Shot or Finetuned? Guiding Language Model Choice for Specialized Applications in Healthcare
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21191v1)
- **Authors**: Lovedeep Gondara, Jonathan Simkin, Graham Sayle, Shebnum Devji, Gregory Arbour, Raymond Ng
- **Abstract**: This study aims to guide language model selection by investigating: 1) the necessity of finetuning versus zero-shot usage, 2) the benefits of domain-adjacent versus generic pretrained models, 3) the value of further domain-specific pretraining, and 4) the continued relevance of Small Language Models (SLMs) compared to Large Language Models (LLMs) for specific tasks. Using electronic pathology reports from the British Columbia Cancer Registry (BCCR), three classification scenarios with varying difficulty and data size are evaluated. Models include various SLMs and an LLM. SLMs are evaluated both zero-shot and finetuned; the LLM is evaluated zero-shot only. Finetuning significantly improved SLM performance across all scenarios compared to their zero-shot results. The zero-shot LLM outperformed zero-shot SLMs but was consistently outperformed by finetuned SLMs. Domain-adjacent SLMs generally performed better than the generic SLM after finetuning, especially on harder tasks. Further domain-specific pretraining yielded modest gains on easier tasks but significant improvements on the complex, data-scarce task. The results highlight the critical role of finetuning for SLMs in specialized domains, enabling them to surpass zero-shot LLM performance on targeted classification tasks. Pretraining on domain-adjacent or domain-specific data provides further advantages, particularly for complex problems or limited finetuning data. While LLMs offer strong zero-shot capabilities, their performance on these specific tasks did not match that of appropriately finetuned SLMs. In the era of LLMs, SLMs remain relevant and effective, offering a potentially superior performance-resource trade-off compared to LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the optimal strategy for using language models (LMs) in specialized healthcare applications, particularly using electronic pathology reports. It investigates whether fine-tuning is necessary compared to zero-shot usage, the advantages of domain-adjacent vs. generic pre-trained models, the utility of further domain-specific pre-training, and the continued relevance of smaller language models (SLMs) relative to large language models (LLMs). Using cancer registry data, the study evaluates several models (including RoBERTa, PathologyBERT, Gatortron, and a Mistral-based LLM) across three classification scenarios with varying complexity and data availability.  The results show that fine-tuning significantly improves SLM performance, allowing them to surpass zero-shot LLM performance.  Domain-adjacent pre-training offers further benefits, especially for complex tasks with limited data. The paper concludes that in the realm of specialized applications, fine-tuned SLMs remain relevant and provide a potentially superior performance-resource trade-off compared to LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper addresses a very practical and important question: how to best leverage the power of language models in specialized domains like healthcare. The comparative evaluation across multiple scenarios with varying levels of complexity and data constraints strengthens the paper's conclusions. The focus on electronic pathology reports is a specific yet relevant example within healthcare, which enhances the paper's practicality.

Here are some strengths and weaknesses:

**Strengths:**

*   **Practical Relevance:** The study tackles a real-world problem in healthcare informatics.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper explores various scenarios and models, offering a well-rounded analysis.
*   **Clear and Actionable Recommendations:** The authors provide concrete recommendations for practitioners.
*   **Focus on Trade-offs:** The paper appropriately balances the considerations of performance, resource utilization, and data availability.
