By sampling multiple outputs per problem and defining the Sample Optimal Length (SOL) as the shortest correct response among all the outputs, our method dynamically guides the model toward optimal inference lengths. Applied to the DeepSeek-Distill-Qwen-1.5B model, ShorterBetter achieves up to an 80% reduction in output length on both in-domain and out-of-domain reasoning tasks while maintaining accuracy. Our analysis shows that overly long reasoning traces often reflect loss of reasoning direction, and thus suggests that the extended CoT produced by reasoning models is highly compressible.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "ShorterBetter," a reinforcement learning method designed to guide reasoning language models (LLMs) to discover their own optimal Chain-of-Thought (CoT) lengths for efficient reasoning. The core idea is to define a "Sample Optimal Length" (SOL) as the shortest correct response among multiple generated outputs for a given problem.  The model is then trained using Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) to maximize correctness while minimizing deviation from the SOL, effectively penalizing overly long reasoning traces.  Experiments using the DeepSeek-Distill-Qwen models on math reasoning tasks demonstrate a significant reduction (up to 80%) in output length without compromising accuracy, suggesting that extended CoT outputs are often highly compressible and can reflect a loss of reasoning direction. The paper also explores out-of-domain generalization and conducts ablation studies to validate the importance of SOL.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its *dynamic* approach to CoT length optimization via reinforcement learning, moving away from manually defined length constraints or stopping criteria. The SOL metric and its use in GRPO are a sensible and well-defined approach for learning optimal length, although the underlying GRPO mechanism is pre-existing. The idea of self-discovery of the optimal length by the model is appealing. It builds upon previous work in efficient LLM reasoning and self-play, but provides a distinct improvement by incentivizing concise *correct* reasoning.

*   **Significance:** The potential significance is high. LLMs are computationally expensive, and optimizing inference efficiency is crucial for wider adoption. Demonstrating that reasoning traces can be substantially compressed *without* sacrificing accuracy addresses a key bottleneck. The finding that excessively long reasoning can signal a loss of direction is a valuable insight and could influence future research in CoT prompting. Furthermore, out-of-domain generalization is strong, which increases applicability. The approach is relatively simple to implement, which promotes replicability and adoption.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear problem statement and well-defined methodology:** The paper clearly articulates the issue of overthinking in LLMs and proposes a straightforward and easily understood method.
    *   **Solid experimental validation:** The empirical results demonstrate the effectiveness of ShorterBetter in reducing output length while maintaining accuracy on a variety of math reasoning tasks, both in-domain and out-of-domain.
    *   **Insightful analysis:** The analysis of the relationship between output length and reasoning correctness provides valuable insights into the behavior of LLMs.
    *   **Ablation studies**: The ablation studies effectively demonstrate the usefulness of their particular SOL reward formulation.
    *   **Open source**: The use of existing and accessible frameworks allows for further extension and reproducibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited model scope:** The primary experiments focus on the DeepSeek-Distill-Qwen models. While these are strong baselines, it would be beneficial to see the method's performance on a wider range of architectures and model sizes.
    *   **Task limitations:** While out-of-domain transfer is good, the reliance on math reasoning tasks as the primary testbed leaves room for questions about the method's effectiveness on other types of reasoning, such as commonsense or logical reasoning. Further experiments on tasks beyond mathematical problem solving would strengthen the paper.
    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity**: While the reported ablation studies shed light on the contribution of each design choice, further work could explore a more in-depth study of the hyperparameter space.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in efficient LLM reasoning, CoT prompting, and reinforcement learning for language models. The ShorterBetter method could be used as a building block for more sophisticated approaches to adaptive reasoning and length control. The findings regarding the compressibility of CoT outputs could also motivate new techniques for knowledge distillation and model compression.

*   **Justification for Score:**

    Given the strengths and weaknesses, I am assigning a score of **7/10**.

    *   The paper presents a novel and effective approach to optimizing CoT length.
    *   The experimental results are convincing and support the claims made by the authors.
    *   The paper provides valuable insights into the behavior of LLMs and the relationship between reasoning length and correctness.
    *   There are some limitations in the scope of model architectures and tasks, but the results are promising and the potential influence is high. It is not a *groundbreaking* paper introducing a completely new paradigm, but a *significant and well-executed* contribution to the field of efficient LLM reasoning. A higher score could have been justified with testing against a greater range of LLMs, or a better quantitative exploration of different types of reasoning.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Retrieval-Enhanced Few-Shot Prompting for Speech Event Extraction
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21372v1)
- **Authors**: Máté Gedeon
- **Abstract**: Speech Event Extraction (SpeechEE) is a challenging task that lies at the intersection of Automatic Speech Recognition (ASR) and Natural Language Processing (NLP), requiring the identification of structured event information from spoken language. In this work, we present a modular, pipeline-based SpeechEE framework that integrates high-performance ASR with semantic search-enhanced prompting of Large Language Models (LLMs). Our system first classifies speech segments likely to contain events using a hybrid filtering mechanism including rule-based, BERT-based, and LLM-based models. It then employs few-shot LLM prompting, dynamically enriched via semantic similarity retrieval, to identify event triggers and extract corresponding arguments. We evaluate the pipeline using multiple LLMs (Llama3-8B, GPT-4o-mini, and o1-mini) highlighting significant performance gains with o1-mini, which achieves 63.3% F1 on trigger classification and 27.8% F1 on argument classification, outperforming prior benchmarks. Our results demonstrate that pipeline approaches, when empowered by retrieval-augmented LLMs, can rival or exceed end-to-end systems while maintaining interpretability and modularity. This work provides practical insights into LLM-driven event extraction and opens pathways for future hybrid models combining textual and acoustic features.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Retrieval-Enhanced Few-Shot Prompting for Speech Event Extraction":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a pipeline-based framework for Speech Event Extraction (SpeechEE).  It combines high-performance ASR (Whisper or Canary) with a classification module to identify event-containing speech segments, followed by LLM-based (Llama3, GPT-4o, 01-mini) few-shot prompting enhanced by semantic similarity retrieval to identify event triggers and extract arguments. Semantic search is used to dynamically select relevant examples for few-shot prompting. The paper evaluates different LLMs and ASR systems, demonstrating significant performance gains, especially with the 01-mini model, on trigger and argument classification, outperforming prior benchmarks. The study argues that pipeline approaches, when combined with retrieval-augmented LLMs, can rival or exceed end-to-end systems while maintaining interpretability and modularity.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in the *combination* of existing techniques within the SpeechEE domain. While ASR, LLMs, few-shot prompting, and semantic similarity retrieval are all established, their specific integration within a SpeechEE pipeline, *especially the semantic search-enhanced prompting*, is a worthwhile contribution. The hybrid classification module to filter likely event utterances also provides a practical improvement.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from demonstrating that a well-designed pipeline, empowered by LLMs and strategic prompting, *can* challenge the perceived superiority of end-to-end models in SpeechEE. This is valuable because pipeline architectures offer greater interpretability, modularity, and ease of debugging. The improved performance (relative to baselines), especially on argument extraction, is a tangible benefit. The detailed ablation study provides practical insights for configuring such systems. This can inform future hybrid models combining textual and acoustic features.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   *Clear methodology*: The paper provides a well-defined architecture and detailed description of each component, which is key for reproducibility and follow-up work.
    *   *Comprehensive experiments*: The ablation study offers valuable insights into the impact of different design choices, particularly the filtering mechanism.
    *   *Practical focus*: The work addresses a practical problem in SpeechEE and provides actionable guidance for building effective systems.
    *   *Addresses limitations of LLMs for SpeechEE*: Recognizes the tendency of LLMs to hallucinate events and implements a creative method to reduce false positives.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   *Dataset Limitations:* The paper acknowledges that the SpeechEE dataset is a modified version of ACE2005-EN+, so direct comparisons with other research using the original dataset must be done cautiously. The relatively small size of SpeechEE may also limit the generalizability of the findings.
    *   *Proprietary Model Reliance:* The best results are achieved with 01-mini, a proprietary model which limits accessibility and reproducibility. While other open-source options like Llama3 are tested, their performance is notably weaker.
    *   *ASR Choice Limited Impact:* The conclusion that the ASR system (Whisper vs. Canary) has limited impact may be premature. The inherent non-determinism of LLM outputs could mask more subtle differences in ASR performance. A more robust comparison with multiple runs per prompt would be needed to solidify this claim.
    *   *Audio Cue Neglect*: The paper does not leverage speech-related cues present in the audio. The approach is primarily transcript-based, and it mentions this as a key point of improvement for future work. This is a critical gap.
    *  *Lack of comparison with existing models that use few-shot or retrieval augmented methods*: There has been a growing body of work related to few-shot learning, and also retrieval augmented systems, but there's no comparison to existing models in the results to understand the importance of the work in relationship to similar methods for improvement.

*   **Overall Assessment:**

The paper makes a solid contribution by demonstrating a practical and effective SpeechEE pipeline leveraging recent advances in LLMs and semantic retrieval. While the techniques themselves aren't entirely novel, their specific combination and the resulting performance improvements justify the work. The limitations around the proprietary model reliance and lack of comparison to similar methods negatively affect the overall assessment.

Score: 6
The score reflects the paper's value in demonstrating a practical architecture and providing insights into pipeline-based SpeechEE, along with the fact that it uses off-the-shelf and existing methods, and the reliance on proprietary APIs limits reproducibility and real-world applicability.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Sparse-to-Sparse Training of Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21380v1)
- **Authors**: Inês Cardoso Oliveira, Decebal Constantin Mocanu, Luis A. Leiva
- **Abstract**: Diffusion models (DMs) are a powerful type of generative models that have achieved state-of-the-art results in various image synthesis tasks and have shown potential in other domains, such as natural language processing and temporal data modeling. Despite their stable training dynamics and ability to produce diverse high-quality samples, DMs are notorious for requiring significant computational resources, both in the training and inference stages. Previous work has focused mostly on increasing the efficiency of model inference. This paper introduces, for the first time, the paradigm of sparse-to-sparse training to DMs, with the aim of improving both training and inference efficiency. We focus on unconditional generation and train sparse DMs from scratch (Latent Diffusion and ChiroDiff) on six datasets using three different methods (Static-DM, RigL-DM, and MagRan-DM) to study the effect of sparsity in model performance. Our experiments show that sparse DMs are able to match and often outperform their Dense counterparts, while substantially reducing the number of trainable parameters and FLOPs. We also identify safe and effective values to perform sparse-to-sparse training of DMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces the concept of sparse-to-sparse training for diffusion models (DMs), aiming to improve both training and inference efficiency.  The authors train sparse DMs from scratch on unconditional generation tasks, using Latent Diffusion and ChiroDiff models across several datasets. They explore three sparsity methods: Static-DM, RigL-DM, and MagRan-DM. The results show that sparse DMs can match or even outperform their dense counterparts in performance while significantly reducing trainable parameters and FLOPs. The paper also identifies effective values for sparsity-related hyperparameters.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of applying sparse-to-sparse training to *diffusion models* appears novel.  Previous work has focused more on inference speedups or adaptations of existing, fully trained models for sparsity. Training from scratch with sparsity is a significant and unexplored area. The combination of static and dynamic sparsity techniques within the DM framework adds another layer of novelty.

* **Significance:** Reducing the computational cost of DMs is crucial for their wider adoption and further development. The paper's results demonstrating comparable (or even superior) performance with significantly fewer resources have the potential to impact the field. If the proposed methods can be generalized to other DM architectures and tasks, it would be a valuable contribution. The identification of appropriate sparsity levels and prune/regrowth ratios is also significant for practical application.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear problem statement:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of DMs and the motivation for improving their efficiency.
    * **Systematic evaluation:** The authors conduct a comprehensive set of experiments across multiple models, datasets, and sparsity techniques.
    * **Reproducibility (potential):**  The methodology is described in sufficient detail, and the authors commit to releasing code and trained models.
    * **Interesting findings:** The observation that sparse DMs can sometimes *outperform* dense DMs is intriguing and warrants further investigation. The analysis of static vs. dynamic sparsity and the effect of prune/regrowth rates provides valuable insights.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited scope:** The paper focuses on unconditional generation. While this is a reasonable starting point, the results need to be validated on conditional generation and other DM applications.
    * **Hardware limitations:** As acknowledged by the authors, the current hardware isn't optimized for unstructured sparsity, which potentially limits the true gains in runtime. Real-world performance gains might be significantly higher with specialized hardware.
    * **Lack of a comprehensive theoretical explanation:** The paper provides empirical results, but lacks a deep theoretical understanding of *why* sparsity works so well in DMs. For instance, understanding the interaction between sparsity and regularization in the context of DM training could add significant value.
   * **Limited comparisons:** Although the approach is novel, further comparisons with existing efficient diffusion training methods or model compression techniques (e.g. quantization, knowledge distillation) would better contextualize the advantages of the proposed methods.
   * **FID as the sole metric:** Relying solely on FID as the performance metric can be limiting. Exploring other metrics like perceptual quality, or diversity metrics (e.g. inception score) would provide a more comprehensive evaluation.

* **Potential Impact:**
    * **Future research direction:** This paper could stimulate further research into sparse-to-sparse training for DMs, potentially leading to even more efficient architectures and training methods.
    * **Practical applications:** The reduced computational cost could make DMs more accessible for resource-constrained environments and accelerate research and development in various domains.

* **Justification of Score:**
While the paper presents a significant and novel approach to training diffusion models, several shortcomings limit its overall impact, preventing it from being rated as a 9 or 10. The lack of theoretical underpinnings and comparisons with related work diminishes the overall impact of this work. Nevertheless, given the potential benefits of training diffusion models efficiently, this is a key first step.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### IDDM: Bridging Synthetic-to-Real Domain Gap from Physics-Guided Diffusion for Real-world Image Dehazing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21385v1)
- **Authors**: Shijun Zhou, Yajing Liu, Chunhui Hao, Zhiyuan Liu, Jiandong Tian
- **Abstract**: Due to the domain gap between real-world and synthetic hazy images, current data-driven dehazing algorithms trained on synthetic datasets perform well on synthetic data but struggle to generalize to real-world scenarios. To address this challenge, we propose \textbf{I}mage \textbf{D}ehazing \textbf{D}iffusion \textbf{M}odels (IDDM), a novel diffusion process that incorporates the atmospheric scattering model into noise diffusion. IDDM aims to use the gradual haze formation process to help the denoising Unet robustly learn the distribution of clear images from the conditional input hazy images. We design a specialized training strategy centered around IDDM. Diffusion models are leveraged to bridge the domain gap from synthetic to real-world, while the atmospheric scattering model provides physical guidance for haze formation. During the forward process, IDDM simultaneously introduces haze and noise into clear images, and then robustly separates them during the sampling process. By training with physics-guided information, IDDM shows the ability of domain generalization, and effectively restores the real-world hazy images despite being trained on synthetic datasets. Extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of our method through both quantitative and qualitative comparisons with state-of-the-art approaches.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper introduces Image Dehazing Diffusion Models (IDDM), a new approach to address the domain gap between synthetic and real-world hazy images.  IDDM integrates the atmospheric scattering model into a diffusion process, guiding the learning process with physical principles of haze formation.  The method involves simultaneously adding haze and noise during the forward diffusion process and then robustly separating them during sampling. A specialized training strategy is developed. The authors claim that IDDM achieves domain generalization and effectively restores real-world hazy images even when trained on synthetic data. Extensive experiments are conducted to compare IDDM with state-of-the-art dehazing methods, showing improved quantitative and qualitative results.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the explicit integration of the atmospheric scattering model into the diffusion process for image dehazing. While diffusion models have been applied to image restoration tasks before, incorporating domain-specific physical models as a guided condition within the diffusion framework is a notable contribution.  The three-stage training strategy, which first learns to generate haze, then estimates it, and finally integrates the estimate into the dehazing network, is also a novel aspect of the approach.  The idea of relating the forward diffusion process to haze formation has potential.

*   **Significance:** The problem of domain generalization in image dehazing is significant.  Many existing methods trained on synthetic data fail to generalize well to real-world scenarios. A method that can bridge this gap, especially by leveraging physical principles, would be valuable. The results shown on RTTS and BEDDE datasets, with reported improvements in multiple metrics, support the claim of improved generalization. The approach is also significant for addressing the hazy image degradation problems in real-world applications like autonomous driving and surveillance.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Physically-informed approach: Incorporating the atmospheric scattering model provides a strong inductive bias, making the learning process more robust and interpretable.
    *   Domain generalization: The method demonstrates improved performance on real-world datasets despite training on synthetic data, addressing a key limitation of existing methods.
    *   Quantitative and qualitative results:  The paper presents strong quantitative results demonstrating improvement over existing methods on standard benchmarks. Qualitative results support the claim of better haze removal and artifact reduction.
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the IDDM framework and its training strategy.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Complexity:  Diffusion models are computationally expensive, both during training and inference. The paper does not sufficiently address the computational overhead of the IDDM approach compared to other dehazing methods.
    *   Limited Real-World Data: While performance on real-world datasets improves, using more diverse and challenging real-world hazy images for training/fine-tuning, along with associated ablation studies, would further strengthen the impact of the work.
    *   Hyperparameter sensitivity. The approach seems to require fine-tuning many hyperparameters, such as the time steps T and the atmospheric parameters.
    *   The paper mentions using "careful" normalisation.  This step could be a more carefully considered as a component of the architecture.
    *   Some details on the network architecture of the HtNet could have been helpful.

*   **Potential Influence:** If the approach proves scalable and computationally efficient, it could become a standard technique for image dehazing, especially in scenarios where labeled real-world data is scarce. The idea of integrating physical models into diffusion-based restoration methods could inspire further research in other image processing domains.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper introduces a genuinely novel approach by embedding physical priors into a diffusion model for image dehazing, leading to improved domain generalization capabilities. The results are compelling, demonstrating advantages over state-of-the-art methods. However, the computational complexity inherent in diffusion models presents a practical limitation. More extensive evaluations on a diverse set of real-world datasets and further ablation studies, including addressing the computational requirements, could raise the impact even higher. Given these considerations, a score of 7.5 reflects the strong contributions of the paper balanced with some remaining limitations in practical applicability and scope of evaluation.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Who Gets the Callback? Generative AI and Gender Bias
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21400v1)
- **Authors**: Sugat Chaturvedi, Rochana Chaturvedi
- **Abstract**: Generative artificial intelligence (AI), particularly large language models (LLMs), is being rapidly deployed in recruitment and for candidate shortlisting. We audit several mid-sized open-source LLMs for gender bias using a dataset of 332,044 real-world online job postings. For each posting, we prompt the model to recommend whether an equally qualified male or female candidate should receive an interview callback. We find that most models tend to favor men, especially for higher-wage roles. Mapping job descriptions to the Standard Occupational Classification system, we find lower callback rates for women in male-dominated occupations and higher rates in female-associated ones, indicating occupational segregation. A comprehensive analysis of linguistic features in job ads reveals strong alignment of model recommendations with traditional gender stereotypes. To examine the role of recruiter identity, we steer model behavior by infusing Big Five personality traits and simulating the perspectives of historical figures. We find that less agreeable personas reduce stereotyping, consistent with an agreeableness bias in LLMs. Our findings highlight how AI-driven hiring may perpetuate biases in the labor market and have implications for fairness and diversity within firms.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates gender bias in several mid-sized open-source Large Language Models (LLMs) used for recruitment. Using a large dataset of real-world job postings, the authors prompt the LLMs to recommend whether a male or female candidate should receive an interview callback. The study finds that most models exhibit gender bias, favoring men, especially for higher-paying positions. The analysis reveals a connection between model recommendations and traditional gender stereotypes, occupational segregation, and linguistic features in job ads. The paper also explores how LLM "personality" (induced through prompting) affects bias, finding that less agreeable personas reduce stereotyping. The authors conclude that AI-driven hiring could perpetuate existing biases in the labor market and discuss implications for fairness and diversity.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Relevance and Timeliness:** The topic is highly relevant and timely given the increasing adoption of AI in recruitment and the growing concerns about algorithmic bias.
    *   **Large-Scale Analysis:** The use of a large dataset of real-world job postings (332,044) provides a robust foundation for the findings.
    *   **Comprehensive Methodology:** The study employs a multi-faceted approach, including analysis of callback rates, occupational segregation, wage disparities, linguistic features, and the influence of LLM personalities.
    *   **Novelty in Personality Steering:** Exploring how LLM personality affects bias is a relatively novel aspect that provides insights into the internal mechanisms and biases of these models. The simulated historical figure analysis is particularly interesting.
    *   **Policy Implications:** The paper directly addresses policy considerations by investigating how to adjust LLM thresholds for fairness and mitigate wage gaps and occupational segregation.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Model Scope:** While the paper examines several LLMs, the findings may not generalize to all models, especially those with different architectures or training data. The focus on mid-sized, open-source models, while practical, limits the generalizability to closed-source or larger models like those from OpenAI.
    *   **Indian Context:** The dataset is based on the Indian job market, which may have unique cultural and socio-economic factors influencing gender biases. The results may not be directly transferable to other countries.
    *   **Prompt Engineering:** The study's results are sensitive to the specific prompts used. While the authors provide the prompts in the Appendix, the potential for prompt bias influencing the LLM's responses should be carefully considered.
    *   **Oversimplification of "Personality":**  While the attempt to modulate LLM behavior by infusing personality traits is interesting, personality is a complex construct, and this paper may be oversimplifying it. The methodology lacks a ground truth comparison between what the researchers tried to achieve and what the model actually ended up acting like.
    *   **Lack of Model Interpretability:** LLMs are still largely black boxes. While the paper identifies correlations between linguistic features and bias, it doesn't fully explain the underlying mechanisms driving these biases. The models' choices for recommendations remains rather opaque.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The study provides valuable empirical evidence of gender bias in LLMs used for recruitment, raising awareness among researchers, practitioners, and policymakers.
    *   The analysis of linguistic features and LLM personality offers new avenues for understanding and mitigating algorithmic bias.
    *   The findings have implications for designing fairer AI-driven hiring systems and promoting diversity in the labor market.

**Justification for Score:**

While the study has several strengths, the limitations regarding model scope, context specificity, prompt engineering, oversimplification of "personality", and model interpretability hold it back from achieving a higher score. The large dataset and novel analysis of personality traits contribute meaningfully, but the specific context of the study and the limited generalizability reduce the potential influence on the field.
It adds significantly to the recent auditing literature, but the reliance on mid-sized open source models and single country/culture diminishes it.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: econ.GN
- **Score**: 7/10

### Diff-Prompt: Diffusion-Driven Prompt Generator with Mask Supervision
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21423v1)
- **Authors**: Weicai Yan, Wang Lin, Zirun Guo, Ye Wang, Fangming Feng, Xiaoda Yang, Zehan Wang, Tao Jin
- **Abstract**: Prompt learning has demonstrated promising results in fine-tuning pre-trained multimodal models. However, the performance improvement is limited when applied to more complex and fine-grained tasks. The reason is that most existing methods directly optimize the parameters involved in the prompt generation process through loss backpropagation, which constrains the richness and specificity of the prompt representations. In this paper, we propose Diffusion-Driven Prompt Generator (Diff-Prompt), aiming to use the diffusion model to generate rich and fine-grained prompt information for complex downstream tasks. Specifically, our approach consists of three stages. In the first stage, we train a Mask-VAE to compress the masks into latent space. In the second stage, we leverage an improved Diffusion Transformer (DiT) to train a prompt generator in the latent space, using the masks for supervision. In the third stage, we align the denoising process of the prompt generator with the pre-trained model in the semantic space, and use the generated prompts to fine-tune the model. We conduct experiments on a complex pixel-level downstream task, referring expression comprehension, and compare our method with various parameter-efficient fine-tuning approaches. Diff-Prompt achieves a maximum improvement of 8.87 in R@1 and 14.05 in R@5 compared to the foundation model and also outperforms other state-of-the-art methods across multiple metrics. The experimental results validate the effectiveness of our approach and highlight the potential of using generative models for prompt generation. Code is available at https://github.com/Kelvin-ywc/diff-prompt.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "DIFF-PROMPT: DIFFUSION-DRIVEN PROMPT GENERATOR WITH MASK SUPERVISION".

**Summary:**
The paper proposes a new method called Diff-Prompt for generating effective prompts for fine-tuning pre-trained multimodal models. Addressing the limitations of existing prompt learning methods, which often struggle with complex and fine-grained tasks, Diff-Prompt uses a diffusion model to generate rich, task-specific prompt information. The approach involves three stages: (1) training a Mask-VAE to compress masks into a latent space, (2) leveraging an improved Diffusion Transformer (DiT) to train a prompt generator in the latent space using mask supervision, and (3) aligning the denoising process of the prompt generator with the pre-trained model in the semantic space. Experiments on referring expression comprehension demonstrate that Diff-Prompt outperforms other parameter-efficient fine-tuning approaches.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's core novelty lies in the innovative use of a diffusion model for prompt generation. This approach deviates from traditional methods that directly optimize prompt parameters via backpropagation. Using masks as supervision to guide the diffusion model is also a novel idea for generating fine-grained and informative prompts. While diffusion models are established, their application to prompt engineering in this manner presents a significant contribution. The multi-stage framework combining Mask-VAE, DiT, and semantic alignment offers a novel architectural design.

*   **Significance:** The proposed method has the potential to significantly impact the field of prompt learning, particularly in the context of complex multimodal tasks. By generating more informative and task-specific prompts, Diff-Prompt improves the performance of pre-trained models without requiring extensive fine-tuning. This addresses the limitations of existing methods and opens new avenues for applying pre-trained models to a wider range of applications. The improvements in performance metrics (R@1 and R@5) demonstrate the practical significance of the approach. The extensive experiments and ablation studies further support the method's effectiveness and robustness.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Effective Prompt Generation:** The diffusion model-based approach enables the generation of richer and more fine-grained prompts compared to traditional methods.
    *   **Improved Performance:** Experimental results demonstrate significant performance gains on a complex multimodal task, outperforming other state-of-the-art prompt learning techniques.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper includes thorough experiments, ablation studies, and qualitative analysis to validate the effectiveness and robustness of the proposed approach.
    *   **Clear and Well-structured:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the proposed method, experimental setup, and results.
    *   **Mask Supervision:** Guiding the diffusion model using mask supervision is a clever way to inject task-specific information into the prompt generation process.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational Complexity:** Diffusion models are computationally expensive. While the paper attempts to mitigate this by using a latent space and DDIM for accelerated sampling, Diff-Prompt still exhibits higher computational complexity than other prompt learning methods. The paper needs to further clarify and possibly optimize time complexity.
    *   **Dependence on Pre-trained Models:** The method relies on the quality of the pre-trained Mask-VAE and DiT models. Poorly trained models could limit the effectiveness of Diff-Prompt. While some effort is put into detail about model parameters, the paper does not have any analysis on the robustness of the performance given different foundation models
    *   **Limited Task Domain:** Experiments primarily focus on referring expression comprehension. While this is a complex multimodal task, further evaluation on other diverse tasks is needed to assess the generalizability of Diff-Prompt. There is a lack of a task domain with a real life scenario.
    *   **No analysis on edge cases:** The experiments conducted in the paper do not address or discuss the failures or any edge cases where the model could perform poorly.
*   **Potential Influence:** Diff-Prompt has the potential to inspire further research into the use of generative models for prompt engineering. It could lead to the development of more sophisticated prompt generation techniques that can adapt to a wider range of tasks and modalities. The insights gained from this work could also inform the design of more effective pre-trained multimodal models. The integration of a diffusion model for prompt generation is unique and provides an avenue for generating more specialized and accurate prompts.

**Score:** 7.5
**Justification:** The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of prompt learning by leveraging diffusion models for prompt generation. The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed approach on a complex multimodal task. However, the higher computational complexity and limited task domain evaluation slightly temper the overall score. Further research to address these limitations and explore the generalizability of Diff-Prompt across diverse tasks would increase its potential impact. Despite these limitations, the innovative approach and significant performance gains justify a strong score.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### UAV-VLN: End-to-End Vision Language guided Navigation for UAVs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21432v1)
- **Authors**: Pranav Saxena, Nishant Raghuvanshi, Neena Goveas
- **Abstract**: A core challenge in AI-guided autonomy is enabling agents to navigate realistically and effectively in previously unseen environments based on natural language commands. We propose UAV-VLN, a novel end-to-end Vision-Language Navigation (VLN) framework for Unmanned Aerial Vehicles (UAVs) that seamlessly integrates Large Language Models (LLMs) with visual perception to facilitate human-interactive navigation. Our system interprets free-form natural language instructions, grounds them into visual observations, and plans feasible aerial trajectories in diverse environments. UAV-VLN leverages the common-sense reasoning capabilities of LLMs to parse high-level semantic goals, while a vision model detects and localizes semantically relevant objects in the environment. By fusing these modalities, the UAV can reason about spatial relationships, disambiguate references in human instructions, and plan context-aware behaviors with minimal task-specific supervision. To ensure robust and interpretable decision-making, the framework includes a cross-modal grounding mechanism that aligns linguistic intent with visual context. We evaluate UAV-VLN across diverse indoor and outdoor navigation scenarios, demonstrating its ability to generalize to novel instructions and environments with minimal task-specific training. Our results show significant improvements in instruction-following accuracy and trajectory efficiency, highlighting the potential of LLM-driven vision-language interfaces for safe, intuitive, and generalizable UAV autonomy.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "UAV-VLN: End-to-End Vision Language guided Navigation for UAVs":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces UAV-VLN, a novel end-to-end vision-language navigation framework for Unmanned Aerial Vehicles (UAVs). The system integrates Large Language Models (LLMs) with visual perception to enable UAVs to interpret natural language instructions and navigate complex environments. The UAV-VLN framework decomposes high-level instructions into sub-goals, uses an automated task planner to generate low-level control commands, and incorporates a cross-modal grounding mechanism for aligning linguistic intent with visual context. The framework is evaluated across indoor and outdoor navigation scenarios, demonstrating improved instruction-following accuracy and trajectory efficiency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** While the integration of LLMs into robot navigation is not entirely new, the application to UAVs and the explicit consideration of 3D environments, altitude control, and environmental factors introduces a worthwhile nuance. Furthermore, the fine-tuning of a language model for UAV-specific tasks is a practical approach. The curation of a UAV-specific dataset is a valuable contribution in itself. However, the individual components (LLM, vision model, task planner) are all existing elements, and the innovation lies primarily in their integration and adaptation for the aerial robotic domain.

*   **Significance:** UAV-VLN demonstrates significant potential for advancing human-robot interaction and autonomous navigation in real-world settings.  The ability of UAVs to interpret and execute natural language instructions enhances their usability and expands their applications in areas like package delivery, surveillance, and search and rescue. The experiments show promising results, suggesting the approach generalizes well to unseen environments. However, the system still relies on a simulated environment, and its robustness in highly dynamic, unpredictable real-world scenarios is yet to be demonstrated. Further, a more granular, quantitative discussion of the robustness of its safety mechanisms would be relevant.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   End-to-end framework: The complete pipeline, from language input to UAV control, is a well-structured and practical approach.
    *   Integration of LLMs and visual perception: Exploiting the complementary strengths of these modalities leads to enhanced situational awareness and decision-making.
    *   UAV-specific fine-tuning: Adapting the language model to the UAV domain improves accuracy and reliability.
    *   Dataset and evaluation: A new dataset, and careful quantitative metrics offer an important initial benchmark.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Reliance on Simulation: The results are limited to a simulated environment, which may not fully capture the complexities of real-world UAV operation, like complex wind conditions, GPS failures, and more varied lighting.
    *   Component-level Analysis: A stronger evaluation of the individual components of the pipeline (LLM, vision model, planner) would be beneficial.
    *   Lack of Detail on Safety Mechanisms: The specifics of how the safety checks are implemented and their effectiveness are not thoroughly discussed.
    *   Limited comparison to alternatives: The approach would benefit from more comprehensive comparison with existing baseline approaches, particularly existing navigation approaches, specifically for drone use cases.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper can potentially influence the direction of research in UAV autonomy and human-robot interaction, encouraging further exploration of LLM-driven vision-language interfaces for aerial robotic platforms. It may inspire new datasets and benchmark tasks tailored to UAV navigation, and contribute to the development of more robust and generalizable autonomous systems.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper presents a practical and well-integrated framework for vision-language navigation of UAVs. While the individual components are not ground-breaking, the adaptation and integration of LLMs and visual perception for the specific challenges of aerial robotics is a valuable contribution. The paper has some limitations in terms of evaluation in real-world environments and a lack of comparative evaluation with existing navigation frameworks, warranting a lower score.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 6/10

### SeriesBench: A Benchmark for Narrative-Driven Drama Series Understanding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21435v1)
- **Authors**: Chenkai Zhang, Yiming Lei, Zeming Liu, Haitao Leng, ShaoGuo Liu, Tingting Gao, Qingjie Liu, Yunhong Wang
- **Abstract**: With the rapid development of Multi-modal Large Language Models (MLLMs), an increasing number of benchmarks have been established to evaluate the video understanding capabilities of these models. However, these benchmarks focus on \textbf{standalone} videos and mainly assess ``visual elements'' like human actions and object states. In reality, contemporary videos often encompass complex and continuous narratives, typically presented as a \textbf{series}. To address this challenge, we propose \textbf{SeriesBench}, a benchmark consisting of 105 carefully curated narrative-driven series, covering 28 specialized tasks that require deep narrative understanding. Specifically, we first select a diverse set of drama series spanning various genres. Then, we introduce a novel long-span narrative annotation method, combined with a full-information transformation approach to convert manual annotations into diverse task formats. To further enhance model capacity for detailed analysis of plot structures and character relationships within series, we propose a novel narrative reasoning framework, \textbf{PC-DCoT}. Extensive results on \textbf{SeriesBench} indicate that existing MLLMs still face significant challenges in understanding narrative-driven series, while \textbf{PC-DCoT} enables these MLLMs to achieve performance improvements. Overall, our \textbf{SeriesBench} and \textbf{PC-DCoT} highlight the critical necessity of advancing model capabilities to understand narrative-driven series, guiding the future development of MLLMs. SeriesBench is publicly available at https://github.com/zackhxn/SeriesBench-CVPR2025.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "SeriesBench," a new benchmark designed to evaluate the ability of Multi-modal Large Language Models (MLLMs) to understand narrative-driven drama series. Recognizing that existing video understanding benchmarks primarily focus on standalone videos and visual elements, SeriesBench aims to assess the models' comprehension of complex, continuous narratives, character relationships, and plot structures prevalent in series-based content. The benchmark comprises 105 curated drama series (totaling 1072 videos) spanning various genres and 28 specialized tasks across five dimensions (visual, script, audio, augmentation, and comprehension). The authors also introduce a novel long-span narrative annotation method and a reasoning framework called PC-DCoT (Plot & Character Dual Chain of Thought) to enhance MLLMs' performance on these tasks. Experimental results show that existing MLLMs struggle with understanding narrative-driven series and that PC-DCoT can lead to performance improvements.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the benchmark itself. SeriesBench is a significant contribution because it fills a gap in the landscape of video understanding datasets, specifically targeting narrative-driven series. Most existing benchmarks focus on simpler video content or visual elements, while this benchmark tackles the challenges inherent in understanding character development and plot structures within and across multiple episodes. The PC-DCoT is a reasonable extension of the CoT paradigm and tailored to the specifics of the dataset.
*   **Significance:** The paper makes a valid and important observation: the current focus on standalone videos doesn't adequately prepare MLLMs for real-world scenarios where videos often come as series. By creating a dataset specifically for this area and showing that current models don't do very well, this paper makes a solid case for its importance.

**Strengths:**

*   **Dataset Curation:** The paper provides detail on the curation process, emphasizing the selection criteria for drama series (e.g., follower base, engagement metrics). The inclusion of subtitle data and detailed character backgrounds strengthens the dataset.
*   **Comprehensive Task Design:** The five task dimensions and 28 sub-tasks cover a wide range of video understanding abilities, from visual analysis to narrative comprehension.
*   **Innovative Annotation Method:** The introduction of a long-span narrative annotation method, where annotators track events and characters over extended temporal spans, is a valuable contribution.
*   **Reasoning Framework:** The PC-DCoT framework is a logical approach for improving MLLMs' performance on narrative-driven series by explicitly modeling plot event chains and character temporal chains.
*   **Thorough Evaluation:** The extensive experiments on multiple Video-MLLMs provide a robust evaluation of the benchmark and the effectiveness of PC-DCoT.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Annotation Cost and Complexity:** While valuable, the long-span narrative annotation method is likely costly and complex to implement. This might limit the scalability and future expansion of the dataset.
*   **Limited Audio Analysis:** Though included as a dimension, the actual exploitation of audio information appears relatively superficial in the experiments. Further research into truly integrating and analyzing audio elements alongside visual and textual cues would be beneficial.
*   **Limited Generalization of PC-DCoT:** While PC-DCoT improves performance on SeriesBench, the paper doesn't demonstrate its applicability to other datasets or scenarios. It's unclear whether the framework is specific to this dataset or has broader potential.
*   **Dataset Bias:** The focus on content from Kuaishou introduces a platform-specific bias. It would be beneficial to include drama series from different platforms and regions to enhance the benchmark's diversity and representativeness.

**Potential Influence:**

SeriesBench is likely to influence future research in video understanding by shifting the focus towards more complex, narrative-driven content. It will also encourage the development of new methods for modeling long-range dependencies and character relationships in videos. The PC-DCoT framework, while limited in its current scope, could inspire further research into reasoning frameworks tailored to narrative-based video content.

**Score:** 7.5/10

**Justification:**

SeriesBench represents a significant and novel contribution to the field of video understanding. The comprehensive dataset, innovative annotation method, and thoughtful task design make it a valuable resource for researchers. The PC-DCoT framework demonstrates a promising approach for improving MLLMs' performance on narrative-driven series.

However, the weaknesses related to annotation cost, limited audio analysis, lack of PC-DCoT generalization, and potential dataset bias prevent the paper from achieving a higher score. A score of 7.5 reflects the strong positive aspects of the paper while acknowledging its limitations. The paper sets a new research direction by focusing on narrative driven content. Future research, focusing on enhancing generalizability and expanding the types of narrative structures, will greatly expand this direction.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Wasserstein-Aitchison GAN for angular measures of multivariate extremes
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21438v1)
- **Authors**: Stéphane Lhaut, Holger Rootzén, Johan Segers
- **Abstract**: Economically responsible mitigation of multivariate extreme risks -- extreme rainfall in a large area, huge variations of many stock prices, widespread breakdowns in transportation systems -- requires estimates of the probabilities that such risks will materialize in the future. This paper develops a new method, Wasserstein--Aitchison Generative Adversarial Networks (WA-GAN), which provides simulated values of future $d$-dimensional multivariate extreme events and which hence can be used to give estimates of such probabilities. The main hypothesis is that, after transforming the observations to the unit-Pareto scale, their distribution is regularly varying in the sense that the distributions of their radial and angular components (with respect to the $L_1$-norm) converge and become asymptotically independent as the radius gets large. The method is a combination of standard extreme value analysis modeling of the tails of the marginal distributions with nonparametric GAN modeling of the angular distribution. For the latter, the angular values are transformed to Aitchison coordinates in a full $(d-1)$-dimensional linear space, and a Wasserstein GAN is trained on these coordinates and used to generate new values. A reverse transformation is then applied to these values and gives simulated values on the original data scale. The method shows good performance compared to other existing methods in the literature, both in terms of capturing the dependence structure of the extremes in the data, as well as in generating accurate new extremes of the data distribution. The comparison is performed on simulated multivariate extremes from a logistic model in dimensions up to 50 and on a 30-dimensional financial data set.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a new method called Wasserstein-Aitchison Generative Adversarial Networks (WA-GAN) for simulating multivariate extreme events. It addresses the challenge of estimating probabilities of rare events, particularly in high-dimensional settings. WA-GAN combines extreme value theory (EVT) for modeling marginal tails with a Wasserstein GAN for capturing the dependence structure between variables.  Key aspects include: (1) transforming data to a unit-Pareto scale, (2) separating radial and angular components, (3) using Aitchison coordinates to map angular components to a linear space, (4) training a Wasserstein GAN on these coordinates, and (5) transforming the simulated values back to the original scale using multivariate Generalized Pareto (GP) distributions. The method is evaluated on both simulated data with a logistic dependence structure and a real-world financial dataset, demonstrating its performance against existing methods like HTGAN and GPGAN.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The combination of Aitchison coordinates with Wasserstein GANs for modeling the angular measure of multivariate extremes is a novel approach. While each component is established, their integration provides a new way to tackle the challenges of high-dimensional extreme value analysis. The specific use of Aitchison coordinates addresses the simplex constraint inherent in angular measures, potentially improving GAN performance.

*   **Significance:**  The problem of simulating and estimating the probabilities of multivariate extreme events is crucial in risk management, finance, and environmental science. WA-GAN contributes a valuable tool for practitioners and researchers in these fields. The ability to model dependence structures non-parametrically using a GAN, while still incorporating EVT for marginal tail behavior, is a strength. The experimental results suggest the method can outperform existing approaches in certain scenarios, particularly as dimensionality increases. The method tackles the complex issue of estimating extreme values in multivariate scenarios, where data is sparse by definition. The emphasis on generating accurate samples from the angular measure is significant because the asymptotic independence argument is a key assumption of the method. By working in the space of Aitchison coordinates, this becomes more tractable.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-motivated methodology combining parametric (EVT) and non-parametric (GAN) approaches.
    *   The explicit modeling of the angular measure on the simplex using Aitchison coordinates. This facilitates the effective use of a GAN in a high-dimensional simplex setting.
    *   The empirical results show competitive or superior performance compared to HTGAN and GPGAN, especially in higher dimensions.
    *   The paper provides a detailed description of the algorithm and experimental setup.
    *   The application to a real-world financial dataset demonstrates the practical relevance of the method.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Computational cost: GANs are notoriously computationally expensive to train. The paper doesn't explicitly address the computational burden of WA-GAN compared to alternative methods, which could be a limiting factor for some applications. It would have been insightful to include training time information with the results.
    *   Hyperparameter Sensitivity: GANs are sensitive to hyperparameter settings. While the paper describes a random search strategy, it doesn't provide a thorough analysis of how different hyperparameters affect performance or guidelines for optimal parameter selection.
    *   Theoretical guarantees: While the method is empirically evaluated, a more rigorous theoretical justification for the convergence and consistency of WA-GAN would strengthen the paper.  Establishing some theoretical properties, even under simplifying assumptions, could be beneficial.
    *   Limited Scope: The comparison is only done against two recent methods, HTGAN and GPGAN. A broader comparison to more traditional methods from the EVT literature or other recent NN methods could further contextualize the contributions.

*   **Impact and Potential Influence:** WA-GAN has the potential to influence the field of multivariate extreme value analysis by providing a flexible and scalable tool for simulating rare events. It could be particularly useful in applications where accurately modeling dependence structures is crucial. The use of Aitchison coordinates could become a standard technique for handling compositional data in GAN-based models. The paper would be more impactful if the software was readily available.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:** The paper presents a novel and significant method with empirical evidence of good performance. The use of Aitchison coordinates in conjunction with Wasserstein GANs for this specific problem is a valuable contribution. However, the weaknesses related to computational cost, hyperparameter sensitivity, and the lack of rigorous theoretical guarantees limit the overall impact. A more extensive comparison with existing methods and a better discussion about the limitations of the method would also raise its rating. The method is clearly motivated, well-executed, and the results show promise.

- **Classification**: stat.ML
- **Score**: 7/10

### Rethinking Visual Layer Selection in Multimodal LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21447v1)
- **Authors**: Haoran Chen, Junyan Lin, Xinhao Chen, Yue Fan, Xin Jin, Hui Su, Jianfeng Dong, Jinlan Fu, Xiaoyu Shen
- **Abstract**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have achieved impressive performance across a wide range of tasks, typically using CLIP-ViT as their visual encoder due to its strong text-image alignment capabilities. While prior studies suggest that different CLIP-ViT layers capture different types of information, with shallower layers focusing on fine visual details and deeper layers aligning more closely with textual semantics, most MLLMs still select visual features based on empirical heuristics rather than systematic analysis. In this work, we propose a Layer-wise Representation Similarity approach to group CLIP-ViT layers with similar behaviors into {shallow, middle, and deep} categories and assess their impact on MLLM performance. Building on this foundation, we revisit the visual layer selection problem in MLLMs at scale, training LLaVA-style models ranging from 1.4B to 7B parameters. Through extensive experiments across 10 datasets and 4 tasks, we find that: (1) deep layers are essential for OCR tasks; (2) shallow and middle layers substantially outperform deep layers on reasoning tasks involving counting, positioning, and object localization; (3) a lightweight fusion of features across shallow, middle, and deep layers consistently outperforms specialized fusion baselines and single-layer selections, achieving gains on 9 out of 10 datasets. Our work offers the first principled study of visual layer selection in MLLMs, laying the groundwork for deeper investigations into visual representation learning for MLLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Rethinking Visual Layer Selection in Multimodal LLMs" addresses the issue of how visual features are selected from CLIP-ViT encoders in multimodal large language models (MLLMs).  It challenges the common practice of relying on deep layers, particularly the penultimate layer, and proposes a Layer-wise Representation Similarity (LRS) approach to group CLIP-ViT layers into shallow, middle, and deep categories based on the similarity of their hidden states. Through extensive experiments on various datasets and MLLM scales (1.4B to 7B parameters), the paper demonstrates that shallow and middle layers can outperform deep layers on certain tasks like reasoning involving counting, positioning, and object localization. The paper also introduces a lightweight fusion strategy that combines features from all three layer categories, achieving superior performance compared to specialized fusion baselines and single-layer selections.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's main contribution lies in its systematic analysis of visual layer selection in MLLMs, moving beyond empirical heuristics. The LRS approach provides a more principled way to understand the characteristics of different ViT layers and their impact on MLLM performance. While some previous works have explored hierarchical feature fusion, this paper offers a focused and in-depth investigation specifically tailored to MLLMs, along with a LRS analysis, a feature largely missing from prior work. The observation that shallow and middle layers are sometimes better than deep layers for certain tasks is also noteworthy.  However, the fusion strategy itself (simple concatenation with a linear layer) is relatively standard.

* **Significance:** The findings have significant implications for MLLM design and training. The results suggest that MLLMs could benefit from more sophisticated visual feature selection strategies, rather than simply relying on deep layers.  The lightweight fusion approach offers a practical way to improve performance without significantly increasing computational cost. The work could prompt further research into more effective visual representation learning techniques for MLLMs. By systematically analyzing the role of different layers, the study lays the groundwork for more targeted improvements in visual understanding within multimodal models.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Systematic Analysis:** The paper provides a comprehensive and well-organized analysis of visual layer selection.
    * **Extensive Experiments:** The experiments cover a wide range of datasets and MLLM scales, increasing the reliability of the results.
    * **Clear Findings:** The key findings are clearly presented and supported by evidence.
    * **Practical Implications:** The paper proposes a simple yet effective fusion strategy that can be readily implemented.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Fusion Strategy Simplicity:** While effective, the proposed fusion strategy is relatively basic. More advanced fusion techniques could potentially lead to further performance improvements.
    * **Limited Exploration of Fusion Methods:** The paper primarily focuses on the benefits of different layers and only includes a simple fusion. It should have included more baseline fusion methods in the experiment.
    * **Lack of in-depth analysis:** The paper should have provided more in-depth explanations of the reasons behind their findings. For example, the authors should have provided explanations for why shallow layers work well with particular tasks and when/why middle layers' performances are better.
    * **Incremental Contribution:** While the paper addresses an important problem, its contribution to the field is rather incremental. It does not propose a novel technique, architecture or method that is completely novel in design. Instead, it improves the current design and method by a little.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research by highlighting the importance of careful visual layer selection and prompting further exploration of more sophisticated visual representation learning techniques for MLLMs. The systematic analysis and clear findings could serve as a valuable resource for researchers and practitioners in the field.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper offers a valuable, methodical examination of a crucial issue in MLLMs: visual layer selection. It challenges the prevailing reliance on deep layers and furnishes compelling evidence of the utility of shallow and middle layers for particular tasks. Nevertheless, the fusion strategy's inherent simplicity and the somewhat incremental enhancement to the current design, prevent the paper from attaining a higher score.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### GarmentDiffusion: 3D Garment Sewing Pattern Generation with Multimodal Diffusion Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21476v1)
- **Authors**: Xinyu Li, Qi Yao, Yuanda Wang
- **Abstract**: Garment sewing patterns are fundamental design elements that bridge the gap between design concepts and practical manufacturing. The generative modeling of sewing patterns is crucial for creating diversified garments. However, existing approaches are limited either by reliance on a single input modality or by suboptimal generation efficiency. In this work, we present \textbf{\textit{GarmentDiffusion}}, a new generative model capable of producing centimeter-precise, vectorized 3D sewing patterns from multimodal inputs (text, image, and incomplete sewing pattern). Our method efficiently encodes 3D sewing pattern parameters into compact edge token representations, achieving a sequence length that is $\textbf{10}\times$ shorter than that of the autoregressive SewingGPT in DressCode. By employing a diffusion transformer, we simultaneously denoise all edge tokens along the temporal axis, while maintaining a constant number of denoising steps regardless of dataset-specific edge and panel statistics. With all combination of designs of our model, the sewing pattern generation speed is accelerated by $\textbf{100}\times$ compared to SewingGPT. We achieve new state-of-the-art results on DressCodeData, as well as on the largest sewing pattern dataset, namely GarmentCodeData. The project website is available at https://shenfu-research.github.io/Garment-Diffusion/.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the GarmentDiffusion paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GarmentDiffusion, a generative model for creating 3D garment sewing patterns from multimodal inputs (text, image, incomplete patterns). It addresses limitations in previous approaches, like reliance on single modalities and inefficient generation. The key innovations include: 1) an edge-oriented encoding scheme that dramatically reduces the token sequence length compared to autoregressive methods, enabling faster processing; 2) a diffusion transformer that denoises all edge tokens in parallel, maintaining consistent denoising steps; and 3) a new multimodal data annotation pipeline to generate richer text descriptions and garment sketches.  The model achieves state-of-the-art results on DressCodeData and GarmentCodeData.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces several novel components:

    *   **Efficient Edge Encoding:** The edge-based representation for sewing patterns is a significant improvement over coordinate-based or vector-quantized approaches.  Reducing the sequence length is a practical contribution, allowing for larger datasets and faster training/inference.
    *   **Diffusion Transformer for Sewing Patterns:**  Applying diffusion transformers to this specific task, with the adapted conditional injection (cross-attention), is a novel contribution. The parallel denoising is a major efficiency gain.
    *   **Multimodal Data Annotation Pipeline:**  While LLMs and image generation tools are increasingly common, the specific pipeline designed to generate multi-level text descriptions and garment sketches tailored for sewing patterns is valuable and contributes to the data availability and quality in this niche field.
*   **Significance:**

    *   **State-of-the-Art Results:** Achieving state-of-the-art results on multiple datasets, including the largest one (GarmentCodeData), demonstrates the effectiveness of the proposed approach. The 100x speedup over SewingGPT is a practically important result.
    *   **Addressing a Real-World Problem:** Garment design and manufacturing are complex, and automated pattern generation has the potential to significantly improve efficiency and reduce waste.  This paper contributes to this goal by providing a more efficient and versatile generative model.
    *   **Multimodal Input Support:** The ability to condition the generation on text, images, and incomplete patterns makes the model more flexible and user-friendly, which can impact its practical usability.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper provides extensive quantitative evaluations, comparing the proposed model against state-of-the-art methods on multiple datasets. Ablation studies further validate the effectiveness of individual components.
    *   **Clear Problem Definition and Solution:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of previous approaches and proposes a well-defined solution with clear technical details.
    *   **Practical Impact:** The speed improvements and multimodal capabilities significantly enhance the practical applicability of the model in a real-world garment design workflow.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependence on Existing Models (CLIP, LLMs):**  While the integration is well-done, the model relies on pre-trained CLIP and LLMs for feature extraction and data annotation. This means that performance is partly dependent on the capabilities and limitations of these external models.
    *   **Limited Discussion of Edge Cases/Failures:**  The paper focuses on the strengths of the approach but could benefit from a more detailed discussion of failure cases or situations where the model struggles. While figure 6 does indicate failures, more detailed explanations could be added.
    *   **Room for improvement with stiching information:** Lack of stiching information may create limitations in the garment stimulation.
*   **Potential Influence:** This paper is likely to influence future research in garment design and generation.  The efficient edge encoding scheme and the successful application of diffusion transformers provide a strong foundation for further work.  The multimodal data annotation pipeline can also be adopted and adapted by other researchers in the field.

**Justification for Score:**

I'm assigning a score of **8** to this paper.  The paper demonstrates significant novelty by introducing a novel approach to 3D garment sewing pattern generation that improves efficiency and versatility. The empirical results clearly show that the proposed method achieves state-of-the-art performance on multiple datasets. These strengths are slightly tempered by the reliance on external pre-trained models and the lack of a deeper analysis of potential limitations. Overall, the paper contributes meaningfully to the field and is likely to stimulate further research and development in AI-driven fashion technology.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### DGSolver: Diffusion Generalist Solver with Universal Posterior Sampling for Image Restoration
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21487v1)
- **Authors**: Hebaixu Wang, Jing Zhang, Haonan Guo, Di Wang, Jiayi Ma, Bo Du
- **Abstract**: Diffusion models have achieved remarkable progress in universal image restoration. While existing methods speed up inference by reducing sampling steps, substantial step intervals often introduce cumulative errors. Moreover, they struggle to balance the commonality of degradation representations and restoration quality. To address these challenges, we introduce \textbf{DGSolver}, a diffusion generalist solver with universal posterior sampling. We first derive the exact ordinary differential equations for generalist diffusion models and tailor high-order solvers with a queue-based accelerated sampling strategy to improve both accuracy and efficiency. We then integrate universal posterior sampling to better approximate manifold-constrained gradients, yielding a more accurate noise estimation and correcting errors in inverse inference. Extensive experiments show that DGSolver outperforms state-of-the-art methods in restoration accuracy, stability, and scalability, both qualitatively and quantitatively. Code and models will be available at https://github.com/MiliLab/DGSolver.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DGSolver, a new diffusion-based image restoration framework designed to address the challenges of universal image restoration, where a single model handles various degradation types. DGSolver aims to improve both accuracy and efficiency compared to existing methods. It achieves this through two main innovations:

1.  **High-Order Generalist Solvers:** The paper re-formulates the diffusion process as a semi-linear Ordinary Differential Equation (ODE) and then develops customized high-order ODE solvers with a queue-based accelerated sampling strategy.  This reduces accumulated discretization errors during the reverse diffusion process and speeds up inference.

2.  **Universal Posterior Sampling:** The method integrates Bayesian posterior sampling to provide manifold-constrained gradient guidance for noise estimation. This serves as a form of training-free accuracy compensation, particularly beneficial when dealing with the commonality of degradation representations.

The paper validates DGSolver through extensive experiments on various image restoration tasks, including deraining, low-light enhancement, desnowing, dehazing, and deblurring, demonstrating superior performance compared to state-of-the-art methods in terms of accuracy, stability, and scalability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates a solid blend of theoretical and practical novelty. The reformulation of the generalist diffusion process into an ODE and the subsequent development of tailored high-order solvers is a valuable contribution.  Furthermore, the integration of universal posterior sampling as a training-free mechanism for improving noise estimation is also a novel aspect. The combination of these elements, particularly in the context of a generalist image restoration model, is reasonably new.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper is substantial. Universal image restoration is a crucial problem with wide-ranging applications. DGSolver offers a compelling approach to address the inherent challenges of balancing commonality and restoration quality in generalist models. The reported experimental results demonstrate a clear advantage over existing state-of-the-art methods, particularly in real-world scenarios and remote sensing image restoration, showcasing potential for practical adoption. The training-free aspect of the posterior sampling adds further value, reducing the burden of extensive retraining that plagues many existing approaches.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Strong theoretical foundation:**  The ODE reformulation and subsequent solver customization are well-motivated and theoretically sound.
    *   **Effective combination of techniques:**  The synergy between high-order solvers and universal posterior sampling is a key strength, addressing both discretization errors and the limitations of purely data-driven noise estimation.
    *   **Extensive and diverse experiments:** The paper presents a broad range of experiments across different tasks, datasets, and application domains (natural images and remote sensing), supporting the claims of accuracy, stability, and scalability.
    *   **Training-free refinement:** The universal posterior sampling doesn't require additional training, making it a practical and readily deployable technique.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Computational Overhead:** The high-order solvers, while theoretically sound, introduce computational overhead due to the total derivatives, as highlighted in the paper. The queue-based accelerated sampling strategy partially mitigates this, but the trade-off between accuracy and efficiency needs careful consideration. The overhead should be more explicitly compared to competing methods which employ other acceleration strategies.
    *   **Parameter sensitivity:**  While the method is training-free, the sensitivity of the performance to the parameter δτ (which controls the commonality of degradation representations) is a potential concern.  The need to potentially tune this parameter across different tasks could limit the truly "universal" nature of the model. The choice of 8 timesteps seems somewhat arbitrary and requires further validation.
    *   **Limitations of posterior sampling approximation**: The approximate nature of the posterior sampling method warrants caution, particularly in scenarios with complex and highly varied degradation scenarios. Even though the upper bound is given, a better explanation is warranted on when the bound can become loose in real applications.
    *   **Clarity of writing**: The technical descriptions, while detailed, can be dense and challenging to follow. Improving the clarity of the writing would significantly enhance the paper's accessibility.

*   **Potential Influence:** DGSolver has the potential to significantly influence the field of image restoration. Its novel approach to addressing the challenges of generalist models, combined with its strong experimental results, makes it a valuable contribution. It can serve as a foundation for future research in developing more efficient, accurate, and versatile image restoration frameworks. The training-free aspect is particularly appealing and could lead to wider adoption.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of universal image restoration.
