### HoloTime: Taming Video Diffusion Models for Panoramic 4D Scene Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21650v1)
- **Authors**: Haiyang Zhou, Wangbo Yu, Jiawen Guan, Xinhua Cheng, Yonghong Tian, Li Yuan
- **Abstract**: The rapid advancement of diffusion models holds the promise of revolutionizing the application of VR and AR technologies, which typically require scene-level 4D assets for user experience. Nonetheless, existing diffusion models predominantly concentrate on modeling static 3D scenes or object-level dynamics, constraining their capacity to provide truly immersive experiences. To address this issue, we propose HoloTime, a framework that integrates video diffusion models to generate panoramic videos from a single prompt or reference image, along with a 360-degree 4D scene reconstruction method that seamlessly transforms the generated panoramic video into 4D assets, enabling a fully immersive 4D experience for users. Specifically, to tame video diffusion models for generating high-fidelity panoramic videos, we introduce the 360World dataset, the first comprehensive collection of panoramic videos suitable for downstream 4D scene reconstruction tasks. With this curated dataset, we propose Panoramic Animator, a two-stage image-to-video diffusion model that can convert panoramic images into high-quality panoramic videos. Following this, we present Panoramic Space-Time Reconstruction, which leverages a space-time depth estimation method to transform the generated panoramic videos into 4D point clouds, enabling the optimization of a holistic 4D Gaussian Splatting representation to reconstruct spatially and temporally consistent 4D scenes. To validate the efficacy of our method, we conducted a comparative analysis with existing approaches, revealing its superiority in both panoramic video generation and 4D scene reconstruction. This demonstrates our method's capability to create more engaging and realistic immersive environments, thereby enhancing user experiences in VR and AR applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "HoloTime: Taming Video Diffusion Models for Panoramic 4D Scene Generation" presents a framework for generating immersive 360-degree 4D scenes from a single prompt or reference panoramic image.  It addresses the lack of existing methods for generating dynamic, panoramic 4D environments. The framework includes three main components: (1) a curated dataset called "360World" containing fixed-camera panoramic videos, (2) "Panoramic Animator," a two-stage image-to-video diffusion model to convert panoramic images into high-quality panoramic videos, and (3) "Panoramic Space-Time Reconstruction," a method to transform these panoramic videos into 4D point clouds using space-time depth estimation, enabling a 4D Gaussian Splatting representation. The authors demonstrate their method's superiority through comparisons with existing approaches.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the integration of several components into a cohesive framework for generating dynamic panoramic 4D scenes, rather than a breakthrough in any single component.
    *   *Dataset:* The 360World dataset is a useful contribution given the lack of publicly available fixed-camera panoramic video data. This in itself holds decent novelty.
    *   *Panoramic Animator:* The two-stage motion-guided generation approach attempts to address the specific challenges of panoramic video generation, but the core architecture still relies on existing diffusion models (DynamiCrafter). While the hybrid fine-tuning is a sensible strategy, its novelty is limited to the specific application rather than a fundamental change in diffusion modeling.
    *   *Panoramic Space-Time Reconstruction:* While the approach employs panoramic optical flow estimation and space-time depth estimation for depth consistency, it builds upon existing depth estimation and 4D reconstruction techniques.

*   **Significance:**
    *   *Immersive VR/AR:* The ability to create engaging VR/AR experiences using generated panoramic dynamic scenes is potentially impactful. The work directly targets a need in this field for more realistic and engaging environments.
    *   *Dataset Contribution:* The 360World dataset will likely be beneficial to other researchers working on related problems.
    *   *Incremental Advance:* However, the significance is somewhat limited by the fact that the gains are incremental. The improvements are achieved by careful integration and adaptation of existing techniques rather than disruptive innovation.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Complete Framework: The paper provides a complete pipeline from image input to a reconstructed 4D scene.
    *   Empirical Validation:  The paper presents quantitative and qualitative results, including user studies, to demonstrate the effectiveness of their approach.
    *   Well-Written: The paper is generally well-written and explains the approach in a clear and understandable manner.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Dependency on Existing Models:  The framework is heavily reliant on existing diffusion models and depth estimation techniques, limiting its potential for groundbreaking innovation.  The two-stage motion-guided refinement also relies on a super-resolution model, potentially introducing artifacts.
    *   Limited Scope: The focus on fixed-camera panoramic videos might restrict the generalizability of the approach to other types of 4D scene generation.
    *   Qualitative Evaluation: In Figure 4, there is a lack of diversity. The same type of motion (e.g., people moving) repeats across all cases. Showing a range of motions, objects, and scenes would lend more weight to their claim of generating complex and diverse motions.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence the development of more accessible and automated tools for generating VR/AR content. The 360World dataset might serve as a benchmark for evaluating future 4D scene generation methods.

**Justification:** The paper presents a functional and well-engineered system for generating panoramic 4D scenes, primarily through a combination of existing video diffusion and 3D reconstruction techniques. The incremental improvements and the construction of a specialized dataset warrant a moderate evaluation score, while the absence of a major, game-changing conceptual advance limits its overall impact. Given these considerations, I consider the integration of components as a significant aspect, and the value of the dataset.

### AdaR1: From Long-CoT to Hybrid-CoT via Bi-Level Adaptive Reasoning Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21659v1)
- **Authors**: Haotian Luo, Haiying He, Yibo Wang, Jinluan Yang, Rui Liu, Naiqiang Tan, Xiaochun Cao, Dacheng Tao, Li Shen
- **Abstract**: Recently, long-thought reasoning models achieve strong performance on complex reasoning tasks, but often incur substantial inference overhead, making efficiency a critical concern. Our empirical analysis reveals that the benefit of using Long-CoT varies across problems: while some problems require elaborate reasoning, others show no improvement, or even degraded accuracy. This motivates adaptive reasoning strategies that tailor reasoning depth to the input. However, prior work primarily reduces redundancy within long reasoning paths, limiting exploration of more efficient strategies beyond the Long-CoT paradigm. To address this, we propose a novel two-stage framework for adaptive and efficient reasoning. First, we construct a hybrid reasoning model by merging long and short CoT models to enable diverse reasoning styles. Second, we apply bi-level preference training to guide the model to select suitable reasoning styles (group-level), and prefer concise and correct reasoning within each style group (instance-level). Experiments demonstrate that our method significantly reduces inference costs compared to other baseline approaches, while maintaining performance. Notably, on five mathematical datasets, the average length of reasoning is reduced by more than 50%, highlighting the potential of adaptive strategies to optimize reasoning efficiency in large language models. Our code is coming soon at https://github.com/StarDewXXX/AdaR1
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AdaR1, a novel two-stage framework designed to improve the efficiency of reasoning in large language models (LLMs) without sacrificing accuracy. The first stage involves creating a "hybrid reasoning model" by merging a Long-CoT (Chain-of-Thought) model with a Short-CoT model. This equips the model with the ability to generate both detailed and concise reasoning paths. The second stage employs a "Bi-Level Adaptive Reasoning Optimization" strategy. This involves training the model to (1) select the appropriate reasoning style (long or short CoT) based on the input problem (group-level preference) and (2) further refine the reasoning within the chosen style to be as concise as possible while maintaining correctness (instance-level preference). Experiments on mathematical datasets demonstrate that AdaR1 significantly reduces inference costs (reasoning length) while maintaining strong performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a reasonably novel approach to the problem of reasoning efficiency in LLMs. The combination of model merging and bi-level preference training is a unique contribution. Unlike many existing approaches that focus solely on optimizing within the Long-CoT paradigm (e.g., pruning or compressing reasoning paths), AdaR1 takes a step back and allows the model to choose between different reasoning styles. The bi-level optimization strategy, with its group-level and instance-level components, provides a structured way to achieve adaptive reasoning.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial challenge in deploying powerful LLMs: computational efficiency. The high cost of inference with Long-CoT reasoning is a significant bottleneck, particularly for real-world applications. AdaR1 offers a promising solution by enabling adaptive reasoning, potentially making LLMs more practical and accessible for resource-constrained environments. The results showing substantial reductions in reasoning length without significant accuracy loss are compelling. The paper also includes useful empirical analysis regarding when Long-CoT is actually beneficial and how model merging can be effectively used, but potentially at the cost of performance.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the problem of inefficient reasoning in LLMs and motivates the need for adaptive strategies.
    *   **Novel Approach:** The combination of model merging and bi-level preference training is a unique contribution.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The experiments on several mathematical datasets provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of AdaR1. The ablation studies effectively demonstrate the contribution of each component of the framework.
    *   **Comprehensive Analysis:** The paper provides an in-depth analysis of the model's behavior, including the "Thinking Ratio" study and adaptive reasoning analysis across different difficulty levels.
    *   Well-organized and clearly written.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope:** While the paper focuses on mathematical datasets, it would be valuable to see how AdaR1 performs on other types of reasoning tasks, such as commonsense reasoning or natural language inference. The analysis is limited to mathematical datasets. The generalizability of the approach should be discussed further.
    *   **Merging Implementation Details:** The linear merging approach is a simple implementation, while it works in practice, this could create some limitations as to how different the merged models can be. The paper does not extensively discuss the limitations of using a linear merge compared to other merge methods.
    *   **Dependence on Short CoT Data Generation:** The short-CoT data used in the initial fine-tuning stage is generated using a distinct model. The impact of generating short-CoT dataset should be further studied.

*   **Potential Influence:** The AdaR1 framework has the potential to influence the field by promoting adaptive reasoning strategies for LLMs. The idea of combining different reasoning styles and training models to intelligently select the appropriate style could inspire further research in this area. The paper's empirical findings on the benefits of Long-CoT reasoning could also inform the design of future reasoning models.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

The paper addresses an important problem (reasoning efficiency), introduces a novel approach (AdaR1), and provides strong empirical evidence to support its claims. However, the scope of the experiments is somewhat limited, and the potential for further improvements in the model merging process and the diversity and performance of the short-CoT datasets are limitations. Thus, the contribution is solid and impactful, but some aspects limit the influence it might have in the field.

### From Precision to Perception: User-Centred Evaluation of Keyword Extraction Algorithms for Internet-Scale Contextual Advertising
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21667v1)
- **Authors**: Jingwen Cai, Sara Leckner, Johanna Björklund
- **Abstract**: Keyword extraction is a foundational task in natural language processing, underpinning countless real-world applications. A salient example is contextual advertising, where keywords help predict the topical congruence between ads and their surrounding media contexts to enhance advertising effectiveness. Recent advances in artificial intelligence, particularly large language models, have improved keyword extraction capabilities but also introduced concerns about computational cost. Moreover, although the end-user experience is of vital importance, human evaluation of keyword extraction performances remains under-explored. This study provides a comparative evaluation of three prevalent keyword extraction algorithms that vary in complexity: TF-IDF, KeyBERT, and Llama 2. To evaluate their effectiveness, a mixed-methods approach is employed, combining quantitative benchmarking with qualitative assessments from 552 participants through three survey-based experiments. Findings indicate a slight user preference for KeyBERT, which offers a favourable balance between performance and computational efficiency compared to the other two algorithms. Despite a strong overall preference for gold-standard keywords, differences between the algorithmic outputs are not statistically significant, highlighting a long-overlooked gap between traditional precision-focused metrics and user-perceived algorithm efficiency. The study highlights the importance of user-centred evaluation methodologies and proposes analytical tools to support their implementation.
- **Summary**: Here is a concise summary and a critical evaluation of the research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a user-centered evaluation framework for comparing keyword extraction algorithms in the context of online contextual advertising. It compares three algorithms (TF-IDF, KeyBERT, and Llama 2) using both precision-based metrics and qualitative user feedback obtained through survey experiments involving a large participant pool (552). The study identifies factors to consider during user-centered evaluation, assesses the performance of the algorithms based on precision and user perception, and discusses the implications for contextual advertising. The results suggest a slight user preference for KeyBERT due to its balance of performance and computational efficiency, highlighting a gap between traditional precision-focused metrics and user-perceived effectiveness. It emphasizes the importance of incorporating human-in-the-loop evaluation methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution by focusing on the user perception of keyword extraction algorithms, an area that has been relatively under-explored compared to precision-focused metrics. It challenges the assumption that higher precision automatically translates to better user satisfaction or effectiveness in real-world applications like contextual advertising.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The use of a mixed-methods approach (quantitative and qualitative) provides a comprehensive understanding of the algorithms' performance.
    *   The large participant pool (552) strengthens the reliability of the user feedback.
    *   The paper addresses a practical problem (contextual advertising) and provides insights into how keyword extraction can be optimized for better user experiences.
    *   The thorough discussion of properties of good keywords and the novel "horizontal" and "vertical" analysis provides a good method and approach that could be used by other researchers in the field.
    *   The inclusion of the Llama 2 analysis puts it in the context of large language models.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The study is limited to a specific domain (online news articles) and a specific set of algorithms. The findings may not be generalizable to other domains or keyword extraction techniques.
    *   The definition of "distinctiveness" as a target property may be confusing for participants and introduce variability in ratings.
    *   The study does not explore the impact of the extracted keywords on actual ad performance metrics (e.g., click-through rates, conversion rates).
    *   The experiments all utilize one model for KeyBert and LLama 2, potentially limiting the understanding of fine-tuning or using different models (either larger or more specific).

*   **Novelty:** The paper offers novel insights by connecting user perceptions to algorithmic output in the context of keyword extraction. It pushes the field towards more human-centric evaluation methodologies. The "horizontal" and "vertical" analysis of the feedback also creates a novel way of looking at the data.

*   **Significance:** The paper is significant because it shows there's a gap between how *we* measure the success of the system and how the *users* do. Furthermore, as the algorithms become more computationally expensive, it is important to ensure the algorithms are *significantly* better than their predecessors. It will likely influence researchers and practitioners to consider user feedback in the design and evaluation of keyword extraction systems, especially for applications where user experience is a critical factor. The suggestion of KeyBERT strikes a balance between the user feedback and the computationally complexity shows how future researchers could focus on these trade-offs.

**Justification for Score:**

Despite the limitations, the paper's focus on user perception, its practical relevance, the mixed-methods approach, and the novel analytical methods make it a meaningful contribution to the field. It challenges the existing focus on purely algorithmic precision and offers a more nuanced perspective on how to evaluate keyword extraction systems. However, the limitations regarding generalizability and the lack of direct ad performance metrics limit its overall impact, and there are existing works that have previously considered user feedback. It provides good suggestions and suggestions on trade-offs and limitations.

### Traceback of Poisoning Attacks to Retrieval-Augmented Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21668v1)
- **Authors**: Baolei Zhang, Haoran Xin, Minghong Fang, Zhuqing Liu, Biao Yi, Tong Li, Zheli Liu
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) integrated with retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) systems improve accuracy by leveraging external knowledge sources. However, recent research has revealed RAG's susceptibility to poisoning attacks, where the attacker injects poisoned texts into the knowledge database, leading to attacker-desired responses. Existing defenses, which predominantly focus on inference-time mitigation, have proven insufficient against sophisticated attacks. In this paper, we introduce RAGForensics, the first traceback system for RAG, designed to identify poisoned texts within the knowledge database that are responsible for the attacks. RAGForensics operates iteratively, first retrieving a subset of texts from the database and then utilizing a specially crafted prompt to guide an LLM in detecting potential poisoning texts. Empirical evaluations across multiple datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of RAGForensics against state-of-the-art poisoning attacks. This work pioneers the traceback of poisoned texts in RAG systems, providing a practical and promising defense mechanism to enhance their security.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RAGForensics, a novel traceback system for identifying poisoned texts in Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems, a key area of concern for LLM security.  It addresses the vulnerability of RAG systems to poisoning attacks, where malicious actors inject crafted texts into the knowledge database to manipulate the LLM's responses.  Unlike existing defenses that focus on inference-time mitigation, RAGForensics aims to identify and remove the root cause: the poisoned data itself.  The system operates iteratively, using the RAG retriever to identify potential poisoned texts, and then employing a specialized prompt and LLM to classify texts.  The identified poisoned texts are then removed, improving the RAG system's resilience.  The authors provide empirical evaluations demonstrating the effectiveness of RAGForensics against several poisoning attacks and datasets, including adaptive attacks designed to evade the system.  The paper also explores how to differentiate between poisoned feedback and naturally occurring errors in LLMs and proposes a benign text enhancement strategy to improve accuracy when faced with such errors.

**Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary strength is its novelty.  It is the first work that systematically addresses the problem of tracing back poisoning attacks within RAG systems to their origins in the knowledge database. Existing work focuses on mitigation *during* inference. This is a distinct and valuable contribution as it allows for proactive rather than reactive defense.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the work is substantial. As RAG systems become increasingly prevalent, securing them against data poisoning is critical. By enabling the identification and removal of poisoned data, the proposed system helps improve the trustworthiness and reliability of LLM-based applications.

*   **Technical Soundness:**  The proposed approach, RAGForensics, is technically sound and well-motivated. The iterative retrieval and identification process, combined with the use of a structured prompt and LLM, forms a practical and effective framework.  The design choices are justified, and the algorithm is clearly explained.

*   **Experimental Evaluation:** The paper includes a comprehensive experimental evaluation.  The experiments cover multiple datasets, poisoning attacks, and adaptive attack strategies designed to circumvent RAGForensics.   This breadth strengthens the credibility of the findings. However, it should be noted that although the system maintains strong performance across LLMs and datasets, the complexity of training these large models makes them difficult to rigorously validate.

*   **Adaptive Attacks & Robustness:** The inclusion of adaptive attacks is a key strength, demonstrating an understanding of how adversaries might attempt to bypass the system. The fact that RAGForensics maintains robust performance against these attacks speaks to the effectiveness of the design.

*   **Limitations & Future Directions:** The discussion of limitations is helpful. While RAGForensics effectively handles targeted poisoning attacks, its performance on untargeted poisoning scenarios needs further investigation. This acknowledgment adds credibility to the work and motivates future research.

*   **Clarity and Presentation:** The paper is generally well-written and clearly presented. The problem is well-defined, the proposed solution is explained in detail, and the experimental results are thoroughly discussed.

*   **Potential Impact:**  The research offers a practical defense mechanism for enhancing the security of RAG systems. The identification of malicious sources may inform future directions to improve systems and reduce vulnerabilities.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **LLM Reliance:** The reliance on LLMs for identifying poisoned texts introduces a degree of uncertainty. The performance of RAGForensics is inherently dependent on the capabilities and biases of the LLM used for classification, which although well explained may be a weakness.
*   **Computational Cost:**  The iterative nature of RAGForensics and the use of LLMs for classification might incur significant computational costs, particularly for very large knowledge databases. Addressing the efficiency of the system would be an interesting avenue for future work.
*  The threat model assumes full knowledge of parameters of both the retriever and LLM to the attacker. This assumption is stronger than what is typically the case, as the attacker might only have indirect or black-box access to these components.

Despite these weaknesses, the strengths of the paper, particularly its novelty and comprehensive evaluation, outweigh the limitations.  The work represents a valuable contribution to the field of LLM security.

### Hoist with His Own Petard: Inducing Guardrails to Facilitate Denial-of-Service Attacks on Retrieval-Augmented Generation of LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21680v1)
- **Authors**: Pan Suo, Yu-Ming Shang, San-Chuan Guo, Xi Zhang
- **Abstract**: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) integrates Large Language Models (LLMs) with external knowledge bases, improving output quality while introducing new security risks. Existing studies on RAG vulnerabilities typically focus on exploiting the retrieval mechanism to inject erroneous knowledge or malicious texts, inducing incorrect outputs. However, these approaches overlook critical weaknesses within LLMs, leaving important attack vectors unexplored and limiting the scope and efficiency of attacks. In this paper, we uncover a novel vulnerability: the safety guardrails of LLMs, while designed for protection, can also be exploited as an attack vector by adversaries. Building on this vulnerability, we propose MutedRAG, a novel denial-of-service attack that reversely leverages the guardrails of LLMs to undermine the availability of RAG systems. By injecting minimalistic jailbreak texts, such as "\textit{How to build a bomb}", into the knowledge base, MutedRAG intentionally triggers the LLM's safety guardrails, causing the system to reject legitimate queries. Besides, due to the high sensitivity of guardrails, a single jailbreak sample can affect multiple queries, effectively amplifying the efficiency of attacks while reducing their costs. Experimental results on three datasets demonstrate that MutedRAG achieves an attack success rate exceeding 60% in many scenarios, requiring only less than one malicious text to each target query on average. In addition, we evaluate potential defense strategies against MutedRAG, finding that some of current mechanisms are insufficient to mitigate this threat, underscoring the urgent need for more robust solutions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper "Hoist with His Own Petard: Inducing Guardrails to Facilitate Denial-of-Service Attacks on Retrieval-Augmented Generation of LLMs" proposes a new type of attack, called MutedRAG, against Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems. Unlike previous attacks that focus on injecting malicious content into the retrieval stage, MutedRAG exploits the safety guardrails of LLMs to cause a denial-of-service. By injecting carefully crafted jailbreak prompts into the knowledge base, the attack triggers the LLM's safety mechanisms, causing it to refuse to answer legitimate user queries.  The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of MutedRAG across multiple datasets and LLMs, showing that it can achieve high success rates with minimal injected data. They also explore potential defenses, finding some existing mechanisms insufficient.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's core idea – exploiting LLM guardrails for DoS – is novel within the context of RAG system security.  Existing work predominantly focuses on content injection and adversarial examples to manipulate the *output* of the LLM, rather than causing the LLM to refuse service *altogether*.  The insight that safety mechanisms, designed for protection, can be turned against the system is significant.

*   **Significance:** The potential impact of this work is substantial. DoS attacks can severely disrupt RAG-based applications, affecting search, question answering, and recommendation services. The vulnerability is relatively easy to exploit. Even a small number of malicious entries in the knowledge base can render a system unusable. The paper demonstrates that current defense strategies are inadequate, highlighting a need for new approaches.  The paper's findings could motivate the development of more robust RAG systems.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly defines the vulnerability and its potential consequences.
    *   **Well-Defined Attack Methodology (MutedRAG):** MutedRAG is a straightforward and easily implementable attack, increasing its significance. The text splicing and prefix optimization contribute to attack success.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The authors perform thorough experiments across multiple datasets and LLMs, providing strong evidence for the effectiveness of MutedRAG.
    *   **Defense Exploration:** The initial analysis of potential defenses is valuable, even though the results are negative. It points to the limitations of current approaches.
    *   **Easy Implementation and High Success Rate:** Demonstrating a high attack success rate with minimal investment in malicious data (small injection percentage) makes this a serious practical concern.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Simplicity of Jailbreak Prompts:** The paper relies on relatively basic jailbreak techniques. Future work could explore more sophisticated adversarial prompts to further amplify the attack's effectiveness.
    *   **Limited Defense Analysis:** The defense exploration is somewhat superficial.  A more in-depth analysis of mitigation strategies, including adaptive defenses and detection mechanisms, would strengthen the paper.
    *   **Scope of Transferability:** The paper demonstrates transferability across different LLMs, but further investigation is needed to understand how MutedRAG performs across different RAG architectures and knowledge base types.
    *   **Limited Realistic Scenarios:** While the authors tested across datasets and LLMs, it would have been ideal to include some realistic user-generated data and real world situations.
    *   **Over reliance on LLM APIs:** The paper uses various APIs to optimize its models, but this is problematic from a security perspective as they are vulnerable to policy changes and vendor practices.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in RAG system security by shifting the focus from content manipulation to denial-of-service vulnerabilities. It could also stimulate the development of more robust defense mechanisms and security-aware RAG architectures.

**Justification for Score**

The paper presents a novel and significant vulnerability in RAG systems. The attack, MutedRAG, is simple to implement, highly effective, and difficult to defend against with existing techniques. While there are some limitations in the depth of the defense analysis and the sophistication of the jailbreak prompts, the paper's core contribution – highlighting a previously overlooked attack vector – is valuable and timely.

### Visual Text Processing: A Comprehensive Review and Unified Evaluation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21682v1)
- **Authors**: Yan Shu, Weichao Zeng, Fangmin Zhao, Zeyu Chen, Zhenhang Li, Xiaomeng Yang, Yu Zhou, Paolo Rota, Xiang Bai, Lianwen Jin, Xu-Cheng Yin, Nicu Sebe
- **Abstract**: Visual text is a crucial component in both document and scene images, conveying rich semantic information and attracting significant attention in the computer vision community. Beyond traditional tasks such as text detection and recognition, visual text processing has witnessed rapid advancements driven by the emergence of foundation models, including text image reconstruction and text image manipulation. Despite significant progress, challenges remain due to the unique properties that differentiate text from general objects. Effectively capturing and leveraging these distinct textual characteristics is essential for developing robust visual text processing models. In this survey, we present a comprehensive, multi-perspective analysis of recent advancements in visual text processing, focusing on two key questions: (1) What textual features are most suitable for different visual text processing tasks? (2) How can these distinctive text features be effectively incorporated into processing frameworks? Furthermore, we introduce VTPBench, a new benchmark that encompasses a broad range of visual text processing datasets. Leveraging the advanced visual quality assessment capabilities of multimodal large language models (MLLMs), we propose VTPScore, a novel evaluation metric designed to ensure fair and reliable evaluation. Our empirical study with more than 20 specific models reveals substantial room for improvement in the current techniques. Our aim is to establish this work as a fundamental resource that fosters future exploration and innovation in the dynamic field of visual text processing. The relevant repository is available at https://github.com/shuyansy/Visual-Text-Processing-survey.
- **Summary**: This paper presents a comprehensive survey of visual text processing, covering text image reconstruction (super-resolution, dewarping, enhancement) and text image manipulation (removal, editing, generation). It categorizes text features into structure, stroke, semantics, and style, and examines learning paradigms such as architectures, loss functions, and learning strategies. It also introduces VTPBench, a new benchmark comprising six visual text processing tasks, and VTPScore, a novel evaluation metric leveraging multimodal large language models (MLLMs). The paper empirically evaluates various models and identifies potential areas for improvement.

**Novelty and Significance Evaluation:**

The paper's main contributions lie in its comprehensive scope, benchmark creation, and MLLM-based evaluation metric. While surveys on text detection and recognition exist, a focused review of *visual text processing* specifically is a welcome addition.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Scope:** The paper genuinely offers a well-structured overview of the current state-of-the-art methods in visual text processing, covering various tasks and categorizing approaches based on text features and learning paradigms.
    *   **VTPBench:** The creation of a unified benchmark, VTPBench, is valuable. The paper details the rationale behind its design and composition which are important for standardization across the research field.
    *   **VTPScore:** The introduction of VTPScore, based on MLLMs, is an interesting approach to tackle the challenges in evaluating visual text processing quality.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Novelty:** The survey aspect is relatively straightforward, and a comprehensive review of existing literature, though useful, doesn't introduce revolutionary new ideas. The novelty is more on assembling and synthesizing.
    *   **VTPScore Validation:** While the authors demonstrate consistency between VTPScore and human evaluations, there's limited analysis of how VTPScore compares with established metrics for each individual task within VTPBench. The usefulness of a *unified* metric like VTPScore hinges on its ability to provide comparable or even superior results to existing, more specialized evaluation methods. More insights into specific tasks are useful.
    *   **MLLM reliance:** The evaluation is based on a commercial MLLM.  This reliance has some drawbacks such as potential changes of the model which would make the benchmarks hard to reproduce, costs and accessibility to reproduce the benchmark. The dependence also implies that it is inherently limited by the vision of the MLLM.

*   **Significance:**
    *   The survey will be a valuable resource for researchers and practitioners in the field, offering a structured overview and identifying key trends.
    *   VTPBench has the potential to become a standard benchmark, fostering more rigorous and comparable evaluations of visual text processing methods.
    *   VTPScore offers a promising approach to unified evaluation, potentially simplifying the development and comparison of models across different tasks.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper scores a 7 due to its solid, yet somewhat incremental, contributions. The comprehensive survey aspect is valuable but expected. The introduction of VTPBench and VTPScore are the main reasons for the score. It offers a concrete step towards standardization, and addresses the challenges of diverse evaluation metrics. However, it's unclear if VTPScore truly outperforms established metrics for each individual task, or if it just offers a somewhat correlated but less precise overall score. MLLM dependence is also a limitation.

### XBreaking: Explainable Artificial Intelligence for Jailbreaking LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21700v1)
- **Authors**: Marco Arazzi, Vignesh Kumar Kembu, Antonino Nocera, Vinod P
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models are fundamental actors in the modern IT landscape dominated by AI solutions. However, security threats associated with them might prevent their reliable adoption in critical application scenarios such as government organizations and medical institutions. For this reason, commercial LLMs typically undergo a sophisticated censoring mechanism to eliminate any harmful output they could possibly produce. In response to this, LLM Jailbreaking is a significant threat to such protections, and many previous approaches have already demonstrated its effectiveness across diverse domains. Existing jailbreak proposals mostly adopt a generate-and-test strategy to craft malicious input. To improve the comprehension of censoring mechanisms and design a targeted jailbreak attack, we propose an Explainable-AI solution that comparatively analyzes the behavior of censored and uncensored models to derive unique exploitable alignment patterns. Then, we propose XBreaking, a novel jailbreak attack that exploits these unique patterns to break the security constraints of LLMs by targeted noise injection. Our thorough experimental campaign returns important insights about the censoring mechanisms and demonstrates the effectiveness and performance of our attack.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "XBreaking: Explainable Artificial Intelligence for Jailbreaking LLMs" proposes a white-box jailbreaking attack strategy that uses Explainable AI (XAI) to analyze and exploit behavioral differences between censored and uncensored Large Language Models (LLMs).  The method, called XBreaking, involves: (1) using XAI to identify differences in internal activations and attention patterns between censored and uncensored LLMs, (2) identifying the key layers most influential in censoring behavior, and (3) surgically injecting noise into these identified layers to bypass security constraints. The paper presents experimental results on several open-source LLMs demonstrating the effectiveness of the approach in eliciting harmful responses.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The use of XAI techniques to understand and target specific layers for jailbreaking is a moderately novel approach. While previous work has explored jailbreaking using prompt engineering and other methods, this paper's systematic analysis of internal model representations to pinpoint vulnerabilities is a distinct contribution. However, using activation patterns to determine what's going on in an LLM is not entirely groundbreaking, especially as the "attention is all you need" concept from Vaswani et al. [37] is well known. What's novel is using that concept specifically for identifying layer-level manipulation that can bypass censoring.

*   **Significance:** The paper highlights the vulnerability of LLMs to white-box attacks even when sophisticated censoring mechanisms are in place. The findings underscore the importance of understanding internal model behaviors to develop more robust defense strategies. If the method proves easily adaptable and effective across a broader range of models and threat scenarios, it could have a significant impact. It provides a pathway for researchers to dissect censoring strategies and develop better defenses.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and research questions.
    *   Well-structured methodology with detailed steps.
    *   Empirical evaluation on multiple LLMs.
    *   Use of XAI provides insights into censoring mechanisms, moving beyond purely black-box attack strategies.
    *   The paper attempts to assess both the effectiveness of the jailbreak *and* the maintenance of model functionality.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The threat model assumes access to both censored and uncensored versions of the *same* model, which may not always be realistic in practice. This limits the generalizability of the findings.  Real-world attackers may only have access to the censored version.
    *   The "noise injection" step is somewhat simplistic. More sophisticated perturbation techniques might yield better results. The use of simple gaussian noise on layers with multiple parameters is a rudimentary approach.
    *   Reliance on JudgeLLM for evaluation raises concerns about bias and reliability, especially if the JudgeLLM has similar vulnerabilities. While the authors did assess the JudgeLLM for accuracy, the small sample size of 120 samples is not enough to make significant claims about the robustness of JudgeLLM's ability to correctly evaluate harm.
    *   Lack of comparison to other existing white-box jailbreaking attacks. The paper introduces a new method without situating it against alternatives.
    *   Limited scope: The experiments are carried out only on models of relatively small scale (1B to 7B parameters), so the efficacy of this approach on larger foundation models that are increasingly prevalent in real-world usage is unclear.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence research directions in LLM security, particularly by encouraging a deeper exploration of internal model representations for vulnerability analysis. It may also inspire the development of more targeted and adaptive defense mechanisms. However, the practical applicability of the method in real-world scenarios remains to be seen given the limitations of the threat model.

*   **Rigorous Rationale for the Score:**

    While the use of XAI for jailbreaking LLMs is an interesting and potentially fruitful direction, the paper has notable limitations. The strong assumption about white-box access (censored and uncensored versions available) and the relatively unsophisticated noise injection technique significantly restrict the real-world relevance of the approach. The lack of comparison to other existing attack techniques and the use of potentially biased LLM judge model further weaken the experimental results. Despite these drawbacks, the paper provides a valuable proof-of-concept and suggests a promising path for future research in the field.

### Vision Transformers in Precision Agriculture: A Comprehensive Survey
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21706v1)
- **Authors**: Saber Mehdipour, Seyed Abolghasem Mirroshandel, Seyed Amirhossein Tabatabaei
- **Abstract**: Detecting plant diseases is a crucial aspect of modern agriculture - it plays a key role in maintaining crop health and increasing overall yield. Traditional approaches, though still valuable, often rely on manual inspection or conventional machine learning techniques, both of which face limitations in scalability and accuracy. Recently, Vision Transformers (ViTs) have emerged as a promising alternative, offering benefits such as improved handling of long-range dependencies and better scalability for visual tasks. This survey explores the application of ViTs in precision agriculture, covering tasks from classification to detection and segmentation. We begin by introducing the foundational architecture of ViTs and discuss their transition from Natural Language Processing (NLP) to computer vision. The discussion includes the concept of inductive bias in traditional models like Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNs), and how ViTs mitigate these biases. We provide a comprehensive review of recent literature, focusing on key methodologies, datasets, and performance metrics. The survey also includes a comparative analysis of CNNs and ViTs, with a look at hybrid models and performance enhancements. Technical challenges - such as data requirements, computational demands, and model interpretability - are addressed alongside potential solutions. Finally, we outline potential research directions and technological advancements that could further support the integration of ViTs in real-world agricultural settings. Our goal with this study is to offer practitioners and researchers a deeper understanding of how ViTs are poised to transform smart and precision agriculture.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided survey paper on Vision Transformers in Precision Agriculture:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a comprehensive survey of Vision Transformers (ViTs) and their application in precision agriculture, specifically focusing on plant disease detection and classification. It starts by introducing the Transformer architecture and its adaptation to computer vision (ViTs), contrasting them with Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNs) and discussing the concept of inductive biases. The core of the survey reviews recent literature on ViTs in precision agriculture, categorizing them into pure and hybrid models, analyzing methodologies, datasets, and performance. It also addresses technical challenges like data requirements, computational demands, and model interpretability. Finally, the paper outlines potential research directions and technological advancements that could further integrate ViTs into real-world agricultural settings. The goal is to provide practitioners and researchers with a deeper understanding of how ViTs can transform smart and precision agriculture.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Scope:** The survey provides a well-structured overview of ViTs and their applications in a specific domain (precision agriculture). It effectively covers the theoretical background, different architectural variations (pure vs. hybrid models), and practical challenges.
    *   **Clear Organization:** The paper is logically organized, starting with the basics of Transformers and progressively delving into the specifics of ViTs and their application to agriculture. The categorization of models into "Pure" and "Hybrid" is helpful.
    *   **Emphasis on Challenges:** The paper acknowledges the limitations and challenges associated with ViTs, including data dependence, computational cost, and lack of interpretability, which is crucial for a balanced assessment.
    *   **Focus on a Niche Area:**  A focused review on a specialized area such as ViTs in Precision Agriculture is more useful than a generalized review on all the DL models in the agriculture fields.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Critical Depth:**  While the survey is comprehensive, it sometimes lacks a deep critical analysis of individual papers.  The discussion often summarizes the results without sufficiently questioning the experimental design, statistical significance, or generalizability of the findings. It would be much more helpful if the survey were to provide a rigorous discussion and comparison of those papers.
    *   **Dataset Reliance:** The number of times each dataset has been used across studies is insightful but should be augmented with a critical analysis of these datasets themselves. What are the biases inherent in PlantVillage, and how does that affect research outcomes? Without that discussion, readers may not be able to make informed decisions when designing a research.
    *   **Limited Evaluation Beyond Metrics:** While metrics such as accuracy, precision, and F1 score are listed, there is little discussion on aspects such as deployment costs, data collection costs, and other real-world constraints, which are extremely important in the agricultural domain.
    *   **Limited Novel Insight:**  While comprehensive, the survey doesn't offer substantially novel insights or a bold new perspective on the field. It mainly synthesizes existing knowledge rather than forging a new path.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    The primary value of this paper lies in its consolidation of research on ViTs in precision agriculture. This topic is relatively new and rapidly evolving, so a comprehensive survey provides a useful resource for researchers and practitioners. However, as there are limited analyses of papers, models and Datasets, a rigorous assessment of the quality of the research papers is not possible. This paper still serves as an important resource, and this may encourage more targeted research.

**Score: 6.5**

**Justification:**

The paper merits a 6.5 because it is a well-organized and comprehensive survey of ViTs in precision agriculture, a rapidly growing area.  It offers a good introduction to the field, discusses key challenges, and categorizes existing research. However, it falls short of being truly exceptional due to its limited critical depth and lack of novel insights. While it synthesizes existing knowledge effectively, it could benefit from a more critical assessment of the research papers it covers, a stronger emphasis on real-world deployment challenges, and the generation of novel perspectives for future research.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### TheraQuest: A Gamified, LLM-Powered Simulation for Massage Therapy Training
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21735v1)
- **Authors**: Shengqian Wang
- **Abstract**: Massage therapy training emphasizes hands-on techniques and effective therapist--patient communication. However, many educational programs struggle to provide realistic practice scenarios. To address this problem, we propose TheraQuest, a gamified, web-based simulation platform that employs large language models (LLMs) to generate diverse virtual patients with varying symptoms and cultural backgrounds. Through interactive dialogue, anatomical decision-making, and immediate assessment, trainees develop both diagnostic reasoning and empathetic communication skills in a low-risk environment. Unlike exclusively VR-based solutions, TheraQuest remains accessible via standard web browsers, mitigating the cost and discomfort associated with extended headset use. Preliminary testing suggests that integrating LLM-driven virtual patients with real-time skill metrics can enhance trainee engagement and help bridge the gap between theoretical knowledge and clinical proficiency.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TheraQuest, a gamified, web-based simulation platform designed to enhance massage therapy training. It leverages large language models (LLMs) to create diverse virtual patients with varying symptoms and cultural backgrounds, enabling trainees to practice diagnostic reasoning and empathetic communication skills in a low-risk environment. The system features interactive dialogues, anatomical decision-making, and real-time assessments.  It aims to address the gap in realistic practice scenarios and improve therapist-patient communication, especially when dealing with diverse patient populations. The authors compare their approach to existing VR/AR solutions, highlighting TheraQuest's accessibility through standard web browsers and its potential for trainee engagement. The paper outlines the system's design, architecture, and a proposed two-stage evaluation strategy involving expert reviews and user studies.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper has some degree of novelty, primarily in its specific application of LLMs and gamification to massage therapy training. While virtual patients and gamified simulations are established concepts in medical education, their focused integration within this specific tactile-based discipline represents a more refined contribution. It also address the communication challenges especially dealing with diverse patient backgrounds.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in its potential to bridge the gap between theoretical knowledge and practical skills in massage therapy training. By creating a more realistic and accessible simulation environment, TheraQuest could help trainees develop critical diagnostic and communication skills. This can directly impact the quality of care provided by future massage therapists. The emphasis on addressing communication challenges with diverse patient populations in Canada is also a significant point, reflecting a timely and relevant concern.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addressing a Specific Need:** The paper identifies a clear need in massage therapy training, specifically the lack of realistic communication practice and the challenges of dealing with diverse patient populations.

    *   **LLM Integration:** The use of LLMs to generate diverse and interactive virtual patients is a valuable aspect of the research.

    *   **Accessibility:** The web-based nature of the platform makes it more accessible and affordable than VR/AR-based solutions.

    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation Plan:** The proposed two-stage evaluation plan, involving expert reviews and user studies, is well-structured and rigorous.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Prototype Stage:** The project is currently in the prototype stage, so there are no empirical results to demonstrate its effectiveness. The paper relies on potential benefits rather than demonstrated outcomes.

    *   **Limited Scope:** The system currently focuses on specific aspects of massage therapy training, and some advanced techniques and complex patient conditions are not fully integrated.

    *   **Lack of Tactile Feedback:** As a web-based simulation, TheraQuest lacks the crucial tactile feedback that is central to massage therapy. It's challenging to translate a highly tactile skill into a web-based environment.

    *   **Domain Expertise Required for LLM Training:** The success of generating realistic virtual patients heavily relies on the amount and quality of domain specific training data available to the LLMs, which could be a limiting factor.

*   **Potential Impact:** If the system proves effective in user studies, it could potentially influence massage therapy training programs by providing a scalable and accessible simulation tool. It could also inspire similar approaches in other tactile-based healthcare disciplines.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the above points, TheraQuest represents a modest, yet potentially valuable contribution. While not groundbreaking, its focused application of LLMs and gamification to address specific challenges in massage therapy training, its emphasis on diversity in patient presentations, and its accessibility offer some novelty and significance. The current prototype stage and lack of empirical data limits the impact. Also, the web-based format is not a great replacement for tactile learning.
