### Investigating Literary Motifs in Ancient and Medieval Novels with Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21742v1)
- **Authors**: Emelie Hallenberg
- **Abstract**: The Greek fictional narratives often termed love novels or romances, ranging from the first century CE to the middle of the 15th century, have long been considered as similar in many ways, not least in the use of particular literary motifs. By applying the use of fine-tuned large language models, this study aims to investigate which motifs exactly that the texts in this corpus have in common, and in which ways they differ from each other. The results show that while some motifs persist throughout the corpus, others fluctuate in frequency, indicating certain trends or external influences. Conclusively, the method proves to adequately extract literary motifs according to a set definition, providing data for both quantitative and qualitative analyses.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Investigating Literary Motifs in Ancient and Medieval Novels with Large Language Models," based on the provided text.

**Summary**

The paper investigates the use of literary motifs in ancient and medieval Greek "love novels" (romances) using fine-tuned large language models (LLMs). It aims to identify common and differing motifs across texts spanning from the 1st to 15th centuries CE. The study first extracts motifs from the text using a fine-tuned LLM. Then, it uses sentence embeddings to cluster the extracted motifs and summarizes each cluster. Finally, the study analyzes the motif frequencies across different time periods (Imperial, Komnenian, and Palaiologan) to identify fluctuating and persistent motifs. The results indicate motif trends over time, and the methodology validates the potential of LLMs for extracting literary motifs in providing data for quantitative and qualitative analysis.

**Critical Evaluation**

**Novelty:** The application of fine-tuned LLMs to extract and analyze literary motifs in ancient Greek novels has some degree of novelty, although topic modeling and similar techniques have been applied to literary texts before. The crucial aspect is the fine-tuning of GPT-4 for motif extraction, which allows for more nuanced interpretation than traditional methods, such as word frequency analysis. The paper is also interesting because it directly compares novels in different Greek periods using a LLM, which provides a quantifiable basis to literary theories.

**Significance:** The significance lies in the potential to provide empirical support for literary theories regarding genre evolution, influence, and thematic consistency within and across different periods of Greek novel writing. The quantifiable aspect could offer new insights to scholars, and can give information about the novelistic tradition over time. The extraction of motif data is a tool that can serve as a foundation for further studies.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Research Question:** The paper defines a clear research question: identifying and analyzing literary motifs across ancient and medieval Greek novels.
*   **Methodological Soundness:** The method is clearly outlined and detailed steps described in order.
*   **Interesting Preliminary Results:** the resulting examples given demonstrate a good degree of extraction by the model. The charts comparing motif fluctuation and persistence add to the analysis.
*   **Emphasis on Replicability:** The commitment to open access for the code and the fine-tuned model enhances the study's replicability, encouraging future research to build on the findings.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope:** The paper focuses on a relatively limited corpus of texts. A broader study encompassing more novels and literary works from the periods in question could yield more robust results.
*   **Model Dependency:** The results are dependent on the LLM and the quality of the fine-tuning data. The fine-tuning dataset (74 annotated examples) is relatively small and has the potential to introduce bias or limit the model's ability to generalize. Also, the evaluation seems to be more dependent on the LLMs performance to extract, cluster and summarize the texts, with less analysis from a human perspective. A better evaluation may involve human readability tests, especially since the result of each clustering requires some degree of interpretation.
*   **Oversimplification of Motifs:** Reducing complex literary themes to concise, single-sentence motifs runs the risk of oversimplification and loss of nuance. The interpretation, as mentioned, of the resulting clusters requires detailed prior knowledge of the context, and it is possible that different literary scholars make different interpretations. While quantification is useful, it should be complemented by qualitative analysis to capture the full richness of the motifs.
*   **Lack of a Baseline:** The paper lacks a solid baseline. Although mentioned traditional methods, there is no result presented to give a comparison and how the method performs better. This would give evidence for the effectiveness of using a fine-tuned LLM for these sort of tasks.
*   **Statistical Analysis:** The statistical analysis is rather simple. More advanced statistical techniques could have been employed to identify statistically significant differences in motif frequencies across the different time periods.
*   **Unclear Justification for Architectural Choices:** The paper lacks justification for certain choices such as model architecture for the fine-tuning process. What is the rationale for using the TikTok tokenizer?

**Potential Influence:**

While the study has limitations, it offers a valuable starting point for using LLMs in literary analysis. By demonstrating the feasibility of automatically extracting and analyzing literary motifs, the paper could encourage further research in this area, leading to the development of more sophisticated and nuanced methodologies.

**Score: 6**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel application of LLMs to a specific area of literary study. It has the potential to provide quantitative support for literary theories, it's well-structured and replicable. However, the limitations in scope, evaluation, LLM-dependency and the potential for oversimplification, the need for statistical complexity and missing justification of architectural choices, prevent it from being a truly groundbreaking contribution. It's a solid piece of work that demonstrates potential, but requires further refinement to reach its full potential.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### LLM-based Interactive Imitation Learning for Robotic Manipulation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21769v1)
- **Authors**: Jonas Werner, Kun Chu, Cornelius Weber, Stefan Wermter
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in machine learning provide methods to train autonomous agents capable of handling the increasing complexity of sequential decision-making in robotics. Imitation Learning (IL) is a prominent approach, where agents learn to control robots based on human demonstrations. However, IL commonly suffers from violating the independent and identically distributed (i.i.d) assumption in robotic tasks. Interactive Imitation Learning (IIL) achieves improved performance by allowing agents to learn from interactive feedback from human teachers. Despite these improvements, both approaches come with significant costs due to the necessity of human involvement. Leveraging the emergent capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) in reasoning and generating human-like responses, we introduce LLM-iTeach -- a novel IIL framework that utilizes an LLM as an interactive teacher to enhance agent performance while alleviating the dependence on human resources. Firstly, LLM-iTeach uses a hierarchical prompting strategy that guides the LLM in generating a policy in Python code. Then, with a designed similarity-based feedback mechanism, LLM-iTeach provides corrective and evaluative feedback interactively during the agent's training. We evaluate LLM-iTeach against baseline methods such as Behavior Cloning (BC), an IL method, and CEILing, a state-of-the-art IIL method using a human teacher, on various robotic manipulation tasks. Our results demonstrate that LLM-iTeach surpasses BC in the success rate and achieves or even outscores that of CEILing, highlighting the potential of LLMs as cost-effective, human-like teachers in interactive learning environments. We further demonstrate the method's potential for generalization by evaluating it on additional tasks. The code and prompts are provided at: https://github.com/Tubicor/LLM-iTeach.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "LLM-based Interactive Imitation Learning for Robotic Manipulation."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LLM-iTeach, a novel interactive imitation learning (IIL) framework that uses a large language model (LLM) as an interactive teacher for robotic manipulation tasks.  The framework aims to reduce the reliance on human involvement in IIL by leveraging the reasoning and code-generation capabilities of LLMs.  LLM-iTeach employs a hierarchical prompting strategy to generate a task-specific policy in Python code (called CodePolicy). This policy is then used to provide corrective and evaluative feedback to a learning agent through a similarity-based mechanism, guiding its training. The authors compare LLM-iTeach against Behavior Cloning (BC) and CEILing (a state-of-the-art IIL method with a human teacher) on several robotic manipulation tasks, showing that LLM-iTeach can achieve comparable or even superior performance to CEILing while significantly reducing costs associated with human labor.  They also demonstrate the approach's adaptability to new tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:**

The primary novelty lies in replacing a human teacher with an LLM within an IIL framework for robotic manipulation. While LLMs have been used for planning, action generation, and action evaluation in robotics, the direct substitution of a human teacher with an LLM that provides *interactive* and *corrective/evaluative* feedback based on comparing CodePolicy actions with the agent's actions represents a clear and valuable innovation. The hierarchical prompting strategy to generate CodePolicy and the similarity-checking feedback mechanism are also notable contributions. Adapting hierarchical prompting for IIL corrective feedback is novel.

* **Significance:**

The significance of this work stems from the potential to significantly reduce the cost and effort associated with training robots for complex manipulation tasks.  Human-in-the-loop approaches are often limited by the availability and expertise of human teachers. By demonstrating that an LLM can effectively guide the learning process, the paper opens up new avenues for scaling up robotic training and deployment. The results show performance on par with a state-of-the-art human-in-the-loop IIL method, suggesting that LLMs can be effective substitutes for human teachers, at least in certain settings.

* **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear and well-structured paper:** The paper is easy to follow and the methods are explained in detail.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The authors conduct a thorough evaluation by comparing LLM-iTeach to strong baselines (BC and CEILing) on multiple robotic manipulation tasks. Also, they present additional tasks to demonstrate extension ability.
    *   **Ablation studies:** The ablation studies help to understand the contribution of individual components of the framework (e.g., evaluative vs. corrective feedback, warm-start).
    *   **Scalability:** The authors conduct additional experiments to show scaling up the training episodes. The scaling experiments help validate the efficacy of LLM-iTeach with longer training durations, which are valuable since human teachers have time and expense constraints.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The code and prompts are made publicly available.

* **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Task Complexity:** While the robotic manipulation tasks are relevant, their complexity might be limited, and the environment (RLBench) is simulated. While RLBench is standard in the robotics field, transferring to a real-world setting presents challenges due to perception and motor control errors. The simulation does not fully capture the nuances of the real world.
    *   **Limited LLM's Physical Reasoning:** The LLM has limitations in physical reasoning and awareness of inverse kinematics constraints, which can lead to suboptimal actions and require episode termination. The authors acknowledge this limitation and point to it as a direction for future work.
    *   **Reliance on Ground Truth:** The presented setup for LLM-iTeach relies on ground truth data for generating feedback, which limits the approach's applicability to real-world scenarios.
    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The performance is somewhat sensitive to the choice of the similarity threshold *ß*, though a hyperparameter study is provided.
    *   **Choice of tasks for generalization:** The four new tasks selected for evaluation of LLM-iTeach's generalization ability may be perceived as relatively similar to the tasks included for comparing the approach against other baselines. More diverse and complex robotic manipulation tasks could have made the extension ability evaluation more robust.

* **Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence research in IIL, robotic training, and the application of LLMs to robotics. It provides a compelling demonstration of how LLMs can be used to automate the teaching process and reduce the need for human intervention. The hierarchical prompting and similarity-checking mechanisms could be adopted and extended by other researchers. This approach also opens the door to exploring more sophisticated feedback mechanisms and incorporating other modalities (e.g., vision) into the LLM-based teaching process.

* **Score:**

Given the paper's novelty in directly substituting a human teacher with an LLM for IIL, the clear and well-conducted evaluation, the potential to significantly reduce the cost of robotic training, and the detailed ablation study, I believe a score of 7 is appropriate. The identified limitations (especially the reliance on ground truth data and limited physical reasoning) prevent a higher score.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### LASHED: LLMs And Static Hardware Analysis for Early Detection of RTL Bugs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21770v1)
- **Authors**: Baleegh Ahmad, Hammond Pearce, Ramesh Karri, Benjamin Tan
- **Abstract**: While static analysis is useful in detecting early-stage hardware security bugs, its efficacy is limited because it requires information to form checks and is often unable to explain the security impact of a detected vulnerability. Large Language Models can be useful in filling these gaps by identifying relevant assets, removing false violations flagged by static analysis tools, and explaining the reported violations. LASHED combines the two approaches (LLMs and Static Analysis) to overcome each other's limitations for hardware security bug detection. We investigate our approach on four open-source SoCs for five Common Weakness Enumerations (CWEs) and present strategies for improvement with better prompt engineering. We find that 87.5% of instances flagged by our recommended scheme are plausible CWEs. In-context learning and asking the model to 'think again' improves LASHED's precision.
- **Summary**: This paper introduces LASHED, a novel framework that combines Large Language Models (LLMs) and static analysis techniques for the early detection of security bugs in Register-Transfer Level (RTL) hardware code. LASHED leverages LLMs to identify security-relevant assets, prune false positives from static analysis results, and provide explanations for reported violations, focusing on Common Weakness Enumerations (CWEs). The framework is validated on four open-source SoCs, using both linting and formal property verification for static analysis. The paper explores different prompt engineering strategies, including in-context learning and prompting the LLM to "think again," to improve the precision of bug detection. The results demonstrate promising performance, with the recommended scheme achieving an 87.5% precision in identifying plausible CWEs.

**Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:**

The paper presents a valuable combination of LLMs and static analysis for hardware security, addressing a crucial need for early bug detection in RTL design. However, a critical evaluation reveals some aspects requiring deeper consideration:

**Strengths:**

*   **Novelty:** The approach of integrating LLMs with static analysis tools for hardware security bug detection in RTL code is novel. While similar combinations exist in software security, this is one of the first works applying it to hardware.
*   **Practical Relevance:** The problem of early detection of hardware security vulnerabilities is highly relevant to the industry due to the high costs associated with fixing bugs later in the development cycle.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper performs experiments across multiple open-source SoCs and CWEs, allowing for a reasonably broad understanding of the tool's performance.
*   **Prompt Engineering Exploration:** The investigation of different prompt strategies and their impact on performance is valuable for the practical application of LLMs in this domain.
*   **Framework Architecture:** The division of the framework into asset identification, static analysis, and contextualization is logical and facilitates understanding and further development.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope of CWEs:** While the paper covers five CWEs, it would be beneficial to extend the analysis to a broader range of vulnerabilities to demonstrate the generalizability of the approach. The choice seems guided heavily by the availability of coded examples, which is understandable for initial prototyping, but limits impact if it doesn't generalize.
*   **Reliance on MITRE Examples:** The framework heavily relies on the MITRE examples for in-context learning. This reliance might limit the model's ability to detect novel or less common vulnerabilities. The authors even concede that being inspired by Hack@DAC limited performance on Veerwolf, for example.
*   **Scalability Concerns:** Although time complexity is sub-linear w.r.t. LOC, the cost per run with gpt-4o is significant. While cheaper models are explored, the large difference in cost (6.9x) between the two models calls into question the feasibility and sustainability of the approach at scale. What is the minimal LLM size that still shows benefit from static analysis?
*   **Subjectivity in Evaluation:** The manual evaluation of flagged violations introduces subjectivity. While the authors argue that accompanying failing assertions add credence, a more objective evaluation methodology would strengthen the results (e.g., use of independently verified ground truth). How are the "true positives" even determined?
*   **Lack of Comparison with Baseline LLM-only or Static-analysis only approaches:** There's no discussion in the results of how this improves over a *completely* LLM-only approach for hardware. Or how this does versus a *very good* formal verification tool that does the analysis alone, assuming someone can develop it. The results in their current form only show that improvements can be made via better prompting.
*   **Oversimplification of Static Analysis:** The paper uses either linting or simple formal properties, and no effort is spent investigating the best static analysis strategy, rather the *combination* of LLM and static analysis is the emphasis. This feels like a missed opportunity. What is the *right* formal analysis combined with the *right* LLM prompting for the greatest coverage?

**Significance:**

The paper's contribution lies in demonstrating the potential of combining LLMs with static analysis for hardware security bug detection, but it is still in its early stages. Further research is needed to address the weaknesses outlined above and to demonstrate the scalability, generalizability, and robustness of the approach. The exploration of different prompt strategies is a significant contribution that could inform future research in this area. Overall, the framework is a solid first step but needs further development to prove practical value.

**Score: 6**

**Justification:**

The paper introduces a novel approach to a relevant problem and presents promising initial results. However, the limitations in scope, reliance on MITRE examples, subjectivity in evaluation, lack of detailed comparison with state-of-the-art formal methods and potentially high cost prevent a higher score. The paper shows some performance improvement over baseline for specific use cases, but it is not yet clear that this advantage is significant enough to warrant its adoption in practice or whether this improvement holds across a wide variety of situations.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 6/10

### MAC-Tuning: LLM Multi-Compositional Problem Reasoning with Enhanced Knowledge Boundary Awareness
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21773v1)
- **Authors**: Junsheng Huang, Zhitao He, Sandeep Polisetty, Qingyun Wang, May Fung
- **Abstract**: With the widespread application of large language models (LLMs), the issue of generating non-existing facts, known as hallucination, has garnered increasing attention. Previous research in enhancing LLM confidence estimation mainly focuses on the single problem setting. However, LLM awareness of its internal parameterized knowledge boundary under the more challenging multi-problem setting, which requires answering multiple problems accurately simultaneously, remains underexplored. To bridge this gap, we introduce a novel method, Multiple Answers and Confidence Stepwise Tuning (MAC-Tuning), that separates the learning of answer prediction and confidence estimation during fine-tuning on instruction data. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method outperforms baselines by up to 25% in average precision.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of hallucinations in large language models (LLMs) when answering multiple questions simultaneously (multi-problem setting). It introduces a novel method called Multiple Answers and Confidence Stepwise Tuning (MAC-Tuning).  MAC-Tuning separates the fine-tuning process into two steps: one for predicting the ground-truth answers and another for estimating the confidence in those answers. The method uses automatically labeled confidence data to train the model to better distinguish between reliable and unreliable information, thereby reducing hallucinations and improving accuracy. Experimental results across several datasets demonstrate that MAC-Tuning outperforms baselines, achieving gains in average precision (AP) and reducing the Expected Calibrated Error (ECE).

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's core novelty lies in specifically targeting LLM hallucination within the multi-problem setting.  While prior work has addressed hallucination, this paper highlights the unique challenges of managing multiple sub-questions within a single input and the potential for context overshadowing and reasoning confusion. The two-step tuning approach is a reasonably novel way to tackle the problem of confidence estimation, as it separates the concerns of answer prediction and confidence assessment.

*   **Significance:** The multi-problem setting is becoming increasingly relevant due to efficiency gains in scenarios with shared contexts, reduced model access, and lower API costs. Addressing hallucination in this setting is therefore of practical importance. The demonstrated improvements in AP and ECE suggest that MAC-Tuning can significantly improve the reliability of LLMs in these contexts. The paper contributes to the important and ongoing effort to improve the trustworthiness and reliability of LLMs.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges of LLM hallucination in the multi-problem setting.
    *   **Novel Method:** The proposed MAC-Tuning method is well-defined and intuitively reasonable. The separation of answer prediction and confidence tuning makes sense.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The experiments are comprehensive, covering various datasets and baselines.  The gains in AP and the reduction in ECE provide convincing evidence of MAC-Tuning's effectiveness.
    *   **Ablation Studies:**  The ablation studies (QA-Only, Single-QA, Merge-AC) help to isolate the contributions of different components of the MAC-Tuning method. The analysis with a different base model (Qwen2) demonstrates the generalizability of the approach.
    *   **Detailed Analysis:** The number of questions analysis and the out-of-domain transfer study provide additional insights into the behavior of MAC-Tuning.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of "Multi-Problem":** The definition of "multi-problem" is limited to combining multiple questions from existing datasets.  The problems are essentially independent, and the work does not address more complex forms of compositionality (e.g., nested questions, questions that depend on answers to previous questions).
    *   **Automatic Labeling Reliance:** The automatic labeling of confidence (I am sure / I am unsure) relies on the alignment between the LLM's output and the ground-truth answer.  This approach might be overly simplistic, since the model could give the correct answer for the wrong reasons.
    *   **Modest Improvement in Certain Cases:** While the average improvements are significant, there are certain dataset categories where the improvements are not as substantial, indicating that the method might not be universally effective across all types of questions and knowledge domains.
    *   **Lack of Qualitative Examples:** The paper lacks detailed qualitative examples showcasing instances where MAC-Tuning effectively reduces hallucinations. Adding these could further strengthen the work's arguments.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's findings and method can influence the design and training of LLMs for multi-task scenarios. It provides a valuable approach for improving the reliability of LLMs in practical applications.  Future research can build upon this work by exploring more sophisticated confidence estimation techniques and addressing more complex forms of problem compositionality.

**Justification of Score:**

Considering the strengths and weaknesses, the paper presents a useful and reasonably novel method for addressing a relevant problem.  While the definition of "multi-problem" is somewhat limited and the confidence labeling process is relatively simple, the experimental results are compelling, and the analysis is thorough. The paper makes a solid contribution to the field of LLM reliability, but further work is needed to address its limitations and improve its generalizability.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### DeepSeek-Prover-V2: Advancing Formal Mathematical Reasoning via Reinforcement Learning for Subgoal Decomposition
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21801v1)
- **Authors**: Z. Z. Ren, Zhihong Shao, Junxiao Song, Huajian Xin, Haocheng Wang, Wanjia Zhao, Liyue Zhang, Zhe Fu, Qihao Zhu, Dejian Yang, Z. F. Wu, Zhibin Gou, Shirong Ma, Hongxuan Tang, Yuxuan Liu, Wenjun Gao, Daya Guo, Chong Ruan
- **Abstract**: We introduce DeepSeek-Prover-V2, an open-source large language model designed for formal theorem proving in Lean 4, with initialization data collected through a recursive theorem proving pipeline powered by DeepSeek-V3. The cold-start training procedure begins by prompting DeepSeek-V3 to decompose complex problems into a series of subgoals. The proofs of resolved subgoals are synthesized into a chain-of-thought process, combined with DeepSeek-V3's step-by-step reasoning, to create an initial cold start for reinforcement learning. This process enables us to integrate both informal and formal mathematical reasoning into a unified model. The resulting model, DeepSeek-Prover-V2-671B, achieves state-of-the-art performance in neural theorem proving, reaching 88.9% pass ratio on the MiniF2F-test and solving 49 out of 658 problems from PutnamBench. In addition to standard benchmarks, we introduce ProverBench, a collection of 325 formalized problems, to enrich our evaluation, including 15 selected problems from the recent AIME competitions (years 24-25). Further evaluation on these 15 AIME problems shows that the model successfully solves 6 of them. In comparison, DeepSeek-V3 solves 8 of these problems using majority voting, highlighting that the gap between formal and informal mathematical reasoning in large language models is substantially narrowing.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and evaluation of the DeepSeek-Prover-V2 paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DeepSeek-Prover-V2, a large language model (LLM) for formal theorem proving in Lean 4. It leverages DeepSeek-V3 to decompose complex theorems into subgoals. These subgoals are then solved and formalized in Lean 4. The initial training data is generated through this recursive theorem-proving pipeline and combined with chain-of-thought reasoning to create a cold-start dataset for reinforcement learning. The resulting model, DeepSeek-Prover-V2-671B, achieves state-of-the-art performance on MiniF2F-test, PutnamBench, and a newly introduced ProverBench, demonstrating advancements in bridging informal and formal mathematical reasoning. They also find a few problems solved by a smaller model version which remain unsolved by larger models which employ a different inference strategy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**

    *   **Strengths:** The paper presents a novel approach to combine informal and formal reasoning using LLMs. The recursive theorem-proving pipeline for generating cold-start data is a creative way to initialize the model with both high-level proof strategies and formal proof details.  The framework's ability to then distill this process into a smaller, more computationally efficient model is also valuable. The introduction of the ProverBench benchmark is a useful contribution to the field, enabling more targeted evaluation. Also, the unexpected finding of skills (toNat and natCast\_inj) discovered by some models on PutnamBench is a positive.

    *   **Weaknesses:** Some aspects build upon existing techniques, such as expert iteration and reinforcement learning for theorem proving.  The core idea of subgoal decomposition isn't entirely new. While the integration is novel, the individual components have precedent. The paper is fairly incremental in its approach, it is not disruptive to the existing techniques.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Strengths:** Achieving state-of-the-art results on established benchmarks (MiniF2F, PutnamBench) demonstrates the effectiveness of the proposed approach. The substantial performance gain over previous methods and baseline models is impressive. The work demonstrates the growing potential of LLMs to tackle complex formal reasoning tasks and represents a significant advancement in neural theorem proving. The narrowing gap between informal and formal mathematical reasoning is an important trend.

    *   **Weaknesses:** The study primarily focuses on performance improvement and doesn't delve extensively into the underlying mechanisms that contribute to its success. There is a lack of interpretability - for example why do certain models perform better on some benchmarks but not on others? While impressive, the results are still limited to specific benchmarks and might not generalize perfectly to other formal proving domains.  Also, the models remain far from human-level mathematical reasoning and problem-solving. Further theoretical analysis could strengthen the paper.

*   **Justification for Score:**

    I am assigning a score of 7. The DeepSeek-Prover-V2 paper presents a well-executed and compelling approach for formal theorem proving. The combination of techniques, the development of a synthetic cold-start dataset, and the impressive results warrant a high score. However, a few factors prevent it from receiving a higher rating. First, some components build on existing research, the approach can be thought of as more of an engineering achievement than a breakthrough scientific discovery. Second, the paper could benefit from a deeper theoretical analysis and more thorough interpretability of the model's behavior and how it is able to outperform other models. Overall, the paper is a significant step forward, but not a paradigm shift.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### An Empirical Study on the Effectiveness of Large Language Models for Binary Code Understanding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21803v1)
- **Authors**: Xiuwei Shang, Zhenkan Fu, Shaoyin Cheng, Guoqiang Chen, Gangyang Li, Li Hu, Weiming Zhang, Nenghai Yu
- **Abstract**: Binary code analysis plays a pivotal role in the field of software security and is widely used in tasks such as software maintenance, malware detection, software vulnerability discovery, patch analysis, etc. However, unlike source code, reverse engineers face significant challenges in understanding binary code due to the lack of intuitive semantic information. Although traditional reverse tools can convert binary code into C-like pseudo code, the lack of code comments and symbolic information such as function names still makes code understanding difficult. In recent years, two groups of techniques have shown promising prospects: (1) Deep learning-based techniques have demonstrated competitive results in tasks related to binary code understanding, furthermore, (2) Large Language Models (LLMs) have been extensively pre-trained at the source-code level for tasks such as code understanding and generation. This has left participants wondering about the capabilities of LLMs in binary code understanding. To this end, this work proposes a benchmark to evaluate the effectiveness of LLMs in real-world reverse engineering scenarios, which covers two key binary code understanding tasks, i.e., function name recovery and binary code summarization. To more comprehensively evaluate, we include binaries with multiple target architectures as well as different optimization options. We gain valuable insights into the capabilities and limitations through extensive empirical studies of popular LLMs using our benchmark. Our evaluations reveal that existing LLMs can understand binary code to a certain extent, thereby improving the efficiency of binary code analysis. Our results highlight the great potential of the LLMs in advancing the field of binary code understanding, and provide new directions for binary code analysis techniques.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've reviewed the provided OCR output for the paper "An Empirical Study on the Effectiveness of Large Language Models for Binary Code Understanding." Here's a summary, followed by a critical evaluation:

**Summary**

The paper investigates the effectiveness of Large Language Models (LLMs) in two key binary code understanding tasks: function name recovery and binary code summarization. The authors create an evaluation benchmark dataset by automatically creating datasets with a source code aligned, and use stripping and decompiling techniques to create realistic, yet challenging binary data to evaluate LLMs' capabilities on real-world reverse engineering scenarios.  They evaluate various LLMs (code-specific, general-purpose, and even deep learning (DL)-based expert tools) across different target architectures (x86, x64, ARM, MIPS) and compiler optimization levels. They also examine the impact of few-shot prompting and fine-tuning.  The results indicate that LLMs can understand binary code to a certain extent, especially in MIPS architecture, and ChatGPT performs well in summarizing binary code.  However, the paper also identifies limitations and points to future research directions, such as developing domain-specific LLMs, extending context windows, improving robustness to obfuscation, and integrating multi-modal information.

**Critical Evaluation**

**Novelty:** The paper's main strength lies in providing a systematic and empirical evaluation of LLMs for a practical software engineering task: binary code understanding.  While individual components (like using DL for code understanding) and LLMs themselves aren't entirely new, the *combination* of evaluating a broad range of LLMs *specifically on binary code* and establishing a benchmark for doing so constitutes a valuable contribution.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The study evaluates a wide range of LLMs and architectures providing a broad picture of LLM performance. The use of different architectures and optimization levels adds depth and nuance to the analysis. The comparison against specialized DL-based tools is particularly helpful.
    *   **Practical Relevance:** Binary code analysis is a crucial task in software security and reverse engineering. The study directly addresses the challenges faced by practitioners and explores whether LLMs can be valuable tools.
    *   **Reproducible Benchmark:** Creating and releasing a benchmark dataset will enable future research to build upon this work and compare results in a standardized way. The use of automated methods for generating the dataset also adds to its scalability.
    *   **Insightful Analysis:** The paper dives beyond just reporting scores. It analyzes the reasons behind the performance variations (e.g., the impact of MIPS architecture, limitations of domain LLMs compared to general ones in summaries, limitations of the existing DL-based expert models).
    * **Case Studies** Demonstrates the potential of LLMs in assisting reverse engineers.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Evaluation Metric Limitations:**  The reliance on BLEU and ROUGE for code summarization is a significant concern. As the authors acknowledge, these metrics are designed for text translation and might not fully capture the nuances of accurately summarizing binary code *functionality*. The usefulness of the generated summaries to *actual* reverse engineers should be verified, which would significantly strengthen this aspect.
    *   **Limited Obfuscation Handling:** The lack of obfuscation in the benchmark dataset is a significant limitation.  Real-world binary code, especially malware, is frequently obfuscated.  The performance of LLMs on obfuscated code is a critical factor that this paper does not address, limiting the real-world applicability of the findings.
    *   **Few Shot Prompt limitations:** While the paper studies few-shot prompts, the number of experiments with diverse types and quality are limited.
    *   **Inference time with large models:** Locally deployed LLMs were taking more time, but experiments regarding ways to minimize it were lacking.

**Significance:**  The paper is significant because it provides an early but solid empirical foundation for exploring the potential of LLMs in binary code analysis.  It identifies promising areas (summarization with general LLMs, initial function name recovery) and critical limitations that need to be addressed. The creation of a benchmark encourages further research. It serves as a valuable starting point for researchers and practitioners interested in applying LLMs to software security and reverse engineering. It also highlights limitations that would be good focus points of next-generation techniques for binary code reverse engineering.

**Justification of Score:**

I'm assigning a score of **7/10**.

*   It's not a groundbreaking 9 or 10 because, while well-executed, the core ideas aren't fundamentally new (DL on code, LLMs). The limitations regarding evaluation metrics and obfuscation *significantly* dampen its potential real-world impact.

*   It merits a 7 because the work is well done, comprehensive and insightful evaluation, releases a valuable benchmark, and clearly points towards future research directions.  The potential impact of making binary code analysis more accessible and efficient justifies this score. It should have high impact in reverse engineering domains.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 7/10

### Why Compress What You Can Generate? When GPT-4o Generation Ushers in Image Compression Fields
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21814v1)
- **Authors**: Yixin Gao, Xiaohan Pan, Xin Li, Zhibo Chen
- **Abstract**: The rapid development of AIGC foundation models has revolutionized the paradigm of image compression, which paves the way for the abandonment of most pixel-level transform and coding, compelling us to ask: why compress what you can generate if the AIGC foundation model is powerful enough to faithfully generate intricate structure and fine-grained details from nothing more than some compact descriptors, i.e., texts, or cues. Fortunately, recent GPT-4o image generation of OpenAI has achieved impressive cross-modality generation, editing, and design capabilities, which motivates us to answer the above question by exploring its potential in image compression fields. In this work, we investigate two typical compression paradigms: textual coding and multimodal coding (i.e., text + extremely low-resolution image), where all/most pixel-level information is generated instead of compressing via the advanced GPT-4o image generation function. The essential challenge lies in how to maintain semantic and structure consistency during the decoding process. To overcome this, we propose a structure raster-scan prompt engineering mechanism to transform the image into textual space, which is compressed as the condition of GPT-4o image generation. Extensive experiments have shown that the combination of our designed structural raster-scan prompts and GPT-4o's image generation function achieved the impressive performance compared with recent multimodal/generative image compression at ultra-low bitrate, further indicating the potential of AIGC generation in image compression fields.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates a novel approach to image compression that leverages the power of advanced AIGC (Artificial Intelligence-Generated Content) foundation models, specifically OpenAI's GPT-4o. Instead of traditional pixel-wise compression techniques, the authors explore generating the image from compact descriptions derived from the original image.  They investigate two main compression paradigms: (1) textual coding, where the image is described with a structured prompt and GPT-4o generates the image from the text, and (2) multimodal coding (text + a low-resolution version of the image) to aid generation.  A key contribution is a "structure raster-scan prompt engineering" method that transforms the image into textual space in a manner that maintains semantic and structural consistency during the decoding/generation phase. Experiments show competitive performance against recent generative compression methods at ultra-low bitrates, indicating the potential of AIGC in image compression.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using large language models (LLMs) to generate images from descriptions for compression is not entirely new. Previous works have explored generative models and textual descriptions for image compression. However, this paper distinguishes itself by specifically focusing on the capabilities of the recently released GPT-4o model, which possesses impressive cross-modality generation and editing abilities without fine-tuning. The "structure raster-scan prompt engineering" is another contribution, though raster-scan based coding has been used for decades in compression and prompt engineering has also been actively explored.  The combination of prompt engineering designed for structural integrity with a powerful AIGC without requiring specialized training, offers some novelty in the image compression landscape.

*   **Significance:** If successful, this approach has the potential to significantly alter how image compression is approached, especially at ultra-low bitrates where traditional codecs struggle to maintain perceptual quality. AIGC-based compression could potentially circumvent the limitations of pixel-wise coding by focusing on semantic and structural generation.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Exploitation of a Cutting-Edge Model:** The paper wisely capitalizes on the advanced generation capabilities of GPT-4o, showing a potential avenue for using this model in practical compression applications.
    *   **Structured Prompt Engineering:** The introduction of structural constraints in the prompt helps improve semantic and structural consistency, which are crucial for image reconstruction.
    *   **Quantitative and Qualitative Results:** The paper presents both types of results to support claims. Experiments show competitiveness at ultra-low bitrates.
    *   **No Fine-Tuning Required:** A key strength is that it achieves competitive results without requiring additional training of the AIGC model. This reduces the complexity and training burden.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependency on a Proprietary Model:** The reliance on GPT-4o is a significant limitation. Access to the model may be restricted, and the results may not be reproducible if the model changes. Furthermore, the lack of open-source code makes it difficult to verify and improve the presented method. This reduces the long-term applicability and accessibility of the research.
    *   **Limited Evaluation:** The experiments are conducted on a small dataset (10 images from DIV2K). A more thorough evaluation on diverse datasets would provide a better understanding of the method's generalization ability.
    *   **Computational Cost:** While not explicitly mentioned, AIGC models like GPT-4o are computationally expensive. The encoding and decoding times are likely to be much higher compared to traditional codecs. This aspect needs to be addressed, at least by characterizing the runtime performance.
    *   **Limited Technical Depth:** The paper lacks a deep technical analysis of the interaction between the structural prompt and GPT-4o.  A deeper understanding of how the model interprets and utilizes the prompts would strengthen the paper.
    *   **Overclaim on "Abandonment of Pixel-Level Coding":** The paper suggests a shift *away* from pixel-level methods, yet the multimodal approach still leverages a compressed low-resolution image, which is inherently pixel-level.  This is a misrepresentation. It is better described as reducing the pixel-level *reliance*.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is still valuable because it points towards a potential future direction for image compression using generative models. The prompt engineering ideas could be further developed and applied to other AIGC models. Furthermore, this research will encourage more exploration on the intersection of AIGC and compression.

**Score:** 6

**Rationale:**

The paper introduces a relevant application of a powerful AIGC model to the field of image compression, demonstrating competitive results at ultra-low bitrates. The prompt engineering technique and its focus on structural consistency contribute positively. However, the heavy reliance on a proprietary, black-box model like GPT-4o significantly limits the reproducibility and generalizability of the research. The limited evaluation dataset, computational cost, and lack of deep technical analysis further weakens the paper's overall impact. While it points to a promising direction, the lack of open-source code and reliance on a closed API diminish its immediate practical utility and ability for the field to build upon. The novelty is incremental, not revolutionary, and the significance is more potential than proven.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### 3D Stylization via Large Reconstruction Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21836v1)
- **Authors**: Ipek Oztas, Duygu Ceylan, Aysegul Dundar
- **Abstract**: With the growing success of text or image guided 3D generators, users demand more control over the generation process, appearance stylization being one of them. Given a reference image, this requires adapting the appearance of a generated 3D asset to reflect the visual style of the reference while maintaining visual consistency from multiple viewpoints. To tackle this problem, we draw inspiration from the success of 2D stylization methods that leverage the attention mechanisms in large image generation models to capture and transfer visual style. In particular, we probe if large reconstruction models, commonly used in the context of 3D generation, has a similar capability. We discover that the certain attention blocks in these models capture the appearance specific features. By injecting features from a visual style image to such blocks, we develop a simple yet effective 3D appearance stylization method. Our method does not require training or test time optimization. Through both quantitative and qualitative evaluations, we demonstrate that our approach achieves superior results in terms of 3D appearance stylization, significantly improving efficiency while maintaining high-quality visual outcomes.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "3D Stylization via Large Reconstruction Model":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel 3D stylization method that leverages large, pre-trained reconstruction models (LRMs) to transfer the visual style from a reference image to a 3D object reconstructed from a single or few images.  The core idea is to exploit the attention mechanisms within these LRMs, specifically by injecting features from a style image into the later cross-attention blocks of the triplane decoder. This allows for high-quality 3D stylization without requiring per-asset optimization or additional training.  The authors demonstrate that the geometry and appearance are disentangled within different layers of the transformer architecture of the reconstruction model. They show how blending the attention outputs with varying weights controls the degree of stylization and preserves important details.  The method is evaluated both quantitatively and qualitatively, demonstrating superior performance compared to several existing 2D and 3D stylization baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the clever exploitation of pre-trained large reconstruction models for 3D stylization. While 3D stylization itself is not new, using attention mechanisms within existing LRMs for *training-free* style transfer is a valuable contribution. It's a smart way to re-purpose existing models, which is often more efficient than training new ones from scratch.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from its efficiency and practicality. The elimination of per-asset optimization makes it suitable for interactive applications and broader adoption. The approach also allows users to easily create stylized 3D content, which is useful for many different applications such as gaming, XR, and art.
    *   **Strengths:**
        *   **Training-free approach:** This is a significant advantage, making the method faster and more accessible.
        *   **Effective use of attention mechanisms:** Identifying and manipulating attention in LRMs is a key insight that enables stylization.
        *   **Clear separation of geometry and appearance:** The approach allows for a good balance between style transfer and maintaining the 3D object's shape.
        *   **Competitive performance:** The quantitative and qualitative results demonstrate superior performance compared to existing methods.
        *   **Practicality:**  The method utilizes existing models and avoids costly optimization, promoting real-world use.

    *   **Weaknesses:**
        *   **Reliance on LRM performance:** The method is limited by the underlying reconstruction quality of the LRM. The quality of the initial multi-view synthesis limits stylization.
        *   **Stylization Limitations:** The method, as admitted, does not significantly alter the geometric details. It primarily focuses on texture and color. This might limit the range of stylistic transformations possible.
        *   **Ablation study could be more extensive:** While the authors explore blending parameters, a more detailed ablation on the architectural choices (e.g., the specific attention blocks) would have been beneficial.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper could influence the field by inspiring further research into leveraging pre-trained models for 3D content creation and manipulation tasks.  The approach could be extended to other style transfer tasks, and the insights into attention mechanisms in LRMs could be valuable for other applications. By enabling practical and accessible 3D stylization, the work has the potential to broaden the user base and drive innovation in creative applications.

While the paper has limitations and is dependent on other models, the clever use of existing infrastructure makes the approach more scalable and immediately useful. The performance comparisons, the ablation studies, and user study support the findings very well.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and practical approach to 3D stylization that leverages the power of pre-trained models. The training-free nature and competitive performance contribute to the paper's significance. However, the reliance on the quality of the underlying LRM and the geometric detail limitations prevent it from achieving a higher score. While impactful, the impact might be somewhat limited by these dependencies. A future version with a more in-depth investigation of architectural choices in LRMs would be even more impactful.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### COMPACT: COMPositional Atomic-to-Complex Visual Capability Tuning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21850v1)
- **Authors**: Xindi Wu, Hee Seung Hwang, Polina Kirichenko, Olga Russakovsky
- **Abstract**: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) excel at simple vision-language tasks but struggle when faced with complex tasks that require multiple capabilities, such as simultaneously recognizing objects, counting them, and understanding their spatial relationships. This might be partially the result of the fact that Visual Instruction Tuning (VIT), a critical training step for MLLMs, has traditionally focused on scaling data volume, but not the compositional complexity of training examples. We propose COMPACT (COMPositional Atomic-to-complex visual Capability Tuning), which generates a training dataset explicitly controlling for the compositional complexity of the training examples. The data from COMPACT allows MLLMs to train on combinations of atomic capabilities to learn complex capabilities more efficiently. Across all benchmarks, COMPACT achieves comparable performance to the LLaVA-665k VIT while using less than 10% of its data budget, and even outperforms it on several, especially those involving complex multi-capability tasks. For example, COMPACT achieves substantial 83.3% improvement on MMStar and 94.0% improvement on MM-Vet compared to the full-scale VIT on particularly complex questions that require four or more atomic capabilities. COMPACT offers a scalable, data-efficient, visual compositional tuning recipe to improve on complex visual-language tasks.
- **Summary**: Here is a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper "COMPACT: COMPositional Atomic-to-Complex Visual Capability Tuning":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces COMPACT, a novel data recipe for training Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) to improve their compositional visual reasoning abilities. COMPACT generates a training dataset with explicit control over the compositional complexity of the training examples by combining a set of atomic capabilities. It starts from atomic capabilities (e.g., object recognition, color attribution) and systematically combines them to create complex training examples. Experiments show that MLLMs trained with COMPACT, using only a fraction of the data compared to standard Visual Instruction Tuning (VIT), achieve comparable or even superior performance on various benchmarks, especially on tasks requiring the combination of multiple capabilities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of explicitly controlling compositional complexity in the training data for MLLMs is novel. Existing VIT methods primarily focus on scaling data volume without careful consideration of the underlying structure and the balancedness of compositional complexity, leading to a prevalence of simple queries. COMPACT directly addresses this limitation by structuring the training process. The taxonomy of atomic visual capabilities provides a good starting point for systematic dataset creation.

*   **Significance:** The paper demonstrates that explicitly addressing compositional complexity during training can lead to more data-efficient and effective MLLMs. This challenges the prevailing trend of simply scaling data volume, offering an alternative approach that could significantly reduce the computational cost of training while improving performance on complex visual reasoning tasks. The performance gains, particularly on tasks requiring multiple capabilities, are promising. The improved generalization to higher complexity tasks is a key contribution, showcasing an advantage over models trained solely on scaled VIT datasets.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of current VIT methods in handling compositional complexity.
    *   **Well-Defined Approach:** The COMPACT recipe is well-structured and explained, providing a clear methodology for creating compositional training data.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of COMPACT across multiple benchmarks, with significant improvements in data efficiency and generalization.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the key design choices of COMPACT, such as the importance of balanced compositional complexity and the impact of atomic capability coverage.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on Closed-Source Models:** The data generation process relies on Gemini-2.0-Flash, a closed-source model, which may introduce biases and limit reproducibility. Although, the authors mention that they will make the dataset publicly available to mitigate this. The dependence on a specific model version (especially in rapidly evolving models) presents a potential bottleneck.
    *   **Limited Complexity Range:** The recipe currently only supports a complexity level of up to k=3 due to reliability limitations in higher level queries. Extending this range in future works would further validate the method's scalability.
    *   **Benchmarking Challenges:** Some of the chosen benchmarks include non-visual capabilities like knowledge and math, which are not a main focus. This dilutes the evaluation with potentially unrelated aspects.

*   **Potential Influence:** This work could potentially influence future research directions in MLLM training, shifting the focus from data scaling to more structured data generation methods that explicitly address compositional capabilities. It encourages exploration into alternative approaches for improving the generalization capabilities of MLLMs, potentially leading to more efficient and robust models. It could also inspire more work on defining, categorizing, and generating compositional datasets.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper offers a novel perspective on improving compositional capabilities in MLLMs and presents compelling empirical evidence supporting its claims. While the reliance on closed-source models is a limitation, the core idea and demonstrated results warrant a strong positive evaluation. The weaknesses are acknowledged and could be addressed in future iterations to improve the robustness and reproducibility of the method.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### TRUST: An LLM-Based Dialogue System for Trauma Understanding and Structured Assessments
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21851v1)
- **Authors**: Sichang Tu, Abigail Powers, Stephen Doogan, Jinho D. Choi
- **Abstract**: Objectives: While Large Language Models (LLMs) have been widely used to assist clinicians and support patients, no existing work has explored dialogue systems for standard diagnostic interviews and assessments. This study aims to bridge the gap in mental healthcare accessibility by developing an LLM-powered dialogue system that replicates clinician behavior. Materials and Methods: We introduce TRUST, a framework of cooperative LLM modules capable of conducting formal diagnostic interviews and assessments for Post-Traumatic Stress Disorder (PTSD). To guide the generation of appropriate clinical responses, we propose a Dialogue Acts schema specifically designed for clinical interviews. Additionally, we develop a patient simulation approach based on real-life interview transcripts to replace time-consuming and costly manual testing by clinicians. Results: A comprehensive set of evaluation metrics is designed to assess the dialogue system from both the agent and patient simulation perspectives. Expert evaluations by conversation and clinical specialists show that TRUST performs comparably to real-life clinical interviews. Discussion: Our system performs at the level of average clinicians, with room for future enhancements in communication styles and response appropriateness. Conclusions: Our TRUST framework shows its potential to facilitate mental healthcare availability.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper, including a novelty/significance score and justification.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TRUST, an LLM-powered dialogue system designed for conducting formal diagnostic interviews and assessments for Post-Traumatic Stress Disorder (PTSD). The system comprises several key components: a database to store variable metadata, interview history, and assessment scores; and a framework with two LLM-powered modules: a conversation module that manages the dialogue flow and generates clinician responses, guided by a novel Dialogue Acts (DA) schema tailored to clinical interviews, and an assessment module that performs the diagnostic assessment based on the collected information.  A key element is a patient simulation approach using real-life interview transcripts to enable robust system evaluation without relying solely on expensive and time-consuming clinical expert involvement.  The system's performance is evaluated using expert evaluations (conversation expert and domain expert) and automated LLM-based evaluations, considering comprehensiveness, appropriateness, communication style, completeness, and faithfulness. The authors claim that TRUST performs comparably to average clinicians and offers potential benefits in improving mental healthcare accessibility.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper has moderate novelty.

    *   **Positive Aspects:** Combining LLMs with diagnostic interviews in mental healthcare is a growing area, but few existing works focus specifically on formal, structured diagnostic interviews like those for PTSD using a standard instrument (CAPS). The development of a tailored DA schema for clinical interviews is a valuable contribution, providing more control over the dialogue compared to end-to-end LLM approaches. The real-life transcript-based patient simulation is also a strong point, enabling more ecologically valid testing.

    *   **Negative Aspects:** While the system is novel in its comprehensive integration, individual components, like using LLMs for dialogue generation and DA tagging, are established techniques. The system builds upon existing work on automated PTSD diagnosis using LLMs (Tu et al. [11]), essentially operationalizing that diagnosis pipeline into a conversational agent. A more thorough discussion of related work and a clearer articulation of how TRUST *significantly* advances the state-of-the-art is needed.

*   **Significance:** The work has potential significance for improving mental healthcare accessibility.

    *   **Positive Aspects:** Addressing the shortage of mental health professionals and reducing barriers to treatment is a crucial goal. A system like TRUST could serve as a first step in the diagnostic process, potentially streamlining clinical workflows and reducing costs. The focus on PTSD is important, given the frequent under-detection of this condition. The DA schema, and the modular framework, contribute to improved controllability and adaptability, meaning TRUST can evolve into other mental health conditions more easily.

    *   **Negative Aspects:** The paper acknowledges limitations, such as the difficulty of LLMs in replicating the nuances of human conversation and the risk of hallucination in patient simulation. The reliance on expert-annotated data for training the DA tag prediction model could be a bottleneck for scaling up the system. The limited size of the manual expert evaluation (only 5 transcripts) reduces the strength of the claims regarding clinical equivalence. There is no demonstration that use of the system measurably improves accessibility or diagnostic outcomes compared to existing screening procedures.  The paper lacks concrete real-world implications or a discussion about integration with existing clinical workflows and practices. The reliance on a closed-source LLM also hinders reproducibility and widespread adoption.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The clear modular design of the system promotes flexibility and adaptability.
    *   The patient simulation approach based on real-life transcripts enhances evaluation realism.
    *   The tailored DA schema provides improved control over the dialogue flow.
    *   The evaluation metrics are well-defined and address key aspects of the system's performance.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Limited evaluation scale in the expert evaluation.
    *   Dependence on a specific (and closed-source) LLM limits generalizability and reproducibility.
    *   Lack of a comparative evaluation with existing screening tools.
    *   Limited demonstration or discussion of downstream clinical benefits.
    *   Overclaims regarding performance, given limitations.

*   **Potential Influence:** The work has the potential to influence future research in the area of LLM-based diagnostic dialogue systems for mental healthcare. The proposed framework and evaluation methodology can serve as a basis for developing and evaluating similar systems. However, it needs further validation and real-world deployment to demonstrate its true impact.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

While the paper demonstrates a promising approach to automated PTSD diagnosis, the limitations outlined above prevent it from achieving a higher score. The novelty, although present, is incremental, and the significance is potential rather than demonstrated. The evaluation, while well-defined, is limited in scale and raises concerns about LLM-based evaluations in this domain. A stronger contribution would involve a larger, more rigorous clinical evaluation, a comparison with existing screening methods, and a demonstration of downstream clinical benefits. The reliance on Claude further reduces the impact due to limited accessibility.

Score: 5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 5/10

### ReVision: High-Quality, Low-Cost Video Generation with Explicit 3D Physics Modeling for Complex Motion and Interaction
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21855v1)
- **Authors**: Qihao Liu, Ju He, Qihang Yu, Liang-Chieh Chen, Alan Yuille
- **Abstract**: In recent years, video generation has seen significant advancements. However, challenges still persist in generating complex motions and interactions. To address these challenges, we introduce ReVision, a plug-and-play framework that explicitly integrates parameterized 3D physical knowledge into a pretrained conditional video generation model, significantly enhancing its ability to generate high-quality videos with complex motion and interactions. Specifically, ReVision consists of three stages. First, a video diffusion model is used to generate a coarse video. Next, we extract a set of 2D and 3D features from the coarse video to construct a 3D object-centric representation, which is then refined by our proposed parameterized physical prior model to produce an accurate 3D motion sequence. Finally, this refined motion sequence is fed back into the same video diffusion model as additional conditioning, enabling the generation of motion-consistent videos, even in scenarios involving complex actions and interactions. We validate the effectiveness of our approach on Stable Video Diffusion, where ReVision significantly improves motion fidelity and coherence. Remarkably, with only 1.5B parameters, it even outperforms a state-of-the-art video generation model with over 13B parameters on complex video generation by a substantial margin. Our results suggest that, by incorporating 3D physical knowledge, even a relatively small video diffusion model can generate complex motions and interactions with greater realism and controllability, offering a promising solution for physically plausible video generation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the ReVision paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ReVision, a plug-and-play framework for enhancing pre-trained conditional video generation models. ReVision explicitly incorporates parameterized 3D physical knowledge to improve the generation of high-quality videos, particularly those involving complex motions and interactions. The framework operates in three stages: (1) Generating a coarse video using a pre-trained diffusion model (like Stable Video Diffusion), (2) Extracting 2D and 3D features from the coarse video and refining them using a Parameterized Physical Prior Model (PPPM) to create a more accurate 3D motion sequence, and (3) Feeding the refined motion sequence back into the diffusion model as additional conditioning, resulting in a motion-consistent and realistic video. The paper demonstrates ReVision's effectiveness in improving motion fidelity, coherence, and realism, even outperforming larger state-of-the-art models in complex scenarios like dance generation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novelty:** The core idea of explicitly integrating 3D physical knowledge into video generation via a plug-and-play framework is a significant step. Existing methods often rely on scaling model size or data, which this paper demonstrates is insufficient for capturing physical realism. ReVision offers a different avenue, leveraging existing models and enhancing them with geometric understanding.
    *   **Technical Soundness:** The three-stage pipeline is well-defined and implemented. The PPPM, a transformer-based module for refining motion sequences, seems effective.  The fine-tuning strategy for the extended SVD is designed to preserve the original generation quality while adding motion control.
    *   **Experimental Results:** The paper presents compelling qualitative and quantitative results. The user studies demonstrate a clear preference for ReVision over baselines, including SVD and the larger HunyuanVideo model. The ablation studies provide insights into the effectiveness of the various components.
    *   **Impact:** ReVision's approach to video generation has the potential to influence future research by highlighting the importance of incorporating physical priors. The framework is also highly practical as it offers a way to enhance existing pre-trained models without extensive retraining.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependency on Parametric 3D Models:** ReVision heavily relies on parametric 3D mesh models (SMPL-X, SMAL) which limit its applicability to human and animal videos, though the use of bounding boxes attempts to handle general objects. This is addressed to some extent with the 2.5D object parameterization, but that component isn't as sophisticated or potentially as accurate as the mesh-based one. A truly general solution would require methods to represent non-rigid objects in a more flexible manner.
    *   **Computational Cost:** The paper mentions an increase in inference time due to the added steps. While it's presented as minimal, a more thorough analysis of the computational overhead would be beneficial.
    *   **Limited Frame Generation Length:** The model has a 32-frame maximum generation length due to memory constraints. This limits the complexity and scope of the videos it can generate, though this point is addressed as a limitation in the paper, as is the quality of smaller details in the final image.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a key challenge in video generation: achieving physical realism and controllable motion. The explicit incorporation of 3D physical knowledge is a promising approach that sets it apart from models that simply scale up in size and data. The modular design of ReVision makes it a valuable tool for enhancing existing video generation systems. The paper offers a well-executed and theoretically sound framework with significant implications for improving the quality and controllability of generated videos.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:**

The paper's novelty and strong experimental results warrant a high score. The use of explicit 3D physical knowledge represents a significant conceptual advance, leading to compelling performance improvements. While ReVision is limited by its reliance on parametric models, which restricts its current broad applicability, the 2.5D parameterizations and integration with existing models and pipelines highlight a valuable contribution.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10
